# Local agents

Verified against live source: 2026-10-09.

Shared wiki storage (verified with isolated runners, 2026-10-24): `createRunner` in `src/runner.cjs` always constructs its `WikiStore` under the runner data directory. Source and installed Windows apps therefore use the same `%APPDATA%/Phasma Harness/Wikis` store, alongside sessions and connectors; headless runners use their selected private data directory. The former source-only `app.getAppPath()/Wikis` branch gave otherwise identical bots different, sometimes empty, wikis. Existing checkout stores are preserved and require an explicit non-overwriting import. `tests/runner.test.cjs`, "source, installed and headless runners share the data-directory wiki across Main Bot, bots and delegated workers", checks the actual runner and instruction paths, retained notes, and unchanged source files across all three modes.

Test-pool shutdown (verified on Windows, 2026-10-24): closing a controller cancels its owned pool process tree and prevents queued checks from starting. Intentional cancellation is logged as information; uncancelled exits still warn, and actual failure reports remain saved notices during shutdown (`src/controller/task-gate.cjs`, `src/controller.cjs`). `tests/testpool-harness.test.cjs`, "controller shutdown cancels a real test pool quietly, stops its child and drops queued checks", launches a real holding test command, shuts down the controller, and verifies process exit, one start and no warning. Companion cases preserve unexpected-exit warnings and late failure notices. Registered in pool `bot-setup`.

Source installer launcher (changed 2026-10-28): Bitdefender's behaviour check blocked the old `Install Phasma Harness.cmd` three times on 2026-10-28 (`CMD:Heur.BZC.Boxter.1293.A0BB186A`), so it printed only "Access is denied." The cause was its cmd/PowerShell polyglot: `powershell -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -Command` read the .cmd's own text and ran it via `[scriptblock]::Create`. The incident report is under `C:\ProgramData\Bitdefender\Bitdefender Security App\ctc\rca\`; it listed only the cmd and PowerShell processes and quarantined nothing. The PowerShell now lives in `src/install.ps1`, and the .cmd only runs `powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File "%~dp0src\install.ps1" %*`; arguments pass through, so `Install Phasma Harness.cmd -CheckOnly` checks the payload without installing. `tests/installer.test.ps1` reads `src/install.ps1` and fails if the .cmd runs anything else. Verified 2026-10-28 14:51: `"Install Phasma Harness.cmd" -CheckOnly` run from pwsh printed `PASS: installer payload and required app files.` with exit 0. The Bitdefender quarantine stayed at 65 items, and no incident report was added or changed. Not covered: a double-click from Explorer, and a full install. Bitdefender also scans file contents: a tool output file holding the old .cmd text was quarantined as `CMD:Heur.BZC.MNT.Boxter.1293`, so old copies of the launcher, such as `.scratch` exports, can be quarantined when written. Linux has no such launcher (`npm ci && npm start`, or `tools/linux-service.cjs`, see [Linux hosting](hosting.md)).

Installer messages, Start menu and image check (2026-10-31, at the user's request):
- **Old messages.** The Windows "Bundled helper MCP (project_context / tool helpers)…" line was out of date. It named only project context and the tool helpers, and said Codex used dynamic tools, but Codex also connects to the `phasma_harness` MCP (`codexConfig`). It also left out the native CLIs and API models. Both installers named only Codex, Claude or Cursor as enough.
- **New messages.** Both installers now say:
  - Harness tools (project context, bots, connectors, images, settings) come from a local MCP server that Codex, Claude, Cursor and the native CLIs connect to, and API models get the same tools inside Harness.
  - Any one of Codex, Claude, Cursor, DeepSeek, Muse, Antigravity or an API provider (OpenRouter, LM Studio or any OpenAI-compatible endpoint) is enough. (The first version, v0.3.23, said Muse and Antigravity couldn't run the Hub; that was Harness's own untested block, removed the same day.)
  - Jev is optional and adds Jev routing and Jev image assist.
- **Start menu.** `src/install.ps1` writes the same shortcut to the Desktop and to the per-user Start menu (`[Environment]::GetFolderPath('Programs')`).
- **Image check.** After `npm ci`, both installers check that `@napi-rs/canvas` loads (`createCanvas(2, 2).encodeSync('png')`); image attachments and Jev image assist need it. Linux runs the check on the headless path too.
- **Verified.** `tests/installer.test.ps1`; `-CheckOnly`; the exact image command (0 with the library, non-zero for a missing module); the shortcut loop on temp folders; and `bash -n` plus `--check-only` for Linux.
  - Not run: a full install, and the Linux image check on Linux.

Source installer shutdown (verified on Windows, 2026-10-23): `Install Phasma Harness.cmd` (now `src/install.ps1`) calls `src/install-preflight.ps1` before `npm ci`. It sends `--quit-for-install` through Electron's single-instance handoff; `src/main.cjs` accepts only a matching executable path and follows normal runner/worker cleanup. Control-only launches never initialize a runner, including when no instance exists. The installer waits up to 45 seconds for this folder's Electron processes and dependency locks; file/directory probes are non-destructive, detect mapped binaries, and do not follow junctions. Probes request write/delete access with shared read/write/delete, so compatible editor watchers do not falsely block installation. Failure preserves dependencies and names the blocking path or remaining processes. Older running versions require one tray Quit runner. `tests/installer.test.ps1` covers inherited Node mode, path quoting, process matching, ignored other apps, timeout/query failures, held/released files, compatible watchers/readers, mapped files, directory locks and untouched junction targets; it passed on PowerShell 5 and 7. `tests/runner-live.cjs` verifies a real second-process request, owned-worker cleanup, wrong-install rejection and a cold shutdown-only launch with private app data. Pool: `installer` plus `background-runner`. The actual source installer was also run with v0.3.7 open: shutdown, dependency reinstall, bundled-tool/runtime setup and relaunch passed while preserving all four connector records.

Review regressions (verified with local fixtures, 2026-10-22): completed delegated messages are persisted into a normal bot's direct chat by `Agents.retainDelegatedChat` (`src/agents.cjs`), including quoted context for its next Codex request. CLI/API follow-ups use the same stored messages through `handoff`; export and clear operate on that real history. Imported message IDs are retained across clears to prevent replay, and save failures roll back the import. `tests/agents.test.cjs`, "delegated history belongs to the direct chat for follow-ups, export, clear and retry", checks model input, export, clear, late submission context and save retry. Status-only final turns retain the preceding substantive answer; legacy multipart answers remain complete ("worker reports keep substantive answers before a status-only turn and legacy multipart answers"). Empty queued executions keep their targeted Stop control (`tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`).

Access and asynchronous checks (verified 2026-10-22): composer access uses `Agents.settings` so inheriting sub-bots follow it; management checks both the current chat and default access before changing another bot (`src/controller.cjs`, `src/agents.cjs`; "composer permissions update inherited access and management checks the effective chat access"). Shared local/remote handlers reject sub-bot chat mutations, including compact, clear, rename, archive and delete-session, while approvals and Stop remain available (`src/runner-commands.cjs`, `tests/runner-commands.test.cjs`). Test-pool runs serialize canonical repository paths and retain all waiting chats. A late failure becomes a saved notice if its chat was cleared, changed, restricted or busy; it cannot start or queue another turn there (`src/controller/task-gate.cjs`, `tests/testpool-harness.test.cjs`). The runner remains Harness-bundled and strips Jev credentials from repository test commands.

Personal instructions and wiki migration (verified 2026-10-22): Claude excludes both its config directory's `CLAUDE.md` and `~/CLAUDE.md` (`tests/worker-defaults.test.cjs`; [documented parent-file loading and exclusions](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory)). Saved custom wiki locations are imported once into a linked subfolder of the fixed managed wiki, preserving both original files and current notes. Conflicts and links fail visibly instead of overwriting content (`src/workspace/wiki-store.cjs`, `tests/wiki-store.test.cjs`). Enter activation remains disabled on buttons as requested; task-bot limits remain opt-in.

Batch project copies (verified 2026-10-18): `src/workspace/job-workspaces.cjs` reserves per-job directories before dispatch acknowledgement. One asynchronous setup operation captures the batch input and then, for Git projects, creates independent shallow Git repositories without hard links, alternates or remotes. It copies the current working files, nonignored untracked files, ignored root instructions, dirty initialized submodules and the active wiki; every copy uses its own wiki index. Credentials and ignored outputs stay out; tracked credential paths, links, merge conflicts, moving inputs, insufficient disk and oversized inventories fail setup. Working/wiki inputs are bounded at 50,000 files/8 GiB; committed Git data also has an 8 GiB cap. Free-space admission includes both, so deleted working files cannot hide large committed inputs. Credential paths in HEAD are blocked even after a working-tree deletion. Helpers keep the same project copy; registered project aliases allow the original owner conversation to coordinate without granting another project access. Scoped Hub handoffs preserve that directory and do not expose older unrelated execution history to the new helper. Configured checks remap their working directory and plain absolute project arguments; embedded original-checkout commands require review. Ordinary setup failures return one failed result per job without inference or shared-checkout fallback. Interrupted setup stays held; partial files are retained. The updater waits for live setup and Quit cancels it. No automatic merge or commit occurs. UI details, owner receipts, context reads and Markdown export retain the output path. Sources: `src/agents.cjs`, `src/controller/coordinator.cjs`, `src/conversation-export.cjs`, `src/runner.cjs`, `ui/app.js`, `ui/index.html`, `ui/style.css`. Regressions: `tests/job-workspaces.test.cjs`, `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`, `tests/runner.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` (pools `job-workspaces`, `agents-ui`). New copies use the short registered layout `jobs/<16-hex batch hash>/<index>` to avoid Windows dependency paths exceeding MAX_PATH; legacy registrations remain readable without moving files. Copies of copies retain their original project boundary. A private PhasmaEngine Release player build and startup check passed after a copy-only ScriptRuntimeHooks.h include fix; the original checkout was untouched. Live frame metrics were about 30 FPS and the owned player stopped cleanly. This proves that player starts, not that a generated game works. Gameplay and the three-game acceptance scenario remain unverified. Historical refs, retention controls and real-provider/game acceptance remain unfinished.

Host evidence and batch reports (verified 2026-10-18): configured checks optionally identify Build, Launch, Gameplay or Test; existing checks default to Test. `src/job-reports.cjs` accepts only the latest host-run attempt bound to the current assignment and revision. A passed label requires a recorded zero exit code; missing, stale or malformed evidence never becomes success. Checks describe their recorded time, not later file edits. Failed configured checks block a positive model completion assessment without starting another worker retry; the existing task gate retains its single correction allowance. The original batch request has a compact report of each job, owner, workspace, partial state, artifacts, separate check outcomes and provider cost estimates. Latest retries supersede older receipts. Job details, owner context and Markdown export retain commands, directories, exit codes and times. Unconfigured categories say Not verified; artifact publication proves availability only. Batch summary (2026-10-19): the report header, the activity row and the Markdown export count returned-with-checks-passed, returned-not-verified, failed, interrupted, cancelled, waiting and not-dispatched jobs separately, with no model call. "Checks passed" needs a returned job whose configured host checks all passed; categories without a configured check still show Not verified in its row. A returned job without checks, or with any unrun check, is "returned, not verified". A failed check on a returned job counts as failed. This is the deterministic morning summary of a batch that ran unattended. Sources: `src/tasks.cjs`, `src/controller/task-gate.cjs`, `src/controller/coordinator.cjs`, `src/agents.cjs`, `src/conversation-export.cjs`, `ui/app.js`. Regressions: `tests/job-reports.test.cjs`, `tests/tasks.test.cjs`, `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`, `tests/conversation-export.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` (pools `job-reports`, `agents-ui`). Automatic Phasma verification recipes and autonomous native-provider game acceptance remain unfinished.

Review fixes (verified 2026-10-19):
- **Artifacts across attempts.** When Hub sends follow-up work to the same worker, the newer attempt now keeps the earlier attempts' artifacts when the workspace is the same, choosing the newest version of each artifact id. The live three-game run lost Lights Out's `game-player` (its Run button) this way. Source: `batchReports` in `src/job-reports.cjs`.
- **One credential list.** `PRIVATE_PATH` in `src/tasks.cjs` is now the only credential list. It covers workspace copies, artifact publishing and ZIP downloads (`src/artifacts.cjs` imports it). It adds `.ssh`, `.aws`, `.kube`, `.npmrc`, `.netrc`, `.git-credentials`, `.docker/config.json`, `service-account*.json`, `msal_token_cache*`, every `id_*` key type, `*.keystore` and `*secret*`/`*credential*` json/txt files.
- **Failed first copy.** A failed or aborted first batch copy now removes its half-written input and `input.json`, so sibling jobs no longer fail with `EEXIST`. Budget-paused jobs keep their setup.
- **Stale routine runs.** Superseded 2026-10-20: routines no longer delegate jobs; a run is its queued message or turn (see Routines).

Regressions: `tests/job-reports.test.cjs`, `tests/job-workspaces.test.cjs`, `tests/artifacts.test.cjs`, `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`.

Tier-2 review fixes (verified 2026-10-19 with fixtures, the UI smoke and `tests/remote-live.cjs` through the real `main.cjs` and DPAPI; no live provider run; `server-live` and `quit-live` need Linux and were not run):
- **Browser key at rest.** On the desktop, `RemoteSettings` gets Electron `safeStorage` and keeps the key sealed in `credentials.phasma-remote.enc`; an existing plaintext `credentials.phasma-remote` is migrated and deleted. The headless server has no OS key protection and keeps its 0600 plaintext file. Limit: sealing stops file reads and workspace copies, not a process running as the same user calling DPAPI. Sources: `src/remote.cjs` (`sealedKey`), `src/remote-settings.cjs`, `src/main.cjs`. Regression: `tests/remote.test.cjs`.
- **JPEG images.** `normalizeImage` encodes an opaque image as JPEG q85 too and keeps whichever data URL is smaller; any alpha below 255 keeps PNG. `Controller.send` accepts PNG and JPEG, and the Claude, Cursor, Gemini and Muse adapters take the media type from the data URL (`imageData`). The napi-rs `toDataURL` quality is 0–1 (`encodeSync` takes 0–100). Regressions: `tests/documents.test.cjs`, `tests/gemini.test.cjs`.
- **Images out of snapshots.** `Controller.snapshot` passes sessions through `withoutImages`. Inline `data:image/` values under `url` or `images` become `null`, so counts stay. Base64 tool-result images (`{ data, media_type }` or MCP `{ data, mimeType }`) lose their `data`. The UI loads a shown image with the `image` command (session, item or pending message, image index), which is allowed for browser access. On the real stores (2026-10-19), no base64-like string over 100 KB remained, and all sessions came to 3.3 MB (was 4.3 MB). Regressions: `tests/agents.test.cjs`, UI smoke.
- **Questions hold delivery.** A helper's job is not delivered while its `agent_message` question to the job's owner is still receivable in the owner's queue, or while the owner is busy on an agent-reply turn. An answer then queues for the helper as before. Each of these releases the job: a question read without an answer, a paused owner queue, a failed queued message, or a message the owner cannot receive. A question to another bot never holds the job. A held job keeps no background slot. Source: `Agents.openQuestion`. Regressions: `tests/agents.test.cjs` (answered, unanswered and paused-owner cases).
- **UI.** The attachment tray is rebuilt only when its drafts change, so a state update keeps focus on a remove button. The browser view retries only network failures, busy (408/429) and server errors, with backoff up to 60 s, and shows the runner's own message. Other refusals, such as an oversized history, stop with a reload hint. A network blip no longer shows the key form; only a 401 does. The renderer reports the appearance to main (`setAppearance`, `appearance.json` in userData), so both windows paint the matching launch background. Regressions: `tests/remote.test.cjs`, `tests/appearance.test.cjs`, UI smoke.
- **Antigravity cleanup.** A confirmed result is returned even if cleanup then fails; the host stays pending, so the next run refuses until it is resolved. The generated agent file is removed even when the process tree was not confirmed stopped. At startup, `sweepAgentFiles` removes `.agents/agents/phasma-<uuid>.md` files in known workspaces, but only when the content is byte-for-byte what Harness would generate for that name. Regression: `tests/antigravity.test.cjs`.
- **"Allow for this session" without a tool name.** Did not reproduce: without a tool name `sessionAllowKey` returns null, so `canAllowSession` is false and the UI shows no button. Locked by an assertion in `tests/core.test.cjs`.
- **Holds without a reset time.** `quotaWake` now schedules a recheck `UNKNOWN_RESET_MS` (15 min) out when no reset is reported. The wake reads ChatGPT usage again (`account/rateLimits/read`). The reserve is still not guessed: a fresh report that remains over the threshold keeps the hold. Regression: `tests/agents.test.cjs`.
- **Low items.**
  - ZIP folder entries add search bits wherever read bits are set; Windows reports folders as `0o40666`.
  - An untracked nested repository (Git lists it as `name/`) is copied like a submodule.
  - A case-duplicate instruction file, such as `AGENTS.md` beside a tracked `Agents.md`, is copied once (BigInt inode comparison).
  - `JobBudgets` rewrites `job-heartbeat` every minute while a job is active. A restart charges time only up to that heartbeat.
  - A desktop startup failure no longer detaches the lifetime lease. Before, the monitor terminated Electron 5 s after the lease closed, taking the error window with it.
  - The Windows job name mixes in a private per-installation secret (`credentials.runner-job`).
  - Recovery backups are copied to a temporary file, verified and only then renamed, so a new interrupted copy or an unreadable store leaves no `.bak`. A partial backup left by an older build still stops startup, and the error now names the store to check and the backup to move aside.
  - Regressions: `tests/artifact-downloads.test.cjs`, `tests/job-workspaces.test.cjs`, `tests/job-budgets.test.cjs`, `tests/runner.test.cjs`, `tests/runner-containment.test.cjs`.
- **Document worker memory (R5, fixed 2026-10-19).** pdf.js typed arrays and canvas pixels live outside the worker's 192 MB V8 heap, so each reader process now gets an OS cap of 768 MiB committed memory. Only the worker is capped, never the runner, providers or game builds.
  - Windows: one long-lived `src/document-job.ps1` per `DocumentReader` holds a private job object with `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_PROCESS_MEMORY | KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`. It nests under the runner job and libuv's job (Windows 8+). The worker is forked, its PID is assigned and checked with `IsProcessInJob`, and only then are the bytes sent. While the PID is with the helper the worker is never killed, so the helper cannot open a reused PID. The helper starts once in about 0.4 s; each assignment takes about 10 ms.
  - Linux: the worker is forked through `/usr/bin/prlimit --data=<cap> -- node …`. prlimit execs node, so the IPC channel and environment survive. `--as` would break V8's address-space reservations. A service `MemoryMax=` would cap builds and providers too, and `systemd-run --scope` would leave the service cgroup, so neither is used.
  - macOS has no cap.
  - If the cap cannot be applied (no helper, assignment failed, no prlimit), the file is not read.
  - Measured 2026-10-19 (Windows commit peaks): a 10 MB, 200-page PDF used 150 MB, a 25 MP PNG 250 MB (Electron as node and plain node agree). A 3 KB double-Flate PDF that expands to 1.5 GiB reached 3.5 GB in 6.7 s without the cap, then reported "no extractable text". With the cap it stopped at exactly 768 MiB in about 2 s. In WSL Ubuntu the worker starts at about 50–70 MB `VmData`, and 640 MB of buffers fit before the cap.
  - Hitting the cap usually kills V8 with a fatal out-of-memory error rather than raising a RangeError. Both cases report "needs more memory than Harness allows".
  - Regression: `tests/documents.test.cjs` ("decompression-bomb PDF"), using `memoryBombPdf` from `tests/fixtures/documents.cjs`. Raising the cap to 4 GiB makes that test fail. It also passes in WSL. Pool `documents`.
  - **Lost results (fixed 2026-10-19).** The intermittent "stopped before returning a result" in "opaque photos are sent as JPEG" was not the cap. That fixture peaks at about 100 MB committed under the job (`PeakProcessMemoryUsed`), against 768 MiB.
    - Cause: `src/document-worker.cjs` called `process.disconnect()` straight after `process.send()`. Node 24's disconnect only waits for queued handles, so it closes the channel on the next tick and cancels a write still waiting on the pipe buffer. The worker exits 0 and the parent gets no message. Node's async IPC write callback reports success even when the write was cancelled.
    - Only results larger than the pipe buffer are hit. Image data URLs (89–241 KB) failed 30 of 1,200 reads under six parallel loops, each with worker exit 0 and no stderr. A 2 MiB DOCX part failed 20 of 20 with no load, and a 300 KB part 4 of 20, so real DOCX reads were hit too. Full-suite load probably just slows the parent's reads (inferred; no failing suite run was instrumented).
    - Fix: disconnect in the send callback. After the fix, 0 of 1,200 image reads failed under the same load and all 20 DOCX reads passed. Under Electron 44.4.3 as node (Node 24.21.0) the 2 MiB DOCX read failed 20 of 20 before the fix and passed 20 of 20 after.
    - Full suite (`--test-concurrency=1`): 2 of about 6 runs failed before the fix; 10 consecutive runs (691–699 tests) passed with no warnings after it. Not run on Linux.
    - Regression: `tests/documents.test.cjs` ("larger than the IPC pipe buffer"). It fails against the old worker.

Live three-game acceptance (2026-10-19). The script is `.scratch/phasma-game-acceptance.cjs`, uncommitted: Hub, four Codex `gpt-6.1-sol:low` workers, a prebuilt Release PhasmaPlayer at engine `a421ec3d`, and private Temp copies.
- **Passed:** Lights Out, Take 21 and Grid Courier each passed host build, launch and gameplay checks with Player and source artifacts. The live title correction was applied. At most two background slots were used, and cleanup was confirmed.
- **Failed:** the Stop check.
  - The cancelled witness job was delivered as cancelled, but its Codex command (`node wait.cjs`, started in the foreground of the worker's shell tool) stayed in progress.
  - Its process lived until runner shutdown, about 70 s later.
  - Codex `turn/interrupt` leaves already started commands running. The saved item had `source: unifiedExecStartup`: Codex keeps such commands as the thread's background terminals after the turn ends.
- **Live rerun passed (2026-10-19, with the tier-2 fixes also in the tree):**
  - All four assertions passed: the three games, the applied correction, the Stop check and cleanup.
  - Stop: the cancelled witness's `node` process was gone at the check.
  - At most two background slots were in use, and the source project was unchanged.
  - The Harness log and all three Player logs had no warnings.
  - This covers Codex workers only. Muse and Antigravity Stop are fixture-tested only.
  - The report is `%TEMP%\phasma-harness-games-0MaKoz\native-acceptance.json`.

Live acceptance on v0.2.0 (2026-10-20). `.scratch/phasma-game-acceptance-v020.cjs` (uncommitted, the 19 Oct script plus two checks), same model and engine Player, fresh seed `%TEMP%\phasma-harness-games-9usN8E`:
- **Passed:** all three games passed build, launch and gameplay; the title correction applied; the cancelled witness's process was confirmed gone; at most two background slots; cleanup confirmed; source unchanged.
- **New:** a separate question ("17 times 23") sent to Hub while all four jobs were pending was answered "391" before any job returned. The morning summary read "3 returned, checks passed · 1 cancelled".
- Logs: the run's Harness log stayed empty (no entries) and the three Player logs had no warnings or errors.
- Still not covered here: closing and reopening a window (covered by `tests/runner-live.cjs` with fake providers) and product verification recipes (the checks come from the script).

Live forced restart (2026-10-20). `.scratch/restart-live.cjs` + `restart-live-runner.cjs` (uncommitted): a contained runner with three `gpt-5.6-terra:low` jobs, two running long Node timers and one waiting for a background slot, killed with TerminateProcess; a second runner then started on the same data.
- **Passed:** both timer processes were gone 5 s after the kill (the kernel job ended them); after the restart nothing was replayed, no result arrived twice and cleanup was confirmed.
- **Found:** the job waiting for a slot was classed `uncertain` and held, so it neither resumed nor allowed Start now. Cause: `pump` sets `state: 'running'` and increments `executionAttempts` before `send()` waits for capacity, and the controller load turns a running session into `interrupted` with its queue paused. Also, the morning summary called all three held jobs "waiting".
- **Fixed (2026-10-20):** new jobs carry `admissionTracked`; `Controller.send` saves `admittedAt` once the slot is granted, before routing or any provider call. At restart, a tracked `running` job without `admittedAt` returns to `queued` with zero attempts, its session to idle and unpaused, and its unchecked task is dropped, so normal recovery resumes it. Admitted jobs and records without `admissionTracked` (saved by v0.2.0) stay held. `src/job-reports.cjs` shows an `interrupted` job as needs-review even under its budget review hold and counts needs-review entries as "need review". Regressions: `tests/agents.test.cjs` "a job still waiting for a background slot at a crash resumes once…" (fails without the fix) and `tests/job-reports.test.cjs` "jobs held after a restart count as needing review…".
- **Live rerun after the fix (2026-10-20, `%TEMP%\phasma-harness-restart-quF8RX`):** all three workers ran the long timer, so whichever job was third waited for a slot (the first rerun failed to reach the crash point because job 3 got a slot first; the check is now order-independent). At the kill, jobs 1 and 2 were running and job 3 waited; both timers were gone 5 s later. After the restart, jobs 1 and 2 were held `uncertain` and did not run again (no new start files); job 3 was classed `not-started`, resumed automatically, started its timer again, was stopped by the check and delivered exactly one result (cancelled). The batch summary read "1 cancelled · 2 need review"; cleanup confirmed. Not covered: hosted/server game recovery and older uncontained runs.
- **Fix (2026-10-19):** every Codex turn interrupt now goes through `Controller.interruptTurn`. Stop, the stop during submission and a failed send all use it.
  - It sends `turn/interrupt` at once.
  - It records the processes the context's app-server started between the turn's submission (`submission.startedAt`) and the end of the interrupt, plus everything they started. On Linux that includes processes started by the native `codex` that the npm launcher runs.
  - It then asks Codex to end each of the turn's in-progress command items (`thread/backgroundTerminals/terminate` with the item's `processId`).
  - Finally it kills whatever is left of the recorded tree and checks again (`processTree.stopStartedSince`).
  - The app-server and anything it started before or after that window, such as MCP helpers, are never targeted.
  - New work waits for the cleanup. An unconfirmed result is held as `workerCleanup` and blocks execution, as for native CLI workers.
  - The held record keeps the app-server only as an anchor (pid, start time, window), not as a root. Each retry re-finds that window's processes from the live server and confirms only when they are gone.
  - Codex itself cannot confirm this: `osPid` is always null in `thread/backgroundTerminals/list` (0.156.1 and 0.163.0), and its terminate does not wait for the process to exit. Confirmation therefore comes from the process table.
  - Regression: `tests/agents.test.cjs` ("Stop ends the commands a Codex turn started…") uses a fake client whose app-server is a real Node process, with a helper started before the turn and a long-lived command started during it.
    - With the sweep removed, it fails because the command is left running.
    - With an unreadable process list, the cleanup is held and blocks new work.
    - A retry then stops the command. With the anchor dropped from the record, it fails because the retry confirms while the command still runs.
  - A live probe on 2026-10-19 used real Codex 0.163.0 on Windows with no model call: `thread/start`, then `thread/shellCommand` running a long-lived `node`.
    - The sweep stopped the 3 processes started for the command and confirmed it.
    - The app-server and all 6 processes it had started earlier (MCP helpers) survived.
    - `shellCommand` is not a background terminal, so this checks the Windows process shape, not the unified-exec path.
  - Remaining risks: an MCP helper the app-server itself starts inside the window counts as the turn's. The Linux path (`/proc` table) has not been run on Linux.
  - Job-subtree cancellation stays open until the native acceptance Stop check passes.
- **Engine-side, not Harness:**
  - gamekit's `json.lua` encodes an empty `actions` list as `{}`.
  - Workers added a decode adapter because the prebuilt Player passes Lua tool results through as JSON values.

Model accounting (verified 2026-10-18): delegated job budgets default to twenty recorded model attempts and an optional reported-token limit. Stable request identities persist before worker submission, each structured-response attempt and Jev routing request. Worker reports and SDK final results update the same entry; repeated cumulative reports count once and cached input stays part of input. Missing, malformed, interrupted or overflowing usage remains unknown/incomplete; an explicit zero report is distinct. A configured token limit rejects unmetered backends before admission and holds on unavailable final usage. An admitted call can overshoot before reporting. Changing limits or resuming retains history. CLI dollar reports are shown as provider estimates, not invoices or extra subscription charges; locally calculated rates are not treated as provider cost reports. Initial Hub chat is outside the accepted job's budget. Snapshots expose totals without the request ledger. Sources: `src/job-budgets.cjs`, `src/agents.cjs`, `src/controller.cjs`, `src/controller/turns.cjs`, `src/routing/smart-router.cjs`, `src/providers/jev.cjs`, `src/providers/providers.cjs`, `ui/app.js`, `ui/index.html`. Regressions: `tests/job-budgets.test.cjs`, `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/local-models.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` (pools `job-budgets`, `agents-ui`). These are fixture reports with no paid inference; complete native billing coverage remains unfinished.

Structured retry accounting (verified 2026-10-19): a local/API adapter can reject malformed schema output while attaching valid final usage. `SmartRouter.complete` now retains that usage on the failed attempt before admitting its single formatting retry. Known counts below the configured limit no longer produce a false unknown-usage pause; reaching the limit still prevents a second call. Sources: `src/routing/smart-router.cjs`, `src/providers/providers.cjs`, `src/job-budgets.cjs`; regression: `tests/job-budgets.test.cjs` uses the real API structured parser and budget ledger with deterministic responses.

Rejected submissions (verified 2026-10-19): a `turn/start` or compaction request that the provider rejects before a turn exists now closes its budget request with explicit zero usage. Before, it stayed in `JobBudgets.inflight` until restart, kept the active-time clock running, blocked Resume and updates, and held routine runs open. Sources: `src/controller.cjs` (`submitWorker` and compaction rejection paths), `src/job-budgets.cjs` (`report`); regression `tests/agents.test.cjs` ("rejected turn start closes its budget request").

Direct tracked tasks (verified 2026-10-18): task-enabled foreground requests reserve their allowance before routing. Checks, automatic correction and delegated helpers inherit it; a new untracked question stays separate. Confirmed budget pauses can explicitly continue through the existing private execution context of the same bot. A saved continuation identity prevents duplicate admission after a source-link save failure. The original chat owns the result; its later question is not stopped by cancellation of the old task. Continuations retain the full original goal, accepted model, workspace, minimum current/original permissions, frozen checks, used correction allowance and accepted amendments. Combined images exceeding four/12 MB or missing original images hold without omission. Cached images and the raw request ledger stay out of renderer snapshots. Interrupted foreground execution at restart remains held for manual inspection and a new request; broader uncertain-execution reconciliation is unfinished. Sources: `src/controller.cjs`, `src/controller/task-gate.cjs`, `src/controller/turns.cjs`, `src/controller/providers.cjs`, `src/agents.cjs`, `src/job-budgets.cjs`, `src/runner.cjs`, `ui/app.js`. Regressions: `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/runner.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` (pools `job-budgets`, `agents-ui`).

Asynchronous accounting scope (verified 2026-10-18): routing comparisons capture their originating budget through `AsyncLocalStorage`. A request keeps its stable budget ID and private cancellation callback after its originating turn ends or another chat starts. Live requests keep that job's active-time clock running, and a pause cancels those requests without stopping an unrelated foreground turn. Incomplete requests at restart retain uncertain usage. A budget-originated Jev cancellation does not mark the provider unhealthy or authorize a fallback billing route. Sources: `src/job-budgets.cjs`, `src/agents.cjs`, `src/controller/providers.cjs`, `src/controller/turns.cjs`, `src/providers/jev.cjs`, `src/routing/smart-router.cjs`; regressions: `tests/job-budgets.test.cjs` and `tests/agents.test.cjs`.

Usage handoffs (verified 2026-10-18): owned-job tool listings include current budget totals and limits. Worker delivery saves an immutable usage snapshot in the owner's local result receipt and model-visible handoff text. The snapshot is labelled as observed at delivery, with incomplete totals as lower bounds and subsequent review excluded. This lets Hub report the known figures without reading another bot's storage; job details continues to show current totals. Context reads, retained native history and Markdown export preserve the saved snapshot after the worker conversation is reused. Sources: `src/agents.cjs`, `src/job-budgets.cjs`, `src/conversation-export.cjs`; regression: `tests/agents.test.cjs` (native worker usage fixture).

Active-time budgets (verified 2026-10-18): `src/job-budgets.cjs` adds a persisted allowance to existing delegation records through `budgetId`; it is not a separate job model. Task roots now default to no time, model-attempt or reported-token limits (2026-10-22); explicit limits remain enforced. Regression: `tests/job-budgets.test.cjs`, "task bots and their children have no default time or attempt limits; explicit limits remain enforced". Helpers, scoped mailbox turns and automatic follow-ups retain the same allowance across ownership changes and corrections. One monotonic lease counts elapsed time while any associated controller is executing routing, a provider turn, checks or an approval wait. Capacity/quota waits and idle helper waits are excluded. Clock transitions persist in the primary store; an open lease at restart becomes a paused, bounded estimate. Missing budget records hold affected assignments for review instead of granting unlimited execution. Exhaustion saves the pause before requesting Stop for only that budget's active contexts. Failed saves still stop affected work and keep independent deadline timers armed. Pausing remains visible while execution or cleanup is unresolved; Resume requires the earlier work to settle. The user can change a total limit or explicitly resume with the current version, retaining consumed time, original scope and earlier evidence. Result details remain available from saved receipts after the worker conversation is reused. Complete billing/spend coverage and broader uncertain-execution reconciliation remain unfinished. Sources: `src/job-budgets.cjs`, `src/agents.cjs`, `src/controller.cjs`, `src/controller/coordinator.cjs`, `src/controller/turns.cjs`, `src/controller/task-gate.cjs`, `src/main.cjs`, `src/preload.cjs`, `ui/app.js`, `ui/index.html`, `ui/style.css`. Regressions: `tests/job-budgets.test.cjs`, `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` (pools `job-budgets`, `agents-ui`); all use fake providers, not billed native inference.

Cancellation scope (verified 2026-10-18): stopping an assignment follows its immutable job ancestry, including after ownership transfer. It does not cancel an older transferred helper merely because the original worker conversation is now executing a new assignment. Stopping the retained original ancestor still reaches its descendants. Conversation-level Stop retains its existing broader scope. Sources: `src/agents.cjs` (`cancellationTargets`, `stop`); regression: `tests/agents.test.cjs` (assignment Stop after conversation reuse).

Artifact delivery (verified 2026-10-18): `agent_artifact` publishes existing workspace files/folders during a live delegated or tracked task, with at most sixteen outputs and a 512 MB file limit. Paths resolve within the workspace; known credential paths, file links, escaping junctions and hard-linked files are rejected. Files retain SHA-256 and size, scoped stable IDs and versioned replacement. Saving the local `agentArtifact` receipt and task metadata precedes acknowledgment; failed saves roll both back, and cancellation/revision changes during inspection reject late publication. Completion copies metadata into the single owner's result receipt and model-visible report; context reads and Markdown export retain references. Native resume preserves trusted local receipts and rejects forged artifact items. File availability does not verify a build, launch, rendering or gameplay. Sources: `src/artifacts.cjs`, `src/agents.cjs`, `src/tools/agent-tools.cjs`, `src/conversation-export.cjs`; regressions: `tests/artifacts.test.cjs`, `tests/agents.test.cjs`, pool `artifacts`.

Artifact actions (verified 2026-10-18): result cards and job details offer Open folder and, for recognized native executable headers, explicit Run. Renderer requests carry saved receipt/artifact IDs and the expected version, never arbitrary paths, shell commands or arguments. The runner rechecks the record and content, rejects active work in that workspace, and launches the executable in its containing folder with a filtered environment. This is ordinary user-level execution, not a sandbox; programs can still access their user's files. Open folder requires a current directory, so replacing it with an executable cannot turn navigation into a launch. Runs remain visible globally in the activity disclosure, including after changing chats, with direct Stop and retryable cleanup errors. Window hiding preserves tests; Quit stops them within the existing shutdown deadline, and updates wait for them. Sources: `src/artifacts.cjs`, `src/agents.cjs`, `src/main.cjs`, `src/preload.cjs`, `src/runner.cjs`, `ui/app.js`, `ui/index.html`, `ui/style.css`; regressions: `tests/artifacts.test.cjs`, `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/runner.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` (pool `agents-ui`). Fixtures use temporary files and fake launches, not verified game builds.

`src/agents.cjs` coordinates the existing `Controller` instances. Hub retains `sessions.json`; each named agent uses `agents/<uuid>/sessions.json` under the same application data directory. The primary store holds agent profiles. Each controller owns its provider processes, routing state, approvals, tool bridge, turn queue and cleanup state. `src/runner.cjs` gives each agent its own `Providers` instance but shares the primary's `LocalModels`, so local model locks span agents.

Tray images use ICO on Windows and PNG on other platforms; Linux Electron cannot load the ICO tray asset. The real-app quit test verifies startup and complete worker cleanup (verified 2026-10-20, `src/main.cjs`, `tests/quit-live.cjs`).

Jev quick answers (Jev answering a low-risk yes/no question itself) were removed on 2026-10-29 at the user's request: confusing next to Model for simple messages, which covers cheap answers. There is no `jevQuickAnswers` setting, no `direct`/`quickAnswer` Jev question and no direct-answer branch in `SmartRouter.choose` or `Controller.send`. Routes saved with `directAnswer` before then are still skipped when history is read (`pickPreset`, `turns.cjs`, `suggestions.cjs`, the UI's route display), so old chats show their past Jev answers.

Jev version discovery (verified 2026-10-20 against https://docs.typesafe.ai/models and fixture tests): Harness requests the provider's `jev-latest` stable alias. `JevClient.model` is populated only from a validated successful response's `model` field; controller snapshots and the dropdown use that returned value. Before a successful call, the label says version pending, and later failures retain the last successfully reported version. Since 2026-10-21, at the user's request, one small test (the Test action's sample) runs whenever Jev becomes usable: at start with a key saved and Jev on (`main.cjs` after `runner.initialize()`), after saving a key, and when Jev is switched on (`Controller.checkJev`); a failure is logged as "Jev check failed" and shows in Jev's health. The model-list endpoint currently lists aliases and cannot reliably resolve their serving versions. Sources: `src/providers/jev.cjs`, `src/controller.cjs`, `ui/app.js`; regressions: `tests/routing-calls.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`. The reported version is shared by every bot's client (`runner.cjs` passes one `{ model }` holder to each `JevClient`; found 2026-10-21: each bot had its own client, and Settings shows the selected bot's snapshot, so a Test on the Hub's client never changed a bot's view). It is refreshed by normal Jev use, the Test action and the checks above.

Bot model editing (verified 2026-10-20): composer gear menus are removed; a direct context indicator replaces them. Model and effort controls stay hidden, with model choices in Edit. Model choices live in Edit; `/model` and `/effort` open the selected bot's Edit dialog. Main Bot Edit offers its name and available structured-response models with effort variants, without Auto; existing Auto configurations display their `hubMode`. Worker Edit retains Auto. Opening Main Edit while a worker is selected reads the main snapshot and saves only the main settings. The existing `renameHub` host method accepts either its original name string or `{name, mode}`, validates the model, and saves both fields together with rollback on failure. Main Bot sending still requires an available explicit selection; saved/background Hub Auto flows remain compatible. Sources: `ui/app.js`, `ui/index.html`, `src/agents.cjs`; regressions: `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`.

Routing model control (verified 2026-10-20): General labels its single selector "Routing model" (renamed from "Routing model (Auto)" 2026-10-29), containing Jev (including the provider-reported model identifier from `state.jev.model`) and available routing models. Choosing Jev saves `routing: jev` and retains the saved model for fallback; choosing a model saves `routing: smart` and `routerPreset` together through the existing settings validator. Disabled/unavailable Jev remains visible when saved, with the fallback reason in the selector tooltip; plan routing overrides disable the selector. Agent settings tools expose the same combined `routingModel` value. No stored-settings migration is needed. Sources: `ui/index.html`, `ui/app.js`, `src/settings-tools.cjs`. Regressions: `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`, `tests/settings-tools.test.cjs`.

**Phone layout (2026-10-26, after the user opened browser access on an Android phone through Tailscale and compared it with Grok's app).** At `(max-width:720px), (max-height:500px)`, the same query in `ui/style.css`, `phoneLayout` in `ui/app.js` and the settings-tab orientation in `ui/mosaic.js`:
- One screen at a time: the bot list (`body.phone-list`, the sidebar at full width and scrolling as one page with Settings sticky at the bottom) or the chat (no sidebar, `#phone-back` in the header). This replaced the 64 px icon rail.
- `selectAgent` calls `showPhoneChat` before loading, so a chat that fails to load still opens. Opening a chat from the list pushes one history entry (`{ phoneChat: true }`), so Android's back gesture and the back button return to the list.
- Replies use the full width (`.message:not(.user)`).
- `body` height is `100dvh`: with `100vh`, phone Chrome put the composer under its bottom bar.
- `.remote-view main > header` drops the desktop window-button gap to 12 px.
- Tests: the phone sizes 412x800 and 915x360 in `tests/mosaic-ui-check.cjs` check the chat screen (no sidebar, back visible, the title fits in the browser view), the list (fills the screen, labels visible, the last bot scrolls into view above Settings) and the back/history round trip. Screenshots are `.scratch/mosaic/{phone-chat,list,chat}-dark-WxH.png`, with the "before" set in `.scratch/phone-before/`.
- Tapping a row there is not tested: `selectAgent` needs fleet state that check doesn't load.
- Not like Grok: no time column or reply preview for idle bots in the list, no pill-shaped composer, and no floating header.
- The browser server reads `ui/` from disk with `no-store`, so a phone reload picks up UI changes without restarting the runner; backend changes still need a restart.
- Reply suggestions on touch screens: phones have no Right arrow, so `#suggestion-accept` sits beside Send while a suggestion shows. `acceptSuggestion` is shared with the arrow key.
  - It shows only under `(pointer: coarse)`. There it gets its own grid column (5, with Send at 6 and Stop at 7), so the desktop composer grid is unchanged. In the ≤720 px flex row it follows markup order, before Send.
  - Smoke: `Emulation.setTouchEmulationEnabled` turns on `pointer: coarse`. Wait for `painted()` before capturing, or the screenshot can miss the button.
- **Installable app (2026-10-26, user approved keeping the phone signed in):**
  - `ui/manifest.webmanifest` (start `/ui/index.html`, scope `/ui/`, standalone) with `ui/icon-192.png` and `ui/icon-512.png`, scaled from `ui/icon.png` by `.scratch/app-icons.cjs`.
  - `RemoteRunner` serves `.webmanifest` as `application/manifest+json` and adds the manifest link plus `theme-color` only to the browser's `index.html`.
  - Chrome's criteria (web.dev, updated 2024-09) list no service worker, so there is none.
  - `ui/remote-api.js` keeps the session ticket in `localStorage` (was `sessionStorage`). An installed app starts a new context, so a per-tab ticket signed it out on every launch.
  - `remember()` moves an old tab ticket over, and forgets the ticket on Disconnect or a 401.
  - Tests: `tests/remote.test.cjs` covers the manifest, icons and link, plus the ticket move and forget.
  - Real-device check (2026-10-26, user's Redmi Note 13 Pro+, Chrome, Tailscale Serve `https://harness-pc.tailfa589b.ts.net`, tailnet only): after a runner restart (`--quit-for-install`, then the launcher) and one reload, Chrome installed it. The installed app opens full screen and signed in.
  - Pitfall, same day: after closing Chrome and the app, the phone showed "can't connect". `tailscale status` showed `harness-phone` offline while the runner and the HTTPS address were fine, and Harness logged no requests. Switching Tailscale back on in the phone app fixed it.
  - Cause, per the user: one "close all apps" sweep in recents also closed Tailscale.
  - On this Xiaomi phone, locking Tailscale's card in recents keeps it out of the sweep. Always-on VPN (Settings → Network → VPN) and Autostart / unrestricted battery for Tailscale also bring it back.
  - Security check from the phone (2026-10-27, same phone on LTE, over adb + Chrome DevTools, no key used):
    - `tailscale serve status` and `tailscale funnel status`: tailnet only, no Funnel; the tailnet holds only `harness-pc` and `harness-phone`. Harness listens on `127.0.0.1:8788` only; tailscaled holds 443 on the tailnet addresses.
    - Same-origin requests from the phone's Chrome without credentials: `/api/status` and `/api/call` 401 (no or forged token), `/api/pair` with a wrong key 401, `/src/remote.cjs` and `/ui/%2e%2e/src/remote.cjs` 404, a made-up `/api/download/` 404.
    - From `https://example.com`, all eight requests failed in Chrome before reaching Harness (`LocalNetworkAccessPermissionDenied`, after the user tapped Block on Chrome's local-network prompt). Harness's own Origin check (403) is the second layer, checked from the PC.
    - Tailscale off on the phone: `ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED` in Chrome and 100% ping loss to `100.74.95.90`, although public DNS answers that address. Back on: 200 from `100.74.95.90` over h2.
    - Tailscale was off at the start although the user believed it was on (the recents sweep above). `am broadcast -a com.tailscale.ipn.CONNECT_VPN` (or `DISCONNECT_VPN`) `-n com.tailscale.ipn/.IPNReceiver` switches it over adb.
    - Driving Android Chrome 155: `/json/new` answers "Could not create new page", so open tabs with `Target.createTarget` on the browser endpoint; background tabs freeze, so `Target.activateTarget` before `Runtime.evaluate`.
- **Browser bytes (2026-10-26, the user found Settings and actions slow on the phone; after the fix and restart the user confirmed on the phone that it works):**
  - Cause: `main.cjs` broadcast the full fleet state, uncompressed, to every browser on every change (the desktop window had used `trimFleet` since 2026-10-25), and the browser's Settings button re-fetched it all with `api.bootstrap()`.
  - Measured then: Tailscale direct with a 46 ms round trip, yet 65 MB sent to the phone within minutes of reconnecting (91 MB about 30 minutes later).
  - Now `RemoteRunner.sendState` trims per browser connection (`client.sent`), with everything every `FULL_STATE_MS` as the safety net. It was 60 s at first, which cost about 2 MB a minute over two phone connections (measured 2026-10-26: 0 bytes for 50 s, then a 2.07 MB burst), so it is now 10 minutes. While a browser is behind, only the newest raw state waits (`pendingState`) and it is trimmed when written, so a replaced state cannot leave stale chats.
  - The event stream is gzip (`createGzip({ flush: Z_SYNC_FLUSH })`, back-pressure from the gzip stream). `json()` replies over 1 KB and text UI files are gzip when accepted, compressed asynchronously because this is Electron's main process.
  - In the browser (`api.remote`), Settings opens from the pushed state with no `bootstrap`.
  - Action replies (`load`, `settings`, `send` and so on) still carry snapshots, compressed but not trimmed: trimming them would record chats as sent even if the page never applied that reply.
  - Tests in `tests/remote.test.cjs`: changed versus kept bots, no change lost while behind, gzip stream framing, and gzip replies and assets only when accepted.
- Keyboard voice typing (Gboard) was blocked on the user's phone with an "access" message. Harness sends no microphone restriction (only a CSP, no Permissions-Policy). The user attributes it to Bitdefender on the phone (2026-10-26, not investigated further).

**Settings tabs (2026-10-26, at the user's request).** Settings has three new tabs after General: Browser, Routing and Tools.
- Tab order: General, Browser, Routing, Tools, Providers, Connectors, Benchmarks, Checks, About.
- Browser: the former General → Browser access expander, now `#remote-settings` as a plain `div` under a "Browser access" heading, plus `#remote-logout`. `.remote-view` still hides the settings and shows the logout.
- Routing: Routing model, Effort cap, Model for simple messages, Compare with Jev.
- Tools: Project search, Tool recommendations.
- General keeps Appearance, On Exit, Start at sign-in, Reply suggestions, the Chat checker, reviser, escalator and rule finder model with its four switches (2026-10-29), and Show tips.
- Code: panels in `ui/index.html`, hidden and shown in the tab `onclick` in `ui/app.js`; icons are in `ui/mosaic.js` `paths`. Label spacing (`#panel-general label`) also covers `#panel-routing`, `#panel-tools` and the Checks tab's own switch (`#panel-checks > label`, not the check rows).
- Each of General, Browser, Routing and Tools has a one-line `p.hint` right under its title (user's request). Routing's line says it picks for messages sent on Auto and for the escalation model while it is on Auto (2026-10-29). Connectors already had one; Providers, Benchmarks and Checks open with their own explanation.
- Tips, README and this page now say Settings → Routing / Browser.
- Smoke pitfall: a `capturePage` right after changing a panel's `scrollTop` can show the previous frame, which looked like a tab scrolled past its title or another tab's text above it. Wait about 100 ms before capturing.
- The UI smoke checks the tab order and each tab's controls, and screenshots every tab (`.scratch/settings-tab-*.png`).
- Pitfall: the smoke's 30 s deadline last refreshed at "provider settings", so the extra screenshot steps pushed later phases past it. It now also refreshes at "agent controls".

**Settings model pickers (2026-10-26, user's marked screenshot).** Every Settings model picker is now a model picker with an effort picker beside it, like the composer and the Edit and Hub dialogs:
- Routing model: `#settings-router-preset` + `#settings-router-effort`
- Chat checker, reviser, escalator and rule finder model: `#settings-chat-scan` + `#settings-chat-scan-effort`; its switches `#settings-check-responses`, `#settings-checker-revise`, `#settings-checker-escalate`, `#settings-scan-replies`; Checks tab `#settings-escalate-failed-checks`
- Escalation model: `#settings-escalation-model` + `#settings-escalation-effort` (General); Routing and Checks link to it (`[data-setting]` links, handled next to the tab code in `ui/app.js`)
- Model for simple messages (Auto): `#settings-low-model` + `#settings-low-effort`
- Benchmarks → Refresh worker: `#benchmark-worker` + `#benchmark-worker-effort`

How they work, in `ui/app.js`:
- `fillModelEffort` fills each pair: the non-model choices first (Jev, Off or Auto), then the available models grouped by provider. A saved model that is no longer offered shows as unavailable.
- `modelEffortChoice` turns a pair back into one preset ID; a newly picked model starts at its lowest effort. Saved settings keep their one-ID shape, so nothing migrates.
- `modelEffortSettings` maps the three saved settings to their pairs. `saveSetting` re-renders a pair after saving, since the pickers hold a model and a level rather than the ID.
- The effort picker reads "—" for Jev or Off and "Chosen by Auto" for Auto.

The one-line `presetOption` lists were removed. The smoke screenshots are `.scratch/router-model-setting.png`, `chat-checker-setting.png`, `low-model-setting.png` and `benchmark-worker-setting.png`.

Router speed (measured 2026-10-25, ATH question "which Python command do your project instructions say to use?"): routing time is the router model's thinking, not data reading. With grok-4.7 at xhigh through Cursor, each classification spent about 100 s thinking (first thought 3 s after the prompt, first answer 100 s later), plus about 6 s to start a fresh Cursor process per call; it asked for a workspace look, so two calls took 135 s in the user's chat and 230 s in `.scratch/route-timing-probe.cjs xhigh`. The workspace scan took 0.4 s. The same probe at low effort made one call, about 36 s thinking, 47 s in total; Jev routed the previous Auto message in 3.6 s. The user then compared routers in the app: Terra low, Haiku low, DeepSeek, Gemma (LM Studio), grok-4.7 low and Opus xhigh were all fast; only grok-4.7 at xhigh was slow.

Agent settings tools (verified 2026-10-20 with service, bridge, appearance and UI fixtures): `read_settings({names?, agentId?})` lists current settings, write formats, manual-only fields and recent change outcomes. `write_settings({name,value,agentId?})` requests one change through a desktop confirmation; it returns queued and applies through existing validators only after agents and process cleanup are idle. Agents must finish their reply rather than poll for their own queued change. The UI reports applied/failed outcomes; reads never return provider/Jev secrets. Permissions, workspace paths, browser access, credentials, provider endpoints/connections, executable checks and sign-in autostart remain manual-only by user preference. Appearance, exit behavior, routing/helper options, model choices and benchmark evidence are writable after confirmation. Pending changes are memory-only and cancelled on restart; no raw settings/session JSON writes are exposed. Headless runs without a confirmation UI cannot approve writes. Sources: `src/settings-tools.cjs`, `src/tools/agent-tools.cjs`, `src/agents.cjs`, `src/runner.cjs`, `src/main.cjs`, `src/preload.cjs`, `ui/appearance.js`, `ui/app.js`. Tests: `tests/settings-tools.test.cjs`, `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/appearance.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`. No paid provider calls or real login changes were exercised.

Update indicator (2026-10-20): the small blue sidebar download button remains visible when an available-update banner is dismissed; clicking it reopens confirmation, or opens the official release page when automatic installation is unavailable. Installed Windows builds check 10 seconds after startup and every 12 hours (`src/main.cjs`); manual and agent checks run immediately. Sources: `ui/index.html`, `ui/style.css`, `ui/app.js`. Regression: `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`.

Agent-requested app updates (verified 2026-10-20 with fixtures): `harness_check_update` is exposed through the shared agent tool catalog to Hub and worker providers. It checks the running version against the official release and reopens the desktop update banner with a download/install confirmation. The tool accepts no arguments and has no installation authority; only the user's banner button starts the existing verified-installer flow, after active work finishes. Source/headless runs report the release link without offering automatic installation. Sources: `src/tools/agent-tools.cjs`, `src/agents.cjs`, `src/runner.cjs`, `src/updater.cjs`, `ui/app.js`. Regressions: `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/updater.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`. No real installer or live provider was run for this change.

Update admission (verified 2026-10-20 with 152 focused tests and the full UI smoke): `src/runner.cjs:updateBlockedReason` supplies both installer admission and `Agents.snapshot` so selecting another bot cannot hide a global blocker. The banner disables installation and explains real work, approvals, queued runnable messages, provider connection, or cleanup. Paused saved messages/results, held interrupted assignments, and the retained draft of a cancelled delivered assignment do not block an idle restart; the check does not modify or resume them. Active turns, cleanup, and uncertain submissions still block, including changes during download. Regression fixtures reproduce a cancelled update-checker report in a paused owner queue. Sources: `src/runner.cjs`, `src/agents.cjs`, `src/updater.cjs`, `ui/app.js`; tests: `tests/runner.test.cjs`, `tests/updater.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`. No real installer was launched.

Runner lifetime (verified 2026-10-18): `src/runner.cjs` owns execution startup independently of the window. Closing the view hides it and keeps drafts, jobs and approval waits alive. The tray reopens the same view and offers explicit Quit runner; `src/main.cjs` retains bounded process cleanup on Quit. Electron's per-user single-instance lock and the existing sender/frame checks protect local commands; no HTTP command port was added. The isolated `tests/runner-live.cjs` check verifies close/reopen, a second launch sharing the same runner, preserved work/draft/approval state and rejection of another renderer. Its providers are disabled and it changes no login items. `tests/runner.test.cjs` covers lifecycle and autostart arguments. Settings and the tray offer start at sign-in; it remains off unless explicitly enabled. Updates wait for runnable assignments/messages, approvals and cleanup (see Update admission above), then close admission before installer launch; failed launch reopens admission (`src/updater.cjs`, `tests/updater.test.cjs`). Contained restart recovery and authenticated browser transport are described below; older uncontained interruptions still need manual review.

Native providers (verified 2026-10-18): Gemini CLI was removed on 2026-10-19 (Antigravity CLI serves Gemini models); the native CLIs are Antigravity, Muse and DeepSeek Harness. A native-only account can route and execute without Codex; disabled native accounts perform only binary inspection. `src/providers/muse.cjs` uses the Muse SDK fold over MSP, refuses any MSP schema version other than 1, selects and verifies native permission profiles, resumes the same session and attaches MCP without changing project configuration. A served schema fingerprint that differs from the SDK's pin is accepted (2026-10-25): Muse updates itself, and the SDK's own contract (`checkServedFingerprint` in `@muse-code/sdk` `fingerprint.js`) calls a mismatch a warning under additive-optional evolution. The old hard gate broke Connect when Muse 1.4.13 (`sha256:7fddaf…`) installed itself on 2026-10-24 over SDK 1.4.12 (`sha256:cfdb6d…`): sign-in succeeded, the refresh after it failed, and the row stayed "connect native sign-in". `login()` now refuses an unsupported schema without opening the Meta page. Checked live 2026-10-25 under the checkout's Electron: 1.4.13 refreshes signed in with 5 models; after a restart the user connected it in the app and it answered a short read-only message (SDK 1.4.12 against CLI 1.4.13). Regression: "Muse incompatible schemas…" in `tests/muse.test.cjs`. Muse also answers Hub and routing replies, read-only and tool-free, since 2026-10-31 (see "Every model can run the Hub"). The installed binary reports its sandbox unavailable on this Windows host; Workspace access therefore fails before any prompt, while Ask selects the native read-only profile. Counted-once usage comes from `session/tokenUsage`; missing usage stays unknown. Native discovery checked Gemini 0.49.0 and Muse 1.4.10 without inference; both still need native sign-in. Antigravity has a text-only, Full-access worker adapter; see its current boundaries below. Fixtures and registry regressions are in `tests/gemini.test.cjs`, `tests/muse.test.cjs`, `tests/native-cli.test.cjs` and pool `native-cli`.

`Agents.saveAgent` persists and returns the profile without awaiting `initializeController`. Startup runs in the background with errors retained in the controller snapshot. `Controller.initialize(shared)` reuses the primary Claude/Cursor status and models after primary discovery completes instead of repeating their auth/model-discovery subprocesses; each agent still connects its own Codex app-server. Closing guards prevent a pending configuration or bridge startup from launching another worker after shutdown begins. Shared provider refresh waits for initialization before updating a worker.

Result ownership (verified 2026-10-18): each delegated job has one authoritative `owner` conversation and monotonically increasing `ownerVersion` in its existing recipient store; older records fall back to `source` and version 1. `agent_transfer_owner` requires the active current owner, stable handoff id, expected version and compatible recipient access. A pinned offer and receiver acceptance persist before committing the owner pointer. Retrying cannot change the target or start another worker. Notification failure reports committed ownership with `notificationPending`, rather than falsely reporting that ownership stayed put. The new owner receives the final report once, with the assignment scope; the old owner sees a recorded handoff and can open its receiving chat. Native resume and export retain these local mailbox receipts. Restart reconciles committed receipts and suppresses automatic handoff-message replay. A received final report cannot be transferred during an unconfirmed final worker save. `source` and immutable `ancestorJobIds` retain original cancellation ancestry: stopping a completed parent still cancels its transferred descendants while leaving unrelated new-owner work active. Sources: `src/agents.cjs`, `src/tools/agent-tools.cjs`, `src/controller/coordinator.cjs`, `ui/app.js`; regressions: `tests/agents.test.cjs`, UI smoke, pool `local-agents`. Revision, correction and separate foreground execution behavior are documented below; live-provider validation remains unfinished.

Verified 2026-10-10: agents execute independently, with no global worker-slot cap, workspace lease or Full-access exclusivity. `Agents.acquire` retains closing, Stop and cross-controller cleanup checks but no longer queues by another agent's workspace/access; `Controller.readyToStart` applies the same cleanup guards before execution. `Agents.pump` handles assignment promotion, result delivery and queued conversation/mailbox work. Each controller still permits one active turn and queues its own messages. Same-directory and nested-directory workers keep their original access and can start together. Files remain shared, so concurrent edits can conflict; provider limits and local-server inference/model-management locks remain in effect. `tests/agents.test.cjs` verifies five simultaneous Full-access workers, separate provider conversations/queues, nested workspaces, changing access without a global lock, and targeted Stop. `tests/coordinator.test.cjs` verifies four Full-access batch reviewers start concurrently with the exact requested model and effort.

`src/tools/agent-tools.cjs` defines `agents_list`, `agent_models`, `agent_create`, `agent_delegate`, `agent_read_context`, `agent_stop`, `agent_set_access` and `agent_message`. Since 2026-10-19 there is no `agent_delete`: no agent, including Hub, can delete an agent. Hub's action schema and the helper bridge (`Controller.bridgeTool`, which forwards only listed agent tools) both refuse it. Only the user's own delete (`deleteAgent` IPC from the UI or browser) removes a profile; regressions are "no agent can delete another agent" in `tests/agents.test.cjs` and "Hub cannot delete agents" in `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`. Every agent, including Hub and agents performing delegated work, can create a persistent idle agent. `Agents.tool` accepts only name, role instructions and optional mode/effort for creation, inherits workspace/access from the active conversation, and reuses `saveAgent` validation and background startup. Creation cannot edit an existing profile, expand permissions or start work. Delegation restrictions still apply separately. `src/controller/helpers.cjs` awaits asynchronous creation results and retains active-session/turn checks before dispatch.

Tool-created agents default to Auto, independent of the creator's model. `agent_models` groups available worker presets by provider/model and returns a default mode plus supported efforts. The default uses the saved preferred variant, else medium, else the first catalog variant, consistent with existing model selection fallbacks. Creator instructions require discovering the user's requested model and setting effort only when requested. `agent_create` resolves the mode and an optional effort through the live catalog and stores the resulting existing preset id; unsupported/unavailable selections fail before persistence. Auto rejects an exact effort override and continues using the shared Auto cap. No provider configuration or creator selection changes. The selected mode persists on the child's profile and is used by direct sends and delegation.

`Agents.manageAgent` exposes stop, delete and access changes through the same active-turn MCP/dynamic-tool paths. Tools target other agents and return compact acknowledgements rather than fleet snapshots. Stop uses the existing descendant cancellation and queue pausing, with a stopping/stopped result; Hub is a valid stop target. Delete reuses the UI deletion guards and retained recovery files. It rechecks the initiating turn after awaiting target initialization, so a stopped/finished caller cannot delete later. Hub remains undeletable.

Access changes accept only the three existing `ACCESS_MODES`, cannot exceed the caller’s current conversation level, and require Full access for another workspace. The target must be idle without loading, requests, cleanup, queued messages or pending delegated results. `Agents.settings` updates its profile default and current conversation and clears saved approvals and loaded provider state; Hub is supported. Stop/delete are roster management and can target another workspace. Creation always inherits the caller’s current conversation access, not its profile default; this is covered at all three levels through child and grandchild creation. Changing a parent later does not rewrite existing child permissions.

`Agents.readContext` serves the target's current conversation through the shared `readText` reader in `src/tools/tool-helpers.cjs`, using the same literal query and line/column retrieval as `router_read_output`. `agents_list` includes Hub and current conversation workspace/access. An active caller may read same-workspace conversations; different workspaces or a target with Full access require Full access on the caller. The controller's existing session/turn guard and `Agents.tool` argument validation apply to all providers. Unknown/deleted agents fail closed. No target task or empty conversation is created.

Context retrieval serializes only visible user/assistant/plan messages, commands, changes and recorded tool/search results. It excludes reasoning items, hidden session metadata, unsent messages and user image payloads. Calls return at most 30 lines and 8,000 excerpt characters, with clipping flags, line/column provenance, agent/session identity, a content hash and busy state. The transcript is read live on every call; compare hashes across pages rather than assuming a working agent is finished. It does not expose raw session stores or write shared transcript copies. Retrieved content is untrusted evidence, never fresh instructions or authorization. Workspace/access checks describe the conversation's current settings, not per-message historical scopes.

The bundled MCP in `src/tools/router-bridge.cjs` exports these tools for all providers. `Controller.loadThread` supplies a session-bound Codex MCP configuration on both start and resume, restricted to the agent tools; old conversations keep their thread IDs and history. Saved Codex dynamic agent tools remain supported, while new threads use the MCP. Other helper tools remain restricted to the Claude/Cursor MCP or Codex dynamic-tool path. The stdio child runs Electron in Node mode. Configuration follows the [official OpenAI MCP documentation](https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/extend/mcp?surface=cli); the locally generated Codex 0.162.0-alpha.2 protocol confirms resume accepts config overrides but cannot replace dynamic tools. Agent instructions are appended by `Controller.workerInstructions` and refreshed with the existing instruction-change handling.

Existing Codex conversations can retain their original developer instructions despite a resume override. `Controller.loadThread` explicitly appends the current agent instructions as a developer message with `thread/inject_items` after resume. Those instructions identify the deferred MCP tool by its exact name and require a successful `agent_create` result before claiming creation. Provider-native subagents never enter the Harness sidebar. `loadThread` requests `features.multi_agent=false` for all agent conversations, including untracked turns; the explicit instruction remains necessary because the installed Codex 0.162.0-alpha.2 runtime still exposes its separate collaboration tools.

`RouterBridge.childConfig` preapproves only the local agent tools, which already enforce workspace/access and delegation limits in `Agents.tool`. Other MCP tools retain their approval policies and are excluded from this Codex bridge. Merely discovering the tool is insufficient: without this policy, its execution can be rejected by Codex before reaching Harness. `tests/agent-tools-smoke.cjs` uses the installed Codex with a local mock Responses stream, the real controller/MCP backend, an isolated store and the renderer. It verifies that an existing conversation receives the current developer instructions, a scripted tool call persists an idle agent with inherited permissions, and the state event adds it to the sidebar. It performs no paid model inference and does not test a live model's tool choice.

Live verification on 2026-10-09: Hub's existing Codex conversation, routed to gpt-6.1-sol at low effort, executed `phasma_harness.agent_create` for the original creation request. The profile persisted and appeared Ready in the sidebar without starting a task. Claude/Cursor creation is covered by the deterministic backend tests, not by this live check.

Each agent now has one persistent conversation, selected through `Agents.conversation` and the snapshot `conversationId`. The create IPC returns that same conversation on repeated calls. `migrateConversations` retains the bound/first visible chat and moves other visible chats into named agents, preserving IDs and history; it saves the target and profile before removing the source, and detects an already-copied ID on retry. Archived chats remain in their original stores.

Delegation reuses the target conversation and inherits the lower of the source and target conversation permissions. Busy targets accept persisted `delegationQueue` records without changing the current turn, title or permissions. The queue drains in FIFO order after current work, direct follow-ups, mailbox work and helper waits settle. Acceptance returns a stable `jobId` and session ID; promotion saves the queue removal and new active record together. Permissions use the lower of the accepted ceiling and current conversation access, and the accepted model mode is retained. Failed saves roll back acceptance or promotion. Queue records count toward the existing global ten-assignment limit. Each task has a unique delivery ID and message start index, so successive tasks produce distinct receipts and only return new results. Requests cannot cross workspaces without Full access. Tasks are sent through chat tools; the Delegate UI and IPC are removed. Delegation is asynchronous so a parent can finish and release its workspace before its child starts. Hub requests allow ten delegations; other source requests allow three, including automatic continuations. There are ten pending assignments across the app, counting active and queued records. Agent profile count is uncapped. Every agent may ask another idle agent for work, including Hub and agents already doing delegated work. Task text preserves pasted whitespace and identifies its sender. Stored ancestors and the pending dependency graph reject circular requests, including a cycle through busy recipients; self-requests are refused. Hub uses its controller settings for execution and its permanent name in results.

`Agents.pendingDelegations` keeps parent jobs open until their descendants return and queued result continuations finish. The snapshot exposes waiting conversations so the sidebar shows Waiting and retains Stop. Stopping a requester pauses replies and recursively stops its pending child tasks; cancellation also delivers an interrupted result when a running turn or its checks settle. Results use the existing message queue. A source conversation's delivery receipt and reply are saved together before marking the target delivered, preventing duplicate return messages. Worker reports also persist as local `agentResult` items in the owner conversation before its model resumes, with delivery identity, worker identity, the original owner, task title, actual outcome, bounded report and truncation flag. Reports remain visible with a full or paused owner queue; a failed owner save rolls back the receipt and acknowledgment so delivery can retry without duplication. Context reads and Markdown exports retain the report (`src/agents.cjs`, `src/conversation-export.cjs`, `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/conversation-export.test.cjs`; verified 2026-10-18). Stopped sources keep their queue paused. If a queue is full, the result remains visible without starting another turn. Restart marks unfinished handoffs interrupted; review their conversation before retrying. Queued assignments survive as interrupted records marked Needs review and are not automatically replayed. Their Stop action targets the job ID, remains valid if it has just become active, and is idempotent after cancellation. Stopping a requester cancels its queued children without interrupting a recipient’s unrelated work.
