Queued cancellation is persisted before its return receipt; a failed receipt save cannot make the cancelled job executable. Editing, clearing, deleting or changing workspace/access is blocked while queued work is unresolved. Agent-wide Stop cancels incoming queues too. Verified 2026-10-18 against `src/agents.cjs` and `tests/agents.test.cjs`. `Agents.shutdown` pauses queues, stops every controller, closes peers and invokes the existing global process cleanup once.

`Agents.clearConversation` resets a selected agent only after the main-process confirmation callback returns true. `src/main.cjs` uses a native Cancel/Clear dialog with Cancel selected by default. The method checks the shared `requireIdle` guards before and after confirmation and verifies that the target conversation is still the same. It archives the old conversation and binds a fresh session in one store save, rolling back both the session list and binding on failure. The new session carries only workspace/access and fresh empty state; Codex/Claude/Cursor thread identifiers, history, usage and task state are not reused. Old approvals and loaded-thread caches are dropped. Archives stay local for recovery and are excluded from the visible-conversation migration. Clear is a user action, not an agent tool.

`ui/app.js` handles `/help` (also `/`), `/compact`, `/clear`, `/stop`, `/agents`, `/settings`, `/context`, `/model`, `/effort`, `/access`, `/switch`, `/export`, `/status` and `/wiki` before normal message submission. `/wiki` (2026-10-25, `suggestWiki` in `src/controller/suggestions.cjs`) asks the chat's model at its lowest effort, the same choice reply suggestions use, for durable wiki changes from the last 12 visible messages and `docs/wiki/index.md`; they appear as a notice whose Apply sends them to the chat's bot. Harness writes nothing itself. Commands bypass routing previews and model sends; unknown commands, arguments and attached images are rejected without losing the input. The Commands dialog and composer suggestions share the same `COMMANDS` table. Typing `/` in the focused composer opens all commands; an alphabetic prefix filters names case-insensitively. Up/Down changes the active option, Enter/click completes and dispatches through `runCommand`, Tab only completes, and Escape or blur dismisses. The combobox/listbox retains textarea focus and exposes its selected option with `aria-activedescendant`. No match shows an empty state. Suggestions hide for normal text, arguments and in-progress input operations; existing confirmation and provider guards remain in effect. Clear changes only the selected agent and clears its draft/images after confirmation; Cancel leaves them intact. Stop resolves the selected conversation explicitly so an idle named agent never accidentally stops Hub. Local commands remain accessible offline; compaction retains its provider connection/capability checks.

`/model` and `/effort` open the selected agent's Edit dialog on its Model or Effort picker (since 2026-10-21, at the user's request, both Edit dialogs pick like the composer: models grouped by provider, a model's efforts in a second picker on the same row, greyed out when the model has no choice ("Chosen by Auto" for Auto); Access sits under Model, and the grid (2fr/1fr) is the same at every width (the old auto-fit columns and the 720 px stack are gone). DeepSeek and Antigravity report no effort levels (Antigravity lists each effort as its own model); shared helpers `presetGroups`, `groupedOptions`, `fillEfforts`, `dialogChoice` in `ui/app.js`; the Auto effort cap lives in Settings → Routing and next to Auto in the composer; checked against `openCommandPanel` in `ui/app.js` 2026-10-21). `/access` and `/switch` open the shared searchable command dialog, with keyboard navigation, selected-choice indicators, Escape cancellation and stale-choice validation. Model and conversation-access changes target the selected agent through existing settings/permissions handlers; access chosen before the first conversation is saved to the agent profile and survives switching agents. `/context` uses the same provider-reported estimate as the context meter and shows remaining capacity only when both usage and context size are known. Successful Codex/Claude compaction records `lastCompactedAt`; old chats without a timestamp report that it is unknown. `/status` shows connection, activity, task, model, access, queued messages, input requests and outstanding delegated child tasks. `/export` captures visible message/plan text through `src/conversation-export.cjs`, saves Markdown with a native file picker, marks attachments as omitted and excludes internal/wiki-maintenance messages, private reasoning and raw tool arguments. Cancellation or save failure preserves the command for retry. UI, file export and compaction regressions are in `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`, `tests/conversation-export.test.cjs`, `tests/core.test.cjs` and `tests/providers-optional.test.cjs`. Verified 2026-10-09.

The renderer selects a controller snapshot from `agentStates`; IDs sent over IPC identify the session owner. Stop and approval responses carry the selected agent/session identity. `deleteAgent` rejects Hub, invalid IDs, active work, loading, cleanup, requests, queued messages and outstanding delegated results. After initialization finishes it rechecks those guards, persists profile removal, detaches and closes the controller, and retains its local files for recovery. Shared provider and settings changes are blocked while any agent is running. Profiles supply workspace, mode and access defaults; active conversation permissions remain on the conversation.

`ui/app.js` renders a permanently pinned Hub area, a named-agent list that uses the remaining sidebar height with a visible scrollbar, and an Overview dialog with agent cards. The old session/workspace sidebar controls remain removed. Empty chats use one centered prompt with compact suggestions. Hub also hides the informational model-preview row above its composer; the model used remains visible on each reply. Worker routing previews remain available (ui/app.js, verified 2026-10-10). A compact + button in the sidebar toolbar creates agents; the bottom New agent/Edit controls are removed, and editing remains available from the context menu. The sidebar, chat welcome area, model selector and confirmation dialogs omit decorative brand marks (`ui/index.html`, `ui/style.css`, `ui/dialogs.js`). The PE-style rainbow gear with a central lavender H is shared by the Windows title bar/taskbar, runner tray, installer and shortcut via `ui/icon.ico`, and the About panel loads that same asset. The ICO contains 16, 24, 32, 48, 64, 128 and 256 px frames; `ui/icon.png` retains the full-resolution source (verified 2026-10-20 against `src/main.cjs`, `ui/index.html` and `package.json`). The composer uses 5.5px vertical and 6px horizontal padding: the default single-line bar is 45px tall instead of 54px (one-sixth shorter), with controls closer to the edges. The composer starts as one row: attachment, a borderless native access selector (red shield for full access), message, chat-settings gear, dictation and Send/Stop. When the draft exceeds the space between the controls or contains a newline, it moves to a full-width row above them; shorter drafts return inline. Controls stay anchored at the bottom (`resizeComposer` in `ui/app.js`, `ui/style.css`, verified 2026-10-20). Access keeps its keyboard behavior and exposes its current mode in its accessible label and tooltip. The composer input starts at one line and grows with typed, pasted or restored text up to six lines, then scrolls (`field-sizing: content`). A conversation resize observer keeps the latest chat visible when already at the bottom, while preserving the scroll position when reading history (`ui/app.js`, verified 2026-10-20). Attachment, permissions, dictation, Options and Send/Stop controls share a centered control height (verified 2026-10-20 against `ui/index.html` and `ui/style.css`). The composer attachment button uses a centered SVG plus to avoid font-baseline drift (`ui/index.html`, `ui/style.css`, verified 2026-10-20). On Windows, the native title strip is hidden and native window controls overlay the header. Header padding reserves the overlay safe area; the header and sidebar toolbar support dragging while buttons remain clickable. Control colors follow Light/Dark/System appearance (`src/main.cjs`, `ui/style.css`, verified 2026-10-20). Settings lives at the bottom of the sidebar as an icon-only button with a 20% smaller gear; its accessible name and tooltip remain Settings. Its gear uses an inline SVG in a flex container to stay vertically centered beside the label without Unicode font-baseline drift (`ui/index.html`, `ui/style.css`, verified 2026-10-20). Advanced model, effort and worker-routing choices are available in the composer's Options disclosure. General settings omit the connected-provider count; connection details live in Providers (`ui/index.html`, `ui/app.js`). The Providers tab groups Jev with the subscription rows: its plug toggles Jev on or off while its name/arrow expands inline to reveal API-key Save, Test and Remove actions. It starts collapsed on each Settings open, preserves unsaved key input during provider refreshes, supports keyboard activation and shows an amber status during service failures; routing and tool choices remain in General. Added API providers join the same list as collapsible plug rows; their plug toggles enabled state while the URL, optional key and confirmed removal live in a stacked panel. Add provider is a separate form, and refreshing models preserves open rows and unsaved key drafts (`ui/app.js`, `ui/index.html`, `ui/style.css`). Jev’s persisted `jevEnabled` switch retains the encrypted key and feature preferences. Hub and all worker Jev clients check the shared switch before reading a key or making a request; disabled routing uses Smart, ranking/tools use local fallbacks, and comparisons are skipped. Re-enabling restores those preferences (`src/main.cjs`, `src/providers/jev.cjs`, `src/controller.cjs`, `tests/agents.test.cjs`). Its About tab centers the icon above the Phasma Harness name, short description, technology credits and copyright notice. The runtime version and update controls/status sit below, followed by a separate Diagnostics section for Copy diagnostics and Open logs folder (`ui/index.html`, `ui/app.js`, `ui/style.css`). Agent switching restores the bound conversation and drafts; deleting the selected agent returns to Hub without overwriting its draft. Settings controls save only the changed field immediately through a serialized write queue, with workspace/access scoped to the selected agent. Rejected changes restore the saved value and report an error. Checks save on field changes and removal; incomplete or failed check drafts survive tab switches and block Close, X and Escape until corrected or removed. The footer is Close; explicit provider/key actions keep their own save behavior (`ui/app.js`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`). Verified 2026-10-10. Cards show role, workspace, model and conversation access and current task, distinguishing startup, ready, working, waiting, input requests and connection failures. Open task navigates to that agent's active conversation; card Stop passes that agent's task ID even when another agent is selected. Controls use text nodes for profile content and preserve keyboard focus across status updates.

Composer chrome (verified 2026-10-18): the chat ends at the composer. Persistent permission explanations, keyboard hints, Commands/Clear buttons, token usage, and context meter are removed from below it. Clear, Commands, context, usage and the permission description remain inside the Options disclosure. It closes on an outside click or Escape, while inside controls keep it open. Escape restores focus to the Options trigger and dismisses the panel before the task-stop shortcut; an open modal keeps its own Escape handling. Dismissal preserves the draft and active work. Connection progress appears in the header only while needed. The placeholder names the current bot without command instructions. Sources: `ui/index.html`, `ui/app.js`, `ui/style.css`; regression: `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` (pool `agents-ui`).

Asynchronous mailbox (verified 2026-10-18): `agent_message` sends notes, questions and correlated answers without creating or transferring an assignment. Callers supply a stable message ID; the receiver saves an `agentMail` item and its queued turn together before acknowledging Received, and retries deduplicate against saved receipts. Reusing an ID for different content fails. Optional job IDs must belong to the sender conversation; answers retain the received question’s job identity. Messages cannot reach a broader-access recipient, and crossing workspaces requires Full access. Mailboxes share the ten-message conversation queue bound, with ten pending messages across the app and ten coordination sends per user request. Direct user follow-ups take priority over internal traffic, preserving order within each stream. A bot waiting on a helper can process a question and answer through the same mailbox while its assignment stays open; a currently active model turn must yield first. Stopped/uncertain queues stay paused. Failed saves roll back unsaved receipts. Hub handles mail separately from completion reports and uses the correlated original request even after later chat. The UI collapses coordination text, names the sender, and labels saved messages Received without implying Applied. Queued and active incoming assignments show their result owner and an Open owner shortcut; each assignment retains its own Stop target. Native conversation resume retains trusted local reports/mail and does not accept provider-forged local items. Sources: `src/agents.cjs`, `src/tools/agent-tools.cjs`, `src/controller.cjs`, `src/controller/coordinator.cjs`, `src/conversation-export.cjs`, `ui/app.js`; regressions: `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`, `tests/conversation-export.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`.

Background work in chat (verified 2026-10-18): a compact activity disclosure appears only for the selected conversation's pending assignments or returned reports awaiting review. It names the result owner and offers Open chat and direct, targeted Stop without a model call. Unchanged output snapshots preserve the disclosure and control focus; a new delivery identity resets stale controls. Stop immediately displays Stopping while actual cleanup remains a backend responsibility. Saved report cards distinguish Result received, Failed, Cancelled and Interrupted; receiving a report does not certify task completion or checks. Report/handoff text is escaped and expanded on demand, and normal user corrections retain their Queued status. Internal result queues no longer fill the composer with protocol text. Sources: `ui/app.js`, `ui/index.html`, `ui/style.css`, `src/agents.cjs`, `src/controller.cjs`. The isolated Electron test streams two fake workers and records 20 samples each for input, navigation, persisted queue receipt, event display and local Stop feedback in `.scratch/async-chat-latency.json`; screenshots are in `.scratch/async-chat.png`. These are offscreen fixture measurements, not real model or process-cleanup latency. Pool: `agents-ui`, `local-agents`, `command-data`.

Appearance and sidebar hierarchy (verified 2026-10-18): Hub has a dedicated neutral tile above the Other bots list, with a larger initial and “Your main chat” caption. It stays visually separate while a specialist is selected. Specialist rows show a bounded task/latest-reply preview; their status remains in the accessible label, title and activity indicator. Settings → General → Appearance offers System (default), Dark and Light. `ui/appearance.js` reads a validated local preference before styles paint, changes only the root appearance attribute and local storage, and keeps working if storage is unavailable. `ui/style.css` resolves shared colors through native `light-dark()` and `color-scheme`; System follows live OS changes while explicit choices stay fixed. Switching preserves drafts and makes no provider/routing call. Sources: `ui/appearance.js`, `ui/index.html`, `ui/style.css`, `ui/app.js`. Regression coverage: `tests/appearance.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`, pool entries `appearance` and `agents-ui`; Light/Dark captures are `.scratch/app-light.png` and `.scratch/app-dark.png`.

Resizable layout: drag the divider beside the agents sidebar, or focus it and use Left/Right (Shift for larger steps), Home/End, and Enter to reset. Double-click also resets; Escape cancels an active drag. The width is saved locally across app launches, bounded to 200–520 pixels while leaving 480 pixels for the conversation. Shrinking the window temporarily clamps the sidebar without overwriting the preferred width. In-app dialogs resize from the bottom-right corner or with Alt+arrow keys while focused; minimum sizes and viewport limits keep contents reachable. Settings retains its scrolling body and footer, and Agents cards/editor fields reflow with dialog width. Dialog dimensions remain for the current app run only. Sources: `ui/app.js`, `ui/dialogs.js`, `ui/index.html`, `ui/style.css`; drag, keyboard, reload and narrow-window regressions are in `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` (pool `agents-ui`). Verified 2026-10-10.

Clicking unused composer padding focuses the prompt on pointer-down and prevents the default focus change; interactive controls retain their own behavior. Native mouse clicks on all four padding edges are covered by `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` (verified 2026-10-20).

Desktop interface scaling uses native Electron page zoom: Ctrl+plus (including Ctrl+= and keypad +) / Ctrl+minus adjust all text, icons, controls and spacing in 5 percentage-point steps, clamped to 50–300%. New profiles start at 115%; `zoom.json` in user data persists the choice across reloads and restarts. Font Size is removed from Settings and old `fontScale` preferences no longer apply a second text multiplier. Browser views use their browser zoom. Composer icons share a 15px source size except the plus, reduced 25% from its original size. The other composer buttons are one-sixth smaller than the plus hit area; the shield sits beside plus without an extra gap and its icon shifts 4px toward plus, and the right icons have equal visible spacing; the shield indicates focus with a heavier SVG stroke and never a surrounding outline (`src/window-zoom.cjs`, `src/main.cjs`, `ui/app.js`, `ui/style.css`, verified 2026-10-20).

Right-clicking an agent entry or card (or Shift+F10/Menu key) populates the styled `#agent-menu` popover from the existing card actions in `ui/app.js`. Labels are Open, Edit and Delete, with Stop added for active tasks; Delete is red in the menu and cards. Sidebar entries have no separate delete button; use the context menu. The menu stays within the viewport, supports arrow/Home/End navigation, Enter/Space, Escape with focus restoration, Tab and outside-click dismissal. It opens inside the active dialog when used from Overview. Opening the menu does not switch conversations; choosing an action rechecks the current card's disabled state and invokes its existing handler. Roster changes dismiss stale menus. Hub never receives Edit/Delete menu entries; backend mutation guards still apply. The old native-menu IPC is removed.

The delete dialog clears DOM text selection on open and close. All buttons, nested button content and semantic button controls use `user-select: none`; conversation text, explanations and inputs remain copyable (`ui/app.js`, `ui/style.css`). The Electron smoke covers reopening the delete dialog after selecting text, unselectable button labels throughout the rendered UI, and retained composer selection.

All app confirmations use the shared `ui/dialogs.js` modal with `ui/style.css`: clear, provider install and provider removal. `src/confirmations.cjs` sends prompts through the existing trusted renderer IPC in `src/main.cjs` and `src/preload.cjs`; replies use one-shot ids and strict booleans. Renderer navigation/crash or window destruction cancels pending prompts; hiding the window preserves them. The renderer queues dialogs, renders dynamic content as text, focuses the dialog without selecting a button, blocks Enter activation on buttons and form inputs, supports Escape, restores focus and uses visible cursor styles. Clear keeps its existing backend revalidation around the confirmation await. Provider installation rechecks agent activity after confirmation. Startup errors use the same dialog in a sandboxed error page; only file/folder pickers remain native.

Validation:

- `node --test tests/agents.test.cjs`: immediate profile saving during held startup, shared discovery, startup failure/shutdown, one-chat migration, guarded deletion, repeated delegation, Hub as a target, exact pasted text through all provider bridges, nested results and cancellation, cycle rejection, management through every provider bridge, Hub protection, access ceilings, late-delete rejection, access inheritance, Auto creation defaults, model/effort discovery and selection across provider bridges with persistence and actual turn parameters, invalid/unavailable model rejection, confirmed clear with archive/restart recovery and failed-save rollback, independent same/nested-workspace concurrency at Full access, per-agent queues and Stop, delegation, permissions, recovery and cleanup.
- `npm test`: existing controller/provider regressions, with no paid inference.
- `node --test tests/confirmations.test.cjs`: matched replies, strict acceptance, and cancellation on window loss or reload.
- `npm run smoke`: hidden Electron UI checks, shared clear/close/startup dialogs with cancel focus, Escape, safe text rendering and visible cursor styles, including agent creation, switching, Stop while waiting for child tasks and absence of Delegate controls, slash-command suggestions, case-insensitive filtering, keyboard/mouse selection, responsive popup bounds and dispatch without model/routing calls, and confirmed clear/cancel for the selected agent; checks pinned Hub and scrolling at 840×640 with 150% text, and writes `.scratch/agents-ui.png`, `.scratch/agents-sidebar.png`, `.scratch/confirmation-clear.png` and `.scratch/confirmation-close.png` and `.scratch/slash-menu.png` for visual inspection.

The Electron UI smoke uses offscreen software rendering (`app.disableHardwareAcceleration()`) with background throttling disabled. This avoids GPU readback failures (`UnknownVizError`) on headless Linux/Xvfb runners and stale modal captures from hidden ordinary Windows windows. The screenshot directory is created before the first capture, including the About tab, so clean checkouts work. This setup applies only to the test; the app retains its normal rendering configuration (`tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`). Startup and UI checks each have a bounded 30-second budget (the timer is refreshed after `loadFile`); the test-pool process timeout is 75 seconds. Cold Windows CI used about 10 seconds before its first check, which previously exhausted the shared 15-second limit. Timeout messages now identify the active phase. Verified on Windows and Ubuntu/Xvfb, 2026-10-09.

No offline inference or generic browser/desktop runtime is installed by this feature. Existing provider-native tools remain responsible for tool execution and permissions.

Windows taskbar icon cache: an installed executable can contain the new icon while an existing pinned shortcut still displays the previous image. On 2026-10-10, v0.1.10 had matching `ui/icon.ico` and embedded 16–256 px Phasma frames; the pinned shortcut still referenced the unchanged executable path. Pointing the shortcut icon at the installed `resources/app/ui/icon.ico` and issuing `SHChangeNotify(SHCNE_UPDATEITEM, SHCNF_PATHW, ...)` refreshes that shortcut without restarting Explorer. See [Microsoft’s shortcut refresh guidance](https://devblogs.microsoft.com/oldnewthing/20150903-00/?p=91671). `src/main.cjs` supplies the window icon; `package.json` supplies the executable/installer icon.


Background capacity (verified 2026-10-18): delegated execution and automatic worker replies share two runner-owned slots. FIFO capacity waits start before provider submission; an idle parent waiting for a helper releases its slot, while an active turn, loading or unconfirmed cleanup retains ownership. Stop and Quit cancel waits without starting a provider. Direct foreground chat and Hub coordination do not compete for these slots. Automatic public-chat replies wait for a free slot before occupying the chat, allowing a direct question to start meanwhile. The sidebar and activity row show capacity waits as Queued. Sources: `src/agents.cjs` (`acquire`, `updateCapacity`, `canStartReply`, `pump`), `src/controller.cjs` (`readyToStart`, `settle`), `ui/app.js`; regressions: `tests/agents.test.cjs`, the four-reviewer batch in `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`, pool `local-agents`.

Ownership delivery isolation (verified 2026-10-18): a transferred result does not become a dependency of the receiving bot's unrelated delegated assignment. Internal replies carry `agentAssignmentId`; handoff notices and transferred reports wait until that assignment finishes, while its own helper replies can continue it. Manual queued-send cannot bypass this boundary. The receiver still saves the handoff/report immediately, and direct user messages retain priority. Unknown saved permission levels reject ownership transfer before acceptance. Sources: `src/agents.cjs` (`pendingDelegations`, `deliver`, `finishOwnerHandoff`), `src/controller.cjs` (`canReceiveQueued`, `settle`, `queuedMessage`); regressions: `tests/agents.test.cjs`, pool `local-agents`.

Deferred review delivery (verified 2026-10-18): a full owner queue retains the review message as `pendingReply` on its local result receipt. `Agents.flushReplies` moves eligible saved messages into free queue capacity atomically; a failed save rolls back the move. User messages keep priority. Foreign ownership notices/results stay on their receipts until the recipient's current delegated assignment finishes, so they cannot fill the inbox needed by its own helper reports. A deferred helper report still prevents premature parent completion. Restart pauses deferred review; no model turn is replayed. The activity row counts both queued and deferred reports/mail. Missing owner conversations leave the worker report in `delivery-pending` / Needs review instead of acknowledging it as delivered. Sources: `src/agents.cjs`, `src/controller.cjs`, `ui/app.js`; regressions: `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`, pools `local-agents` and `agents-ui`.

Assignment revisions (verified 2026-10-18): `agent_revise` accepts an active owner's update using a stable amendment id and expected content revision, separately from `ownerVersion`. The worker store saves the update, revision and local Received receipt together before acknowledging it. Retries cannot change content or overwrite a newer revision. Queued tasks include the updates when they start. Safe Codex steering waits for acknowledgment; other execution, checks or helper waits stop the original subtree before replacement. A cleanup or uncertain steering response leaves the change Received and prevents a second writer; a failed steering acknowledgment also requests Stop. Applied requires provider acceptance (Codex acknowledgment or CLI output/completion), not dispatch or a fabricated timer. Earlier evidence stays in the worker chat; revised terminal reports contain the new execution only and retain update status. Earlier-revision child reports are recorded without automatic continuation. Existing approvals are stamped with the assignment revision; stale approvals and helper actions fail closed. The Hub acknowledges a saved change without an extra synthesis round. Direct queued Apply now uses the same path, consumes the queued message in the revision save, retains images and rejects stale revisions. UI, context reads and exports preserve Received/Applied receipts. Sources: `src/agents.cjs` (`revise`, `applyRevision`, `resumeRevision`, `executionAccepted`), `src/controller.cjs`, `src/controller/turns.cjs`, `src/controller/helpers.cjs`, `src/controller/coordinator.cjs`, `src/conversation-export.cjs`, `ui/app.js`; regressions: `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`, `tests/conversation-export.test.cjs`, UI smoke, pools `local-agents`, `coordinator`, `command-data`, `agents-ui`. Live native sign-ins, actual provider trials and reconciliation of uncertain execution remain unverified or unfinished.


Native cross-provider coordination (verified 2026-10-18): the native-only integration fixture uses the real DeepSeek Harness ACP and Muse/Antigravity adapters with simulated protocol peers (migrated from Gemini on 2026-10-19). A DeepSeek owner creates and delegates to a worker execution context through actual local MCP stdio helper processes; the worker asks for a format while active, DeepSeek answers, Muse consumes its mailbox, and the owner receives one final result. The fixture checks retry suppression, inherited access, foreign-context rejection, hidden private thoughts and released background slots. It uses no installed CLI, real model, credential or paid inference, and does not verify an actual generated artifact. Sources: tests/native-coordination.test.cjs, shared protocol peers in tests/fixtures/native-cli.cjs; pool native-cli.

Native CLI safety fixes (verified 2026-10-19, fixtures only): Gemini CLI 0.49.0 sends no `rawInput` in permission requests, so every call of one kind shared a session-allow key and "Allow for this session" on `npm test` also approved later `rm -rf`. `src/providers/gemini.cjs` now sends no tool name without concrete input, so such calls are approved once at a time. `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` is no longer set on the native `muse`, `agy` or `gemini` executables (only Gemini's `process.execPath` script fallback keeps it), so Electron apps started by an agent run normally. Muse stdin write errors now fail the turn ("Muse Code disconnected") instead of crashing the runner. Regressions: `tests/gemini.test.cjs`, `tests/muse.test.cjs`, `tests/antigravity.test.cjs`, pool `native-cli`.

## Built-in Phasma game checks

Built 2026-10-20 for the game-verification gate. `src/phasma-game-checks.cjs` is both the recipe and the checker it runs.

- **Offered when:** on Windows, the workspace has `runtime/PhasmaPlayer.exe` and `runtime/minilua.exe`, and `runtime/phasma_settings.json` points that Player at this workspace's `phasma_project.json` (`project_manifest`, or `project_path` plus the default manifest). Resolution follows the engine's `ProjectSelection.cpp`: paths are relative to `runtime/`, and the manifest's `assets` field is relative to the project. `assetsRoot()` returns null when the Player would open some other project. This is the layout Harness game batches use (`.scratch/phasma-acceptance-prep.cjs`). The game itself need not exist yet, since workers write it. PhasmaProjects games that run the engine's shared Player (`run.ps1`) are not covered.
- **Offered how:** Settings → Checks shows "Add Phasma game checks" for such a workspace (IPC `phasmaGameChecks`, host only, never on browser access). It adds three ordinary checks through the normal save path, never automatically, and is not offered again once they are saved.
- **Commands:** `[process.execPath, <src/phasma-game-checks.cjs>, build|launch|gameplay]`, with timeouts of 30/90/120 s. These are absolute paths: moving the install or checkout breaks them loudly ("was not found"). How `task-gate.cjs` runs a check whose `argv[0]` is Harness's own executable:
  - It always runs it locally (`runCheck`), because Codex `command/exec` takes no environment. Under workspace-write access, that means the honest "without the Codex sandbox" approval prompt.
  - It sets `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`, so a packaged app never starts a second instance. That second instance would quit with exit 0 and falsely pass the check.
  - It passes `HARNESS_CHECK_TIMEOUT_MS`. The checker stops 15 s before that timeout, so it can stop the Player and clean up, even if the user edits the timeout.
- **Build:** compiles every `<assets>/Scripts/**/*.lua` with `runtime/minilua.exe`. The file list goes through a list file, not the command line. Shortcut: minilua is plain Lua 5.1, so LuaJIT-only syntax (`goto`, labels) fails falsely. Gamekit has none (checked 2026-10-20).
- **Launch:** starts the Player and waits for live frames over its MCP server. `game_describe` must report protocol 1 and a game id. When `<assets>/game.json` exists (batch games write one), its `gameId` must match.
- **Gameplay:** generic gamekit play (`verifyGameplay`, `driver.lua` semantics):
  - the game starts unfinished with legal actions, from a reset or, when `reset_supported` is false, from an observe;
  - up to 50 first-legal-action moves, each in the same session with a higher `state_id`, waiting out `busy`;
  - a reset (when supported) starts a new session;
  - an action against the first state is rejected `stale_state` and changes neither the session nor the game. For `state_tracking: game_epoch` games, a stable `state_id` counts as unchanged, since their view keeps moving. Other games must keep the same observation.
  - a PNG screenshot is taken.
  Game rules (solving a board) belong to the game's own tests.
- **MCP opt-in:** the Player starts MCP (which runs any Lua) only from `<assets>/Agent/agent_config.json` (`PlayerHost.cpp` `ReadPlayerMcpPort`; no CLI or environment switch, checked 2026-10-20). The checker backs that file up to `runtime/harness-checks/agent_config.backup.json`, opts in on a free port, and restores it afterwards. Harness also calls `restoreMcpOptIn` after every recipe check whose process tree is confirmed gone, so a Stop or timeout that hard-kills the checker still restores it. A Harness crash mid-check leaves the opt-in until the next recipe run, which restores it first. Shortcut: two recipe checks running at once on the same workspace can race on the backup; batches give each job its own copy.
- **Evidence:** one JSON line on stdout, kept by the check result. Scratch files and `gameplay.png` stay in `runtime/harness-checks/`, out of the game's source.

Verified 2026-10-20:
- **Unit tests:** `tests/phasma-game-checks.test.cjs` covers:
  - detection, including a Player pointed elsewhere and a manifest with another assets folder;
  - opt-in backup/restore, with and without an existing config;
  - a fake gamekit game: busy, real-time (`game_epoch`), no reset, a stale action accepted, and a state that does not advance.
- **Runner test:** `tests/providers-optional.test.cjs` "own runtime" checks the env, local routing with Codex connected, and Harness's restore after a failed recipe check.
- **UI smoke:** a "Phasma game checks" phase. Full suite: 748 pass, 0 fail.
- **Live, free:** all three modes passed on the six batch games from two runs (`.scratch/recipe-live.cjs`). No Player was left, `agent_config.json` was byte-identical afterwards, and the Player logs had no warnings or errors.
  - The gameplay check also passed under `electron.exe` with `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1`.
  - `.scratch/recipe-restore-live.cjs` covered running out of time ("did not render frames in time", Player stopped, file restored) and a hard kill (opt-in and backup left behind, then restored by the next run).
- **Live batch with recipe checks:** `.scratch/phasma-game-acceptance-recipe.cjs`, four Codex `gpt-6.1-sol:low` workers, a fresh seed each time, engine Player `a421ec3d`.
  - **Final code (`%TEMP%\phasma-harness-games-YSh06v`) passed:**
    - Every build, launch and gameplay check came from the recipe and ran through the local runner, in 2.3–4.5 s each.
    - The morning summary read "3 returned, checks passed · 1 cancelled".
    - The title correction applied, the side question was answered ("391"), and the cancelled witness's process was gone.
    - At most two background slots were in use, cleanup was confirmed, and the source was unchanged.
    - Every job folder ended with no opt-in and no backup, and the Player logs had no warnings or errors.
    - The script's game-specific rules (solved boards, the corrected title) passed on all three games after the batch.
    - An earlier pass (`MtbH4k`) used the pre-review recipe.
  - **Unexplained failure (`gIeZex`):** one run failed at dispatch with "Sent 0/4 agent tasks. Model unavailable" (`agent_create` → `primary.available`). The start-of-run availability assert had passed. Hub calls use ephemeral or kept router threads, so a missing rollout does not mean no Hub turn. The cause is still unverified; see the Hub coordination section for the logging and the one retry added afterwards.
- **Not verified:** workspace-write access, which asks "without the Codex sandbox" approval for each recipe check; a packaged install; and a project whose `agent_config.json` already existed, live (unit-tested only).

## Setup fingerprint

Built 2026-10-20 (harness2 item 1 in the plan). `src/setup-fingerprint.cjs`: what shaped a worker run, as readable parts plus `hash` (16 hex of SHA-256 over canonical JSON with sorted keys). It makes no model calls and starts no processes.
- **Parts (`v: 1`):**
  - `harness` version;
  - `provider`, `providerVersion` (cached only: Codex `client.version`, native CLI `status.version`; null for Claude, Cursor and API providers), `model`, `effort`, `access`;
  - `instructions` (separate hashes of the Harness defaults, the project's `PhasmaHarness.md` content and the bot's own instructions);
  - `lessons` (the lesson block, only on the task's first uncorrected run, matching `withTaskLessons`);
  - `skills` (each `skills/<name>/` folder's files by content, cached by size and modification time);
  - `tools` (helpers, context search, Jev tool selection, large responses);
  - `checks` (the task's frozen check commands, with workspace paths made relative).
- **Left out on purpose:** per-job context (batch copy paths, resume notes, the project file's path), so identical setups match across copies. A resumed API chat keeps the system message it started with, so its instruction hashes are the ones saved then (`session.apiSetupInstructions`), or null for older chats. Codex reloads its thread whenever the worker instructions change, so the current ones are what it runs with.
- **Saved on:**
  - each worker route (`Controller.runSetup`, in the Codex `turn/start` path and `sendCLI`);
  - the task attempt of that turn (`runGate`);
  - each delivered `agentResult` item: the last route with a setup, so after a failover it is the provider that finished.
- **Not covered:** Hub turns. Records from before this change have no `setup`; treat that as unknown. There is no UI yet; the data is in the stores.
- **Tests:** `tests/setup-fingerprint.test.cjs` (key order, a skill-file edit, checks in two copies, and a project-path check remapped by the real `Agents.copyChecks` into two copies giving one hash), the "saves its setup fingerprint" test in `tests/core.test.cjs` (route and attempt), and the four-reviewer batch test in `tests/coordinator.test.cjs` (four copies, one hash).

## Release review since v0.2.0

Gate 24 code review (2026-10-20): four read-only reviewers covered every commit from `8c38c59` to `a3fcbb5`. The reply-suggestion and game-check commits had their own review earlier.
- **Tested both ways:** each fix's new test was run against an archived copy of `HEAD` (`ee94d93`/`a3fcbb5`), where it fails, and it passes with the fix. This covers the Claude, Cursor, DeepSeek, API-loop, Hub-admission, updater, update-gate and settings-tools fixes, plus the two dispatch tests in Hub coordination.
- **Not tested both ways:**
  - the approval-focus smoke check was not run against `HEAD`;
  - the updater's agent message wording and the closing-path `changed()` in settings tools have no test;
  - the Muse fix has no test.
- **Guard only:** "a restart keeps a draft …" (`tests/runner.test.cjs`) passes on `HEAD` too. It pins the restart behavior that the update-gate exemption relies on.
- **Whose code:** the updater, update-gate (`src/runner.cjs`) and settings-tools (`src/settings-tools.cjs`) fixes change Codex's code, as do the approval dialog and Claude live input from the v0.2.1 work. The DeepSeek, Cursor, API-loop, Muse and Hub-admission fixes change Claude's Send now and restart commits (`ca06226`, `faadf3c`).
- **Approvals:** a request that opens on its own focuses Decline (`ui/app.js` `renderRequest`). Opening one with Review keeps Enter-to-allow. Smoke: "Approvals that open on their own focus Decline".
- **Claude live input:** a follow-up that is never echoed, or whose write fails, leaves `deliveries` after `DELIVERY_MS` (30 s) of Claude being idle (a running step does not count, see "A foreground wait hides follow-ups" below), so a finished turn closes stdin instead of hanging with a live process. Send now on a Claude turn without live input (it is finishing) leaves the message queued instead of restarting and dropping the saved Claude session.
- **Claude background tasks hold the turn (fixed 2026-10-28):**
  - **Symptom.** The user reported "stuck here and send now is not working". The PhasmaEngine bot had answered at 18:56, but its chat stayed "running" for an hour with "status" queued.
  - **Cause.** The bot's own background command (`while (Get-Process dota2) { Start-Sleep 30 }`, `run_in_background`) kept the CLI alive. Harness had closed Claude's input at the `result`, so `liveInput` was null and Send now took the Claude "finishing" path (the message stayed queued).
  - **What the real CLI does** (2.1.309, Haiku probe; the same for Bash `run_in_background` and Monitor):
    - after `result` it keeps running while any task is live;
    - it emits `system`/`background_tasks_changed` with the full live set (REPLACE semantics, an `ambient` flag for the CLI's own watchers);
    - when the task ends it sends `task_notification`, runs one more turn, emits a second `result`, then exits, even with stdin already closed.
  - **Fix.** `ClaudeCLI.run` (`src/providers/claude.cjs`, `closeIfIdle`) keeps input open after a result while any non-ambient task is live, so Send now and queued messages reach Claude. It closes input once Claude is idle (the last event was a result, not an echoed follow-up), no delivery is pending and the set is empty.
  - **Limit.** The chat still shows "running" and holds the bot's busy slot until the task ends. The working line then reads "… is waiting on N background tasks" (below).
  - **Wake-up turns (fixed 2026-10-28, after v0.3.18).**
    - **Bug.** v0.3.18 closed input the moment the background set emptied while Claude was idle. A finished task always wakes Claude for a turn of its own (`task_notification`, then `background_tasks_changed []`, then `system`/`init`), and that turn can start more tasks.
    - **Live case.** The Dota watcher ended at 22:30:52, input closed, and the wake-up turn then started a render and a progress Monitor. "still going?" stayed queued and Send now hit the silent "Claude is finishing" path for the rest of the turn.
    - **Fix (`ClaudeCLI.run`).**
      - `assistant` or `system`/`init` marks Claude busy again.
      - Once a run has had any task (`tasksSeen`), input closes only if no turn starts within `WAKE_MS` (5 s) of Claude idling with no task left (`settleInput`). That holds both when the set empties and at a result, because a task that ended mid-turn can wake Claude after its answer.
      - A run that never had a task still closes at its result at once.
    - **Verified.**
      - Tests in `tests/claude-permissions.test.cjs`:
        - "a finished task's wake-up turn can start more tasks without losing Claude's input" fails on `7b1a516`.
        - "a task that ends mid-turn wakes Claude after its answer with input still open" fails on the first version of this fix.
        - "a turn that never starts a task closes Claude's input at its answer, without waiting".
      - Real CLI, task ending while Claude is idle (Haiku, `sleep 8`, then a wake-up turn starting `sleep 40`): input stayed live at 13.7 s, a follow-up was answered at 17.7 s, and input closed after "all done".
      - Real CLI, task ending mid-turn (background `sleep 3` during a foreground `sleep 8`): the CLI folded the wake-up into the same turn, which started `sleep 40`. A follow-up was answered at 21.9 s, and input closed 5 s after "all done".
      - **Every send path in the real app, 36/36.** `.scratch/send-e2e.cjs` (git-ignored) runs a separate Harness with the real window on a sanitized copy, using real Claude (Haiku), Codex (`gpt-5.6-terra:low`) and Cursor (Grok) turns.
        - **Claude:** composer Send while idle, while waiting on a task, and after a wake-up turn started new work (answered in 2 s with the task running); Send now on a queued message on another model; Edit; Remove; Stop on a task (session kept), then Send; Stop on the turn with a message queued, then Send now.
        - **Codex:** queue while busy; Send now steering into the turn; the other queued message sends itself after the turn.
        - **Cursor:** Send; Send now replacing the running step.
    - A stopped task (`stop_task`) gets no wake-up turn in its own run, so that run ends 5 s after the set empties. Its notification waits in the session and arrives at the next resume (below).
  - **Resumed sessions (fixed 2026-10-29).**
    - **Bug.** A resumed session first answers what it was left owing, such as a stopped task's `task_notification`, in a turn of its own with its own `result`, before it takes the prompt. Harness took that result as the answer and closed input, so the prompt's tool permission request failed with "Tool permission request failed: AbortError: Stream closed" (twice, then Claude gave up).
    - **Found by** the live Browser-access test (step 6), then reproduced on the real CLI (`resume_probe`: run 1 stops a task, run 2 resumes).
    - The old close-at-result code had the same flaw.
    - **Fix.** The prompt carries a `uuid`, and Claude echoes it back when it takes it (`--replay-user-messages`). Until then (`promptPending`), a result only starts the `WAKE_MS` wait, and the prompt's own result then closes at once when no task ran.
    - **Verified.**
      - Real CLI: the leftover turn's result at 21.3 s kept input live, the prompt's permission request was answered at 22.9 s, the command ran, and input closed after the tasks ended.
      - Test "a resumed session's leftover turn does not close input before Claude takes the prompt" fails without the tracking.
  - **Live test of the running Harness (2026-10-29).**
    - `.scratch/live-pair.cjs` pairs once: the user pastes the connection link from Settings → Browser access → "Copy connection link", and only the session token is saved, git-ignored.
    - `.scratch/live-test.cjs` drives the running app through `/api/call` on 127.0.0.1, addressed with the configured origin's host (`.scratch/live-api.cjs`), on a separate "Live test" bot that is deleted afterwards.
    - Pitfalls:
      - `load` answers with the whole fleet's state, so read `agentStates[botId]`.
      - A bot's `create` reuses its conversation.
      - Count processes by name: a command-line match also finds the shell that runs the query.
    - Run on the 00:01 build: 14/16. Both failures were the resumed-session bug above. The live whole-turn Stop with `sleep 91 & sleep 92` left no `sleep` (pids 2936 and 61704 gone).
    - **After that fix (Harness restarted 01:03): 16/16.**
      - A message after a wake-up turn answered in 2 s.
      - Send now answered in 3 s.
      - Task Stop left 0 loop processes.
      - The resumed chat ran `sleep 91 & sleep 92` (2 running), and whole-turn Stop left 0.
      - Send after Stop answered in 7 s.
  - **Verified.**
    - Fake-process tests "Claude takes follow-ups while a background task runs…" and "a background task that ends while Claude answers…" in `tests/claude-permissions.test.cjs` both fail on the old close-at-result logic.
    - Real CLI through `ClaudeCLI.run` (Haiku, background `sleep 25`): a follow-up sent after the first result was answered at 20 s, input closed when the set emptied at 42 s, and the run resolved after the "bg done" wake-up turn at 44 s.
- **A foreground wait hides follow-ups from Claude (fixed 2026-10-29):**
  - **Symptom.** "status?" to the PhasmaEngine bot vanished for 30 s, came back as "Paused: status?", and Send now stayed greyed out. Claude answered it 5 minutes later.
  - **Cause.**
    - The bot started its regression run with `run_in_background`, then waited for it with foreground Bash loops (`for i in $(seq 1 115); … sleep 5`, 10-minute timeout). Its turn never ended, so the background-task path above never applied, and "running" was accurate.
    - Claude takes follow-ups only between steps. `DELIVERY_MS` ran from the write, so the message turned `uncertain` after 30 s, which disables Send now and Edit.
    - Claude's Send now only joined the same queue (`liveQueue` without `liveSteer`).
    - Claude's session log: enqueued 13:57:17Z, taken 14:02:16Z when the loop ended.
  - **What the real CLI does** (2.1.311, Haiku probes: background `sleep 60`, then foreground `for i in $(seq 1 24); do sleep 5; done`):
    - a follow-up written mid-step waits for the step; the CLI itself refuses a standalone `sleep 120`, so bots wait with loops;
    - folded into a running turn between two tool calls, the follow-up is still replayed with its uuid (`command_lifecycle` `queued`, then the replay and `started` as the first step ended, before the turn's `result`), so the delivery confirms either way;
    - `priority: 'now'` on the follow-up changed nothing: it waited out the whole 120 s step;
    - `control_request` `{ subtype: 'interrupt', send_now: true, message_uuid }` (capability `interrupt_send_now_v1` on `system`/`init`) answered `{ still_queued: [uuid], send_now: 'stopped' }`, ended the foreground step with an `error_during_execution` result, echoed the follow-up 1.2 s later and answered it. The background task finished at 65 s and woke Claude as usual;
    - a plain interrupt ends background tasks unless the client declared `perTaskStopAffordance` on `initialize` (SDK 0.3.302 `.d.ts`), so Harness never sends one.
  - **Fix.**
    - `BACKGROUND_NOTE` (`src/providers/claude.cjs`) tells Claude bots to end the turn after starting background work instead of waiting in the foreground.
    - The delivery timeout counts only while Claude is idle (`watchDeliveries`).
    - Claude has `liveSteer` (`src/providers/capabilities.cjs`). Send now writes the message, then sends the send-now interrupt; on a message Claude already holds it sends only the interrupt. A CLI without `interrupt_send_now_v1` keeps the message waiting and says so.
    - Once Claude holds a message, its row reads "Waiting" with Send now enabled and Edit/Remove off, and the rest of the queue is free (`written` in `Controller.queuedMessage`, `ui/app.js`). An app restart marks a waiting message unconfirmed.
  - **Verified.**
    - `tests/claude-permissions.test.cjs` "a follow-up waits out Claude’s running step…" and "Send now writes a new message…", `tests/core.test.cjs` "a message Claude holds shows as waiting…".
    - Real CLI through `ClaudeCLI.run` (2.1.311, Haiku, workspace access, 2026-10-29): background `sleep 60`, then a foreground 120 s tick loop. A follow-up written at 15.8 s was held for 36 s without timing out. Send now at 51.8 s answered `send_now: 'stopped'`, the follow-up was echoed at 53.2 s and answered, the background task finished and woke Claude, and the run resolved at 78 s.
    - In the app (user, 2026-10-29, Opus): a background agent plus a deliberate foreground wait loop; `status?` showed as Waiting, Send now got it answered and the agent kept running. A second run where the bot ended its turn after starting the agent answered `status?` directly.
- **Stop ends everything a worker started (fixed 2026-10-28):**
  - **Symptom.** Stopping a PhasmaEngine Claude turn ended Claude and the running PhasmaEditor, but the background render (`review_base.sh`, its Python driver) and two Monitor `tail`/`grep` pairs kept running and held the `pe-gpu-lock`.
  - **Cause.** Git Bash runs each command through a short-lived fork, so a command's Windows parent pid names a process that already exited. The render script's parent 77136 was gone before Stop, so `stopTree` (`taskkill /T` plus the parent-pid sweep) could never reach it.
    - Measured with a control (no job): an orphaned `sleep` survives the tree kill.
    - Real CLI, background `sleep N & sleep N`, then Stop: 2 left.
    - Claude's own `stop_task` (per-task Stop) also left both.
  - **Fix, whole turn and every worker.** `spawnOwned` puts every real child process into its own job object, through one helper per app run (`src/worker-job.ps1`, using the `document-job.dll` calls, no cmdlets). `stopTree` ends the job (`TerminateJobObject`) before its sweep, and Quit ends all of them (`stopJobs` in `Controller.shutdown`).
    - The jobs have no kill-on-close, so what a normally finished worker leaves running keeps running until Quit, as before.
    - `main.cjs` starts the helper at launch (`startJobs`). A helper started later joins a worker only about 0.3 s after its first children (measured: job at 323 ms, orphan at 129 ms).
    - The idle helper is unref'd so it never keeps the app alive, but its output pipe is ref'd while a reply is pending. v0.3.19's first CI run had 35 Windows tests cancelled ("Promise resolution is still pending but the event loop has already resolved"): their files awaited a reply with nothing else alive. Test: "waiting on the job helper keeps Node alive when nothing else does" (fails in about 0.1 s without the ref, 2026-10-29).
  - **Fix, per-task Stop (Claude).** Every process of a background task holds its output file (`…\tasks\<id>.output`, the folder Claude names in its tool result, `Controller.taskOutputFile`). `stopFileHolders` lists them through Restart Manager (`holders <file>`, pid plus exact start time), runs Claude's `stop_task`, then kills them and their children with the verified sweep. Running workers and Harness itself are never among them.
  - **Verified.**
    - `tests/shutdown.test.cjs`:
      - "Stop ends what a running worker started through a process that already exited" (an orphan whose parent exited).
      - "stopping one task ends every process holding its output file, but never the worker".
    - Real Claude CLI, whole-turn Stop: 2 orphaned `sleep`s left on the old code, 0 with jobs.
    - Real Claude CLI, per-task Stop: 2 left after `stop_task` alone, 0 through `stopFileHolders`.
  - **Not covered.** A Codex turn's Stop still uses `stopStartedSince` (parent pid within the turn's window), because the app-server's job also holds its MCP helpers. Codex on Windows runs commands in PowerShell, which does not fork.
- **Running tasks list (added 2026-10-28):**
  - **What it shows.** Above the message box, each task the bot has running, with an Output toggle and its own Stop:
    - Claude: background commands, Monitors and background subagents.
    - Codex: commands that have run 5 s or longer. Shorter ones would only flash by.
  - **Claude.**
    - The list comes from `background_tasks_changed` (`Controller.trackBackground`, the `background` map, never saved, cleared when the turn ends).
    - Output and Stop are CLI control requests sent over the open input (`control` in `ClaudeCLI.run`, matched by `request_id`): `get_task_output` returns the last 8 KiB and `stop_task` ends one task.
    - Real CLI 2.1.309: both work without an `initialize`. `stop_task` ended a 60 s task at 14 s with no further turn, and the run then resolved.
  - **Codex.**
    - The list is the chat's in-progress `commandExecution` items with a `processId`; output is their `aggregatedOutput`.
    - Stop is `thread/backgroundTerminals/terminate`. Real Codex 0.164: `thread/backgroundTerminals/list` showed the running command, and `terminate` returned `{terminated:true}`. The item ended as `failed` and the turn went on to its answer.
    - Commands Codex detaches itself (`Start-Process`) are tracked by neither Codex nor Harness.
  - **Other providers.** Cursor and DeepSeek run as ACP with `terminal: false`, so their commands stay inside their own agent. Muse, Antigravity and the API loop report no background work.
    - The `tasks` capability (`src/providers/capabilities.cjs`) is set only for Codex and Claude.
    - Removed 2026-10-31 at the user's request: a note "This provider does not report its background tasks…" under every working non-Codex, non-Claude chat. Their running commands already show as activity rows, so it read as a false warning on every turn. With no listed tasks the area stays hidden on every provider (smoke phase "No task area without tasks").
  - **Wiring.** `backgroundTask` IPC (`src/runner-commands.cjs`; Stop refused on a watched sub-bot), `Controller.backgroundTask`, `renderBackgroundTasks` in `ui/app.js`. Output is polled every 2 s only while open.
  - **Tests.**
    - "a Claude turn lists its background tasks…" and "a running Codex command shows its output…" (`tests/core.test.cjs`).
    - "Harness asks the running Claude for a task's output…" (`tests/claude-permissions.test.cjs`).
    - The "running tasks" phase of `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`, which saves `.scratch/running-tasks.png`.
  - **End to end (2026-10-28).** `.scratch/tasks-e2e.cjs` (git-ignored, built from the freeze bench) runs `createRunner`, the real window offscreen and the real IPC on a sanitized copy holding only the PhasmaEngine and Grok bots. It allows only the test's own `tick` command. All steps pass (`E2E_ONLY=claude`: 9/9; full run: 16/17, the one failure explained below).
    - **Claude** (Haiku low, a background 120-tick loop):
      - the row appears with Output and Stop, and the status reads "waiting on 1 background task";
      - Output shows the real ticks and keeps updating;
      - a follow-up sent on the same model went straight to Claude and was answered at 11 s while the loop ran;
      - Stop removed the task at once, and the turn completed at 13 s with the Claude session kept.
    - **Codex** (`gpt-5.6-terra:low`, a 60-tick PowerShell command):
      - the command is not listed for its first seconds, then appears as "Running: …";
      - Output shows ticks 1–5;
      - Stop ended only the command (item `failed`), and the turn went on to answer at 22 s.
    - **Cursor:** the note appears while it works and leaves when the turn ends.
    - **Pitfall.** A queued message on a *different* model from the running turn (`flushLiveQueue` compares `mode` with `liveInputMode`) is not handed to the running Claude. It waits for its own turn, so a long background task holds it until Send now. In the full run the copy's composer sent on the bot's saved Opus xhigh while the turn ran Haiku; Send now delivered it in 2 s.
- **DeepSeek and Cursor Send now:** after cleanup, `steer` refuses to prompt if the run already ended (Stop or failure during cleanup). The message then stays queued instead of counting as delivered on a closing connection.
- **API loop:** a follow-up counts as delivered only after the model call carrying it succeeds. If that call fails it is rejected with "connection closed", so it stays queued as uncertain.
- **Restart:** Hub-run tracked jobs now save `admittedAt` too (`Controller.markAdmitted`, called by `send` and `sendCoordinator`), so a crash cannot replay them.
- **Muse:** a follow-up acknowledged after the run stopped watching is rejected instead of locking the chat's queue. This one has no test: the window is too narrow for the fixture.
- **Updater:**
  - a check that returns during a download keeps the download state;
  - an agent's confirmation request resets when the release version changes;
  - the agent message no longer claims installation waits for work;
  - a draft stopped or crashed before sending no longer blocks updates (`runner.cjs` `stoppedDraft`; restart keeps it).
- **Settings tools:**
  - a confirmation expires after 2 minutes, and an answer after the asking turn ended applies nothing. Known limit: the desktop dialog (`confirmations.cjs`) cannot be closed per request, so after expiry it stays visible and clicking it does nothing. The browser-access confirmation (`remote.cjs` `ask`, also 120 s) behaves the same way;
  - the undocumented `models` action `model` is removed.

Open, not changed:
- **Live forwarding from the queue (fixed 2026-10-28, user report: Send now greyed during a queued Claude turn):** `queuedMessage(…, 'send')` used to await `send()`, which for CLI providers lasts the whole `runtime.run`. So `queueSending` stayed true for the entire turn, disabling Send now, edit and × on the rest of the queue and skipping `flushLiveQueue`. Codex was unaffected, because its `send()` returns after `turn/start`. `send` → `submitWorker` → `sendCLI` now pass a `started` callback, called once the message is in `session.items` and just before `runtime.run`. `queuedMessage` awaits whichever comes first, so the message leaves the queue before the flag clears, and neither `settle`, `flushLiveQueue` nor Send now can send it twice. A failure before the start still returns it to the queue as Paused. `busy` is already set, so `settle` starts nothing until `finishTurn`, which sends the next queued message. Regression: `tests/core.test.cjs` "a queued message that starts a CLI turn frees the queue while the turn runs" (fails with `queueSending` true against the old code).
- **Approval dialog exits:** the "Stop task" button was removed from the approval and question dialog in `8c38c59`, and Esc is blocked there. Whether that was intended is unconfirmed.
- **Hub Send on a fresh profile:** it can be disabled with no reason shown when no Hub model is remembered (`showMode('auto')` maps to `hubMode`).
- **Message accessibility:** every message has `tabIndex=0` with no role, and the speaker label shows only on hover.
- **Sub-bots (2026-10-22, at the user's request):** a bot a bot makes (`agent_create` from a bot's chat or job, not a batch worker: `Agents.isSubBot`) works under it, like a Claude Code or Codex subagent. It inherits its creator's workspace, access and model live (`inheritWorkspace`/`inheritAccess`/`inheritMode`, resolved by `inheritedSource` down chains; a missing creator or loop ends at the Main Bot's folder, Auto, and Ask access so it never grants more), except a model or effort the creator passes when the user asked for one, and except access or folder when the creating chat had less access or another folder (then fixed at the chat's). It lives until deleted; deleting a bot deletes its sub-bots (`subBotsOf`, each must be idle). It cannot be edited (`saveAgent`, `agents.settings`, `agent_set_access` and `agent_update` refuse), only its creator gives it work or messages it (`delegate`, `sendMessage`; its own sub-bots and answers to its questions excepted), and the UI's `send` and queue calls refuse it, so its chat is read only; Stop and approval answers still work. The sidebar lists it under its creator, indented per level and a little smaller (`.agent-nav-sub`), only while that creator or one of its sub-bots is selected (the selected bot and its creators form the open path), and a creator shows how many bots are under it; Overview has no Edit for it; `/model` and `/effort` refuse; Delete names the bots that go with it. Tests: the inheritance chain, broken-chain, and job-context sub-bot tests in `tests/agents.test.cjs`; smoke "Sub-bots sit under their creator, read only and without Edit".
- **Harness owns what its bots load (2026-10-22, at the user's request):** Claude and Codex bots no longer load the user's personal setup; Harness supplies the rules and skills, and login, the project's own instructions and the user's MCP servers stay. Checked on Claude Code 2.1.306 and codex-cli 0.163.0 (2026-10-22).
  - **Rules added to the worker defaults** (`src/worker-instructions.cjs`, test "workers get the request rules"): build what was asked, a question gets an answer, marked-up images, ask when two readings fit, report log warnings and errors, no Co-Authored-By. The defaults must stay under 4,000 characters even from a long install path ("a relocated checkout" test).
    - 2026-10-26: "A message with no request (hi, thanks, ok) gets a short reply; resume unfinished work only when asked."
      - Why: a bare "hi" to DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash, hand-picked in PhasmaProjects with an open pending task, ran 9 PowerShell commands resuming the Jev audit, with recursive `luajit.exe` searches over PhasmaEngine, for over a minute.
      - Budget: the fixed part, measured as the test does (`.scratch/instruction-budget.cjs`), went from 2,949 to 3,052 of its 3,100 limit, so little room is left.
  - **Claude** (`src/providers/claude.cjs` `withoutPersonalSetup`, `personalMcp`; every `run()`, classifier calls included): `--setting-sources project,local` drops user settings, so user hooks, permissions, skills and subagents are gone; `--settings` adds `claudeMdExcludes` for the home `CLAUDE.md` (it otherwise loads as a "Project" file, because Claude walks up parent folders and the home folder is a parent of every repo) and `enabledPlugins` false for every installed plugin (flag settings outrank a project's untracked `.claude/settings.json`), and `autoMemoryEnabled: false`. Leaving out user settings also drops the user MCP servers, so worker turns pass `~/.claude.json` itself to `--mcp-config` (Claude reads its `mcpServers`; nothing is copied). Measured with `/context` and a real Full-access turn in PhasmaEngine: project `CLAUDE.md` kept, home one gone, built-in skills and agents only, no plugin or user hooks, user and project MCP servers present, `bypassPermissions` works headless. Auto-memory (`~/.claude/projects/*/memory/MEMORY.md`, 7.4k tokens in PhasmaEngine) is off too: `/context` then lists only the project `CLAUDE.md`. Still reaching bots on purpose: the user's MCP servers and the claude.ai account connectors that come with the login. Chats resumed from before keep their earlier system prompt until compacted; the user's `permissions.allow` rules no longer apply, so Ask bots may ask more.
  - **Codex** (`src/providers/codex.cjs` `withoutPersonalSetup`, on every app-server start): `features.hooks=false`, `features.plugins=false`, `features.memories=false`, `notify=[]`, `personality="none"` (deprecated in 0.163; pinned so the user's `friendly` is never used), and `skills.config` entries with `enabled=false` for each skill in `~/.agents/skills` and `$CODEX_HOME/skills` (link paths work; Codex's own `.system` skills stay). Uses `-c` rather than `--disable` so older Codex versions ignore unknown keys instead of failing. Verified with `hooks/list`, `skills/list`, `config/read` and `codex debug prompt-input` (no model call): no hooks, personal skills off, skills prompt 22,163 to 1,962 characters, MCP servers kept. Of the user's other `[features]` flags only `memories` differed from Codex's defaults (an inline `features={...}` override merges rather than replaces, so flags are pinned one by one). Limits: Codex has no switch for `~/.codex/AGENTS.md` (`-c instructions=""` does not remove it); hooks can only be turned off all together (no tracked `.codex/hooks.json` was found in `~/repos`); the saved approvals in `~/.codex/rules/default.rules` (52 `allow` prefix rules) still apply, since Codex only has an internal, unexposed flag to ignore user rules (`ignore_user_and_project_exec_policy_rules`) and moving `CODEX_HOME` would split the login. They only matter where Codex would ask (Ask and Workspace bots). Test: "Codex bots leave out the personal setup".
  - **Cursor and Antigravity bots still load the user's global setup (checked 2026-10-22, Cursor CLI 2026.10.19, agy 1.4.2).** Neither CLI has a switch. Cursor builds `~/.cursor/rules`, `skills`, `hooks.json` and `mcp.json` from the home folder directly (its bundle joins `os.homedir()` with `.cursor`), names Claude Code's user hooks as a hooks source too (`claudeUserConfigPath`: `~/.claude/settings.json`; seen in its code, not tested), and `CURSOR_CONFIG_DIR` moves only `cli-config.json`. agy loads `~/.gemini/GEMINI.md` (and `~/.gemini/AGENTS.md`, `~/.gemini/config/` rules) as always-on global rules; on this machine it has no agy skills, hooks or plugins (whether it injects its own memories from `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/brain`, `implicit` or `conversation_summaries.db` is unverified), and the RTK hook in `~/.gemini/settings.json` belongs to Gemini CLI (agy does not read it, per its docs; unverified). Relocating the home or config folder would split the login, hide the MCP servers and change HOME for every command a bot runs, so Harness does not. The lever is trimming those global files.
  - **Test pool in Harness** (`src/controller/task-gate.cjs` `testPoolAfterTurn`/`runTestPool`; `finishTurn` marks a completed turn, `settle` starts it): once a turn has settled, checks included, Harness runs the bundled `skills/test-pool/scripts/testpool.cjs hook --agent claude` in the background (Harness's executable as Node), one run per repository at a time. It keeps the script's lock, change tracking and per-chat retry cap. A failure goes back to the bot as a "Test pool" message (a `routineRun` with id `test-pool`, so it is not the user's turn); a job's or sub-bot's failure, and pass or invalid-catalog notices, show as the chat notice. A report that cannot go to the bot also becomes the notice (bot busy, queue not empty, or the chat moved on). A notice containing "failed" has no expiry, and the UI shows it for 5 s on every visit. So since 2026-10-28, `send()` clears a notice starting with "Test pool" when the chat's next turn starts, and that turn's own pool run reports anything still failing. Before this, a 01:21 UTC report of 11 failures kept reappearing in the PhasmaEngine chat after all 11 had passed at 11:39. Regression: `tests/core.test.cjs` "the next turn clears a test-pool notice but keeps other notices". Only after Full-access turns, where the bot could already run anything: after a Workspace turn the catalog and its scripts may be the bot's own, and Harness would run them outside the sandbox. Gap, as with the old global Stop hooks: what a Workspace bot wrote earlier still runs on the next Full-access turn in that repository. Pooled test steps get neither `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` (Harness's bundled `testpool.cjs` removes it, so Electron apps under test start normally) nor the Jev key. Not for Cursor bots, which still run the global hook, nor the Main Bot. Harness passes its own Jev key as `JEV_API_KEY`, or `TESTPOOL_NO_JEV=1`, so the script never reads the Windows credential store. Tests: `tests/testpool-harness.test.cjs`.
- **Background work shows in the bot's chat, read only (2026-10-22, at the user's request):** a bot doing a job another bot gave it, and a sub-bot always, shows its background context (`executionStates[id]`) in place of its own chat, so you see the task, progress and answer as they happen (`ui/app.js` `backgroundView`, `state.watching`). The composer is read only with a note saying why (`readOnlyNote`), Stop stays, and `selectAgent` does not reload the foreground chat while watching. A job is watched only while it is undelivered and its owner is not one of the bot's own chats, so a direct task resumed from that chat leaves it writable; once the result returns, `applyState` moves Send back to the bot's own chat and loads it (`watchedAgent`), as selecting the bot does. Composer Stop stops the watched work (`api.stop` sends `selectedId`, the background conversation). An approval for the watched work opens in place on Decline (`renderRequest` uses its `contextId`) and can be deferred. Smoke: "Background work shows read only in the bot's chat" (including the return to the own chat and the resumed own task) and "Watched assignment, nonmodal assignment details…"; `sendIsReachable` checks whichever of Send or Stop is showing.
- **Wikis and inherited workspaces (2026-10-22, at the user's request):** every wiki lives in one fixed folder, `<data>\Wikis` (`runner.cjs` `wikisFolder`; the old `workspace-data` is renamed once, and kept in use if the rename fails), one subfolder per workspace; Change folder and Reset are gone (`chooseWorkspaceWiki` removed), and `WikiStore` honours only project copies' own wikis (`{ root, copy: true }`, set by `job-workspaces.cjs`), ignoring folders chosen before. Bots record `createdBy` ('main' for + and the Main Bot's tools, else the creating bot) and `inheritWorkspace`. New bots inherit by default; bots made earlier keep their own folder. `Agents.inheritedSource` follows creators (a chain) to a bot with its own folder or to the Main Bot (also on a missing creator or a loop); `applyInherited` moves inheriting bots after a workspace change, a save, a delete and at start, and `requireDependentsIdle` refuses the change, by name, while one of them is busy. A tool-made bot inherits only when the creating chat is in the creator's own folder (copies and comparisons keep theirs). The bot dialog's Inherit tick hides and disables the folder field. Test: "a bot that inherits follows its creator's workspace down a chain…" in `tests/agents.test.cjs`.
- **Workspace and access live in the Edit dialogs (2026-10-22, at the user's request):** Settings → General no longer has "Agent workspace" or "Agent permissions" (they acted on the selected bot). The Main Bot's Edit dialog has Workspace (Browse) and Access like a bot's; Save sends only what changed through `router.settings(…, 'main')`, which also updates its conversation, and then `renameHub` saves the name and model. Bots keep theirs in their own Edit dialog. Smoke: "the Main Bot dialog saves its access" and the Browse check in the provider settings step.
- **Bot edits by the Main Bot (2026-10-21, at the user's request):** `agent_update` (Hub-only, in `HUB_TOOLS` in `src/controller/coordinator.cjs`, never in workers' `TOOLS`) changes another bot's name, role & instructions and model/effort through `Agents.updateAgent` → `saveAgent` (bot must be idle). Workspace and access are kept. The limits of messaging that bot apply: another workspace needs Full access, and a bot with more access than the conversation is refused. Test: "the Main Bot changes a bot's name, role, model and effort…" in `tests/agents.test.cjs`.
- **Agent access changes:** `agent_set_access` changes another agent's access without a confirmation. It is capped (`Agents.manageAgent`, checked 2026-10-20): never above the caller's own conversation access, only during an active turn, and another workspace needs Full access. So it cannot escalate past the caller; a Full-access agent can only hand on what it already has.
- **Claude cost:** cost may be double-counted if `total_cost_usd` is already cumulative (unverified).

CI runners for the v0.3.0 release (2026-10-21): hosted Windows runners report `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, so `scrollChatToEnd` jumps instead of rolling; the smoke emulates motion through `webContents.debugger` (`Emulation.setEmulatedMedia`) for that check and then checks the instant path. Slower runners also exposed two smoke races, with no app change: a chat switch sent while dictation was still starting (`selectSession` ignores it while `submitting`), and a late render from the Undo steps keeping the chat at its end. To reproduce the Windows runner locally: `node_modules/.bin/electron --force-prefers-reduced-motion tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` (the switch was checked with `matchMedia` the same day). The v0.3.0 tag's first Windows run then timed out ("UI smoke timed out during document attachments after 30606 ms"): the smoke's one 30 s `deadline` is refreshed only at `settings checks` and `provider settings`, and the hosted PDF/DOCX reader took about 27 s to start in between; a re-run of the failed job passed. It recurred on `454df00` (33.6 s), so the deadline is now refreshed when the document step starts (`1921be3`). The same run's Linux job failed once in the file attachments step with Electron's generic "Script failed to execute" (the renderer's error is not printed); the smoke code before that step was unchanged since three green Linux runs, and the next run passed. Cause unknown.

## Per-turn token use (audit 2026-10-25)

Checked against `HEAD` `6741df5` plus the uncommitted prompt-cache and group-chat work in the tree that day; 1,048 unit tests pass on that tree.
- **Fine as is:** Codex worker threads persist (`turn/start` sends only the new message, lessons on a task's first run, and backend-switch history once: `directContext` entries leave after they are sent). Claude resumes its session and gets the instructions as a cached system prefix (`--append-system-prompt-file`, snapshot off), so the prefix stays stable while the instructions do; they change only with the bot profile, a project copy or a resumed task. The API agent replaces its system message instead of stacking one. Hub calls send only what changed on a kept thread (`conversationDelta`); "direct" delegated results need no model call (`6741df5`). Chat checker is off by default; reflection runs once per lesson input (hash cache); the test pool calls Jev, not a worker model.
- **Fixed by the uncommitted prompt-cache work (live on the next restart of a checkout run, not in releases until committed):** a reloaded Codex thread no longer re-injects the full worker instructions every time (`codexInstructionHash`); Cursor, DeepSeek and Muse get the instructions once per session instead of in every prompt (`keptInstructions`); Hub kept conversations survive restarts and also work for CLI and API routers; API requests carry documented prompt-cache keys (`src/providers/prompt-cache.cjs`).
- **Fixed 2026-10-25:** reply suggestions use the answer's model at its lowest available effort (`src/controller/suggestions.cjs`; a preset with no level counts as above the known ones), not the chat's effort; `handoff()` (backend switches and retained delegated chats) leaves out Codex progress notes (`phase: commentary`). Tests: "a reply suggestion uses the answer's model at its lowest available effort", "a handoff to another backend carries requests and final answers, not progress notes".
- **Measured** from the user's stores (Oct 22-25, `routes[].turnUsage`): Claude bot turns 92% cached (97% on later turns), Claude jobs 64%, Codex jobs 76% (78% on later turns). Two back-to-back Codex bot turns on one thread (PhasmaHarness bot, 2026-10-22 02:19) were 0% cached while input fell from 71k to 21k; cause unverified (a compaction or history rewrite is the likely one).
- **CLI turns counted only what the final result reported (fixed 2026-10-28):** the PhasmaEngine bot's chat showed 18.2M input tokens while its Claude transcripts held 52.4M. Checked turn by turn against the transcripts: each completed Claude turn's `result.usage` equals the sum of its main-conversation calls (deduplicated by message id; for example 3,011,921 / 27,044 both ways), but it leaves out subagent calls (one 35-call subagent, 3.6M input), and cancelled turns recorded nothing (0.8M and 6.6M). Subagent events do reach `onEvent` (all 36 of that subagent's tool ids were in the chat items). `sendCLI` (`src/controller.cjs`) now keeps each streamed call's usage by message id, counting `message_start` too, so a call cut off by Stop counts its input.
