It adds only what changed since its last count to `session.usage.total`, so other writers such as the coordinator's `recordUsage` keep theirs, and keeps `route.turnUsage` live. A stopped turn keeps what it streamed. At the end the result tops up the main-conversation calls (those without `parent_tool_use_id`) to its exact counts, or is the whole count when nothing streamed (Muse, Antigravity), and the turn's usage also becomes `budgetFinalUsage` for the job budget. Output counts (found live the same day on the "Bench Opus" bot, 9 calls: 59 output recorded against 8.1k in its transcript): Claude's `assistant` events carry the output count at the time each was sent, while the transcript keeps the final one. So each call keeps its largest output count, the stream's `message_delta` raises the current call to its final count, and the result tops up the rest. Subagent calls send neither `message_delta` nor a result of their own, so their output stays undercounted (the probe below: 1 streamed against 4 in the transcript), while their input is exact. Codex was already counted live through `thread/tokenUsage/updated`. Tests: tests/core.test.cjs "Claude token counts include subagent calls, update while the turn runs and keep a stopped turn’s calls" (its live, subagent and stop checks each fail on the old code), "Claude output tokens come from message_delta and the result, not the partial counts on assistant events" (fails on the first version of this fix) and "traced worker tokens cover the whole turn…", whose fake calls now add up to the fake result as real ones do; pool test `cli-token-counts`. Real stream check (2026-10-28, Claude Code 2.1.311, one Haiku turn through `ClaudeCLI.run` that started one Agent call): `message_start` carries usage under the same message id as its `assistant` events. The subagent's `assistant` event carries usage with `parent_tool_use_id` set (29,513 input), but its calls send no `message_start`, so a subagent call cut off by Stop still goes uncounted. The three main calls streamed 120,729 input tokens, and the stream total exceeded that by exactly the subagent's call. That Agent ran in the background, so the CLI emitted two results: calls 1-2, then call 3 after the task notification. `claude.cjs` adds results together only with live input (which Harness passes); without it, the last result held call 3 alone (40,938). Not checked in the live app. The `total_cost_usd` question is settled in the next entry.
- **Completed Claude turns count from Claude's cost tracker (2026-10-28):**
  - **Test:** an end-to-end run through the real `Controller.sendCLI`, `ClaudeCLI` and `claude.exe` (Opus 5.5 xhigh). Raw output: `counter_test/out/raw-{A,B,C,D}.jsonl` in that session's scratchpad.
  - **Streaming is exact for some turns:** a 10-call turn, and a stopped turn (plus the cut-off call's `message_start` input).
  - **Streaming cannot give subagent totals:** the subagent's final call never streamed as an `assistant` event. Its usage was only in the parent's Agent `tool_use_result.usage`, which covers that one call. Turn B's stream held 98,856 in / 537 out against 123,272 / 1,666 in its transcripts.
  - **`result.modelUsage` and `total_cost_usd` are Claude's cost tracker:**
    - They cover every call of the Claude session, subagents included (123,272 = main 55,448 + subagent 67,824).
    - They carry on across `--resume`: a resumed "Reply OK" turn reported cache reads 325,822 = 309,258 + 16,564 and cost $0.7418 = $0.4663 + that turn, while its `result.usage` was its own 50,574 / 4.
    - So `route.providerEstimateUsd` was cumulative on every resumed chat; that is where the $14.64 for a 0.77M turn came from.
    - `claude.cjs` also added `total_cost_usd` across the results of one process, counting it twice.
  - **Now:**
    - `trackedTurn` (`src/controller.cjs`) takes a completed Claude turn's usage and cost as the change in the tracker since the session's previous completed run. Input is input + cache read + cache write over all models; cached is cache read; output is output.
    - The running totals are kept in `claudeUsageBase` with their session id, and a new session starts from zero.
    - The result replaces the streamed count (`turnUsage`, `budgetFinalUsage`, `providerEstimateUsd`, and the chat total by the difference).
    - Compaction (`compactClaude`) counts its own change and moves the totals on.
    - Live display and stopped or failed turns still use the streamed count.
    - When the change is unknown, Harness keeps the streamed count and the old cumulative cost for that turn and saves the totals, so the next turn is exact. It is unknown for a resumed session with no saved totals (chats from before this change), a session that came back under a different id, or totals that went down.
    - With several results in one process (live queue), `claude.cjs` still adds the per-result `usage` and now leaves the last result's `total_cost_usd` and `modelUsage`.
  - **Tests:**
    - tests/core.test.cjs "a completed Claude turn counts the change in Claude's cost tracker…" and "a resumed Claude session without saved totals keeps the streamed count once…" (both use the real A/B/D numbers);
    - tests/claude-permissions.test.cjs "several Claude results in one process add their usage but keep the last running cost and model totals".
    - All three fail with the tracker unused and the cost added up.
  - **Unverified:** that `modelUsage` is the running total in later results of a live-queue process (assumed from the resume behaviour). The live-queue rerun (E) checks it.
- **Open:** API-provider chats cannot compact (`capabilities` `compact: false`), so their full history, tool output included, goes out on every request; a retained delegated chat still injects its whole task and final answers once (`retainDelegatedChat`, no size cap); a workspace without `PhasmaHarness.md` sends every nested instruction file found under it on every worker turn and walks the tree synchronously each time project rules are read (537 ms for `C:\Users\Christos\repos`, 152 ms for AgainstTheHero), several times per turn; PhasmaEngine's consolidated `PhasmaHarness.md` is 27 KB (~6.8k tokens) on every worker turn.

## Provider entry files point to PhasmaHarness.md (2026-10-25, at the user's request)

Superseded 2026-10-25 by "Per-workspace rules and wiki" below.

Project rules stay in `PhasmaHarness.md`, but CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md may exist as pointers so interactive Claude Code and Codex load them: the starter and the per-turn wrapper (`src/workspace/project-instructions.cjs`) no longer tell bots never to create or recreate them (test "project rules allow CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md pointers to the shared entry"). Harness bots still read only `PhasmaHarness.md` (Claude excludes `**/CLAUDE.md`/`**/AGENTS.md` for managed workers, Codex runs with `project_doc_max_bytes: 0`). PhasmaEngine got local, gitignored pointers on 2026-10-25: `CLAUDE.md` imports `@PhasmaHarness.md`, `AGENTS.md` points to it, and both name the managed wiki (`%APPDATA%\Phasma Harness\Wikis\PhasmaEngine-<hash>\wiki`, engine pages under `imported-<hash>/`); checked with Claude `/context` and `codex debug prompt-input`. Its `.gitignore` ignores the AI files (INSTRUCTIONS, AGENTS, CLAUDE, GEMINI, HERMES, copilot-instructions, docs/wiki) but keeps `.github/` (CI) and `docs/` (manual) tracked. Later the same day `PhasmaHarness.md` itself became local only (untracked and ignored; Harness still copies it into project copies, since `job-workspaces.cjs` adds instruction files even when untracked), and the agent plans and specs (`docs/superpowers/`, `docs/ANIMATOR_AUTHORING_*.md`, 26 files) moved into the managed engine wiki (`superpowers/`, `animator-authoring/`) with source links made absolute; `PhasmaHarness.md` now says new plans and specs go there, never under `docs/`.

Cleanup the same day, at the user's request: only `PhasmaHarness.md` holds rules. Each removed file was first checked line by line against the entry (git history for files already gone) and byte-copied to `<data>/backups/<Repo>-instruction-and-agent-files-2026-10-25`. PhasmaEngine dropped its Graphify `.agents/` rules, dead AionUi skill links in `.claude/skills` and `.codex/skills`, empty `.claude/worktrees` leftovers and an empty `.codex/hooks.json`, and its March 2026 Claude plan moved into the engine wiki; PhasmaHarness dropped `INSTRUCTIONS.md` and an empty `.agents/`; PhasmaProjects dropped `INSTRUCTIONS.md` and its one Cursor rule. AgainstTheHero got its first `PhasmaHarness.md` from `projectEntry(…, true)` (its `AGENTS.md` = `CLAUDE.md` plus four Cursor rules) and dropped those Cursor rules; then, at the user's request, its `docs/wiki` went through `WikiStore`'s own import (saved old location, then `ensure`) into `Wikis/AgainstTheHero-25e023700dee798fba88/wiki/imported-ff242e65533c`, the Cursor skill's text (byte-identical to the personal Claude copy, which stays) became wiki pages under `skills/ath-crooked-ink/` because Harness's skill store only holds revisions of its bundled skills, and its `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` became local pointers (`git rm --cached`, `.git/info/exclude`). Its old rule that `CLAUDE.md` must stay a full copy existed because Claude sessions started in PhasmaEngine with ATH added as a folder drop `@` imports from it: reproduced ("NOT LOADED"), then fixed by approving external includes for the PhasmaEngine project in `~/.claude.json` (`hasClaudeMdExternalIncludesApproved`, backup in `<data>/backups`), after which the same check quoted the ATH rule. Pointers: `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `GEMINI.md` in PhasmaEngine, PhasmaHarness and PhasmaProjects, each naming its Harness wiki, kept out of git (`.gitignore` in PhasmaEngine, `.git/info/exclude` elsewhere). Checked in PhasmaProjects: `claude -p` with no tools quoted the first working rule, so the `@PhasmaHarness.md` import loads it; `codex exec` answered "NOT LOADED" when told not to read, and read and quoted the rule on a normal request, so Codex (like other `AGENTS.md` readers) reaches the rules by following the pointer. Gemini CLI could not be checked: it stops at account setup (`throwIneligibleOrProjectIdError`). The user's `~/.codex/config.toml` default model `gpt-6-astra` is rejected for a ChatGPT account. Later that day ATH was unmixed from PhasmaEngine: the external-include approval and `CLAUDE_CODE_ADDITIONAL_DIRECTORIES_CLAUDE_MD` were removed (backups in `<data>/backups`), and a PhasmaEngine session with ATH added answers "NOT LOADED" again. Existing rule files test (2026-10-25, a throwaway folder with a distinct codeword in AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, GEMINI.md and an alwaysApply Cursor rule, run through `projectInstructions`, `ClaudeCLI.run` and `CursorCLI.run`): without an entry a read-only bot gets all four merged and nothing is written, a writable bot writes them into `PhasmaHarness.md` and leaves the originals; after the files were edited Harness kept giving the saved copy. A Claude bot with Harness's flags saw only Harness's copy (a control run with its own files on saw the edited CLAUDE.md, not AGENTS.md). `codex exec -c project_doc_max_bytes=0` saw no rule file; without it Codex read the edited AGENTS.md. A Cursor bot saw Harness's copy plus the edited AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md and Cursor rule (not GEMINI.md): two conflicting versions, which pointers avoid. Antigravity, Muse and DeepSeek were not installed and are untested.

## Send-to-start latency (measured 2026-10-25)

Measured on a copy of the user's stores (main + 13 bots + worker contexts, 20 controllers) with the scripts' fake Codex client; the live app was not restarted.
- **Message appears:** a bot send sets `pendingMessage` before routing (`src/controller.cjs` `send`); the Hub pushes its message after `agents.acquire` (immediate for the user's own sends), the cached Hub model choice and, rarely, history summarizing (2 of 149 saved Hub sends). Measured in Electron with the real data through `changed()` -> save -> fleet relay -> `applyState`, with `pendingMessage` set directly (so the synchronous work in `send()` is not included; see Live send below): state change to visible 267 ms before compaction, 167 ms after, 147 ms with the throttle below; with three bots streaming 335 / 238 / 114-131 ms.
- **Streaming starvation (fixed):** `changed()` was a trailing 100 ms debounce that every token delta reset (`src/controller/turns.cjs` calls it per delta), so an answer streaming with gaps under 100 ms reached the UI only at its next pause; in Electron a 30 ms or 60 ms stream showed nothing for 3 s. `changed()` now reports 100 ms after the first unreported change (first text visible at 200 ms of a 30 ms stream), saves at most once a second while changes continue (a changed save fsyncs in about 10 ms on the largest store) with the last change always saved, and `Agents.emitFleet` sends the window at most one fleet state per 100 ms (3 streaming bots: 8-9 pushes/s instead of 16 at 110 ms gaps). Cost: steady renderer work while bots stream (35-48 ms per push measured at the 16.5 MB pre-compaction size; not re-measured at 6.2 MB) and saves deferred up to 1 s; explicit `save()` calls at durable points are unchanged.
- **Routing (Auto):** Jev routes in a median 0.4 s, but 32% of routes since 2026-10-20 scan the workspace and take a median 2.6 s. The scan ran `rev-parse`, `status`, then up to 16 `git diff` calls one after another (1.4-1.8 s); `collectWorkspace` now runs them together, which takes 0.32-0.48 s on PhasmaEngine, AgainstTheHero and PhasmaHarness with output byte-identical to the old version. jev-1.14.0 takes 2-5 s at 13-28k input tokens (jev-1.13.0: 0.5 s median).
- **Provider start (routed to first stored item, saved routes since 2026-10-18):** Codex bots 4.3 s, Claude 7.4 s, Cursor 30 s median (p90 64 s). Cursor ACP alone spends about 6 s before the prompt (spawn + `initialize` 1.1 s, `session/new` about 4 s, `set_model` 0.7 s; measured without a prompt); the rest is model time. Since 2026-10-25 a finished turn parks its ACP process for the conversation's next turn (see Warm Cursor processes below).
- **Store and state size:** Cursor `Edit File` results carried the whole file before and after (up to 190 KB each; 62% of all stored item bytes, one bot chat 10.8 MB). `compactDiff` (`src/providers/cursor.cjs`) keeps the changed lines with three lines of context and `startLine`; `compactStoredDiffs` shrinks saved results once on load after writing `sessions.json.pre-diff-compaction.bak`. On the copy: stores 31.6 MB to 11.3 MB, state per push 16.5 MB to 6.2 MB, the largest no-change save 12 ms to 3.4 ms, item ids unchanged in all 113 stores. Window updates now leave out unchanged bots' chats (see Window updates below).
- **Live send (2026-10-25, restarted app, Claude Opus xhigh bot on the folder of repositories):** a window recording showed the message 2.0 s after the click, arriving together with the task line and "working", so the main process was blocked. A CPU profile of the same send on a copy of the data found two causes. (1) The bot folder has no `PhasmaHarness.md`, so every rules read walked about 2,250 non-repository folders (`legacyInstructionFiles`, about 250 ms), five times per send (task, trials, setup, worker instructions); rule loading is now root-only (about 1 ms), see the project-rules entry below. (2) On this machine reopening a recently written file costs about 100 ms per MB (on-access antivirus scan; 0.45 s at 4 MB) and every save reopened its journal; `SessionStore` now keeps the journal handle open until a checkpoint or failed write (1.5 ms per save at any size). Result on the copy: message in the state update 1.58 s -> 0.48-0.51 s, Claude launched 1.39 s -> 0.33-0.36 s. Streaming in the live app saved the growing answer about once a second (52 -> 2,797 chars), so the throttle fires mid-stream. Claude itself took 17.9 s to its first text (xhigh). After the fixes and a restart (same message, same bot): the recording showed the message 0.50 s after the click (was 2.0 s), the route was recorded 311 ms after the send, and the chat kept updating while the answer streamed.
- **Empty Cursor tool cards (fixed 2026-10-25):** every stored Cursor tool call except edits had `result: []` (328 reads, 213 greps, all shell, find, MCP and web calls in the live stores), shown as `[]` in the expanded card. A logged ACP turn showed why: Cursor sends tool output only as `rawOutput` (a read: `{content: <file text>}`; a grep: `{totalMatches, truncated}`, no match lines) and `content` only for edit diffs, while `src/providers/cursor.cjs` kept only `content`. Cursor also names a tool and fills its input in a later `tool_call_update` (a grep starts as `grep` with `{}`), and the controller ignored repeated `tool_use` events. Now `toolOutput` keeps `rawOutput` (text values capped at 64,000 characters; the largest skill file seen is 36 KB) and later updates rename the card and replace its input. Cards saved before the fix stay empty; Harness never received their output. A logged read of a 10.6 KB file (same day) showed Cursor sends no `rawOutput` or `content` at all for it (only a tiny file's text arrived), so a card with no output now shows its input and "reported no output for this step" instead of `[]` (`ui/app.js`); older empty cards show the same. Cursor's reply text was one message per prompt, created at the first words, so text written after tool calls landed above their cards ("…search.**5** matching lines"); text after a tool call now starts a new message and the turn result joins the parts with a blank line. Toasts sit at the top of the visible chat history, not over the composer (`notify`). Tests: the Cursor case in `tests/cursor-diff-compaction.test.cjs` and the card check in `tests/core.test.cjs`.
- **Window updates (2026-10-25):** the 6.2 MB fleet state was 1.94 MB of Main Bot chats repeated at the top level, 3.19 MB of chat items and 2.39 MB of other per-chat data (tasks, routes); settings and catalogs were about 0.3 MB. `trimFleet` (`ui/agent-state.js`, called by the window listener in `src/main.cjs`) drops the top-level copy and replaces a bot's `sessions` with `sessionsKept` when its mark is unchanged: the controller's `stateRevision` (advanced by `changed()` and `save()`) plus each chat's id, item count and status, so edits other bots' code makes still count. Every 5 s, and after a page load, the window gets everything. `keepSessions` (window side, `applyState`) restores the kept chats and falls back to `api.bootstrap()` when it has none. Browser access and `bootstrap` still get full states. Electron with the real data: one bot streaming, median update 0.96 MB (was 6.2 MB); three streaming, 3.0 MB; streamed text visible after 135-196 ms and send to visible 159-167 ms, as before.
- **Main-process freezes (fixed 2026-10-27, user: "lags and spikes all over… when chat start or thinking"):** a CPU profile of a 3-bot, 2-round discussion in AgainstTheHero (`.scratch/lag-bench.cjs`: real controllers, Agents, task gate, relay and renderer on a copy of the user's stores, fake Codex provider, checks cleared, read-only bots) put the main process blocked for 2.8 s of 10.3 s, with 9 blocks over 100 ms (longest 239 ms). The window never paused for more than 45 ms. Fixes:
  - `captureEvidence` ran `git diff --stat` and `git diff` with `spawnSync` after every task turn (172–318 ms plus 120–290 ms on the user's repos). It is now `execFile`-based and awaited, with a gate-ownership check after it. Turns without checks skip it entirely, because the diff only feeds correction lessons and comparison receipts, which both need checks.
  - `runCheck`'s Codex process lookup uses `processIdentityAsync`. The synchronous PowerShell `Get-CimInstance` call took 0.85–0.9 s and froze the app; startup recovery keeps the synchronous one.
  - `planPreset()` checks the account plan before building the catalog, which every bot's snapshot did twice per update.
  - `Agents.snapshot` builds Hub's snapshot once, not twice.
  - `JobWorkspaces.record` resolves the real path once.

  After (3 runs): longest block 96–127 ms, 0–2 blocks over 100 ms, event-loop p99 44–50 ms (was 140), blocked 1.8–1.9 s. The rest is durable saves (fsync plus the store diff) and fleet snapshots, about 5 ms per update. A per-chat snapshot cache cut snapshots in half but was removed the same day, because the UI smoke caught it showing a field changed without `changed()` (staleness up to 5 s) for only about 3% of main-process time. `tests/core.test.cjs` `until` is now time-bounded (3 s), not 40 ticks, because the asynchronous git start takes tens of milliseconds. Not measured: real-provider start-up, or anything with real Cursor, Claude or Codex processes.
- **One unconfirmed cleanup, 33 copies (fixed 2026-10-27):** the user's log showed "Stopped task processes could not be confirmed stopped" 33 times in 0.4 s at 08:30:42 for one id (`81650eb3-…/90`). Every Controller subscribes to the module-wide `processTree.cleanup` emitter, so each of Hub, about 20 bots and their workers called `holdCleanup` for the same record. Each one logged, saved its store (33 fsyncs, the freeze), blocked itself, and started its own 15 s retry sweep. With `agents` attached, Hub alone keeps the record. `blockedReason` reads Hub's record for every controller (still a global block, as before), and Hub's `applyCleanupResults` re-settles every controller. A standalone controller behaves as before. No store held a leftover record afterwards (126 checked). Tests: tests/coordinator.test.cjs "an unconfirmed cleanup is kept once, by Hub, blocks every bot, and clears for all of them" (fails without it), and the record checks in tests/agents.test.cjs "Stop ends the commands a Codex turn started…", which now read Hub's record.
- **Group replies resent all of Hub's chats (fixed 2026-10-27):** a group reply is copied into Hub's group chat by `mirror` (`src/group-chats.cjs`) on every bot update, and its `changed()` moved the primary's revision, so each window update carried all 36 Hub chats (1.4 MB) on top of the 0.7 MB base. Now `changed(session)` records `chatRevisions[session.id]` and leaves `sharedRevision` alone; plain `changed()` and `save()` still move `sharedRevision` (every chat changed), and `flushSave` restores it because persisting already-reported changes is not a new change. `Agents.snapshot` carries both per bot; `trimFleet` marks each chat by item count, status and max(sharedRevision, its chat revision) and sends only changed chats plus `sessionIds` (full order), which `keepSessions` fills from the window's copies (missing copy -> full reload). Scoped callers: the group `mirror`, Codex `item/agentMessage/delta` and `item/commandExecution/outputDelta` (`src/controller/turns.cjs`), and CLI `content_block_delta` text (`src/controller.cjs` `onEvent`); every other event stays unscoped. Measured in Electron on a copy of the user's stores (36 Hub chats, 20 bots; fake Codex bot streaming 150 deltas 30 ms apart; real controllers, Agents, `trimFleet` relay and renderer; 3 alternating runs each, `BENCH_UNSCOPED=1` reproducing the old marking): group update 2.11 MB -> 0.76 MB, renderer time in >16 ms gaps over 5.1 s 409-662 ms -> 48-80 ms, streamed text visible median 159 -> 137 ms and p95 231 -> 197 ms. A bot's own chat was unchanged at 0.70 MB, 66-83 ms and 88-92 ms, since the bench bot's chat is tiny. Still open: the 0.70 MB base per update (non-chat state) and the second 100 ms throttle on the group copy, which keeps group text about 45 ms behind a direct chat. Tests: tests/state-throttle.test.cjs "a change confined to one chat resends only that chat…" and "changed(session) marks one chat…", tests/coordinator.test.cjs "a streamed group reply resends only the group chat…" (fails with the copy unscoped). Bench: `.scratch/group-stream-bench.cjs` (ignored by git; `BENCH_DATA=<copy of sessions.json, agents/, workers/, jobs/>`, `BENCH_MODE=group|direct`, `BENCH_UNSCOPED=1`, run with `node_modules/electron/dist/electron.exe` and `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` unset); not run on the live app.
- **Warm Cursor processes (2026-10-25):** `CursorCLI.run` parks a connection after a turn that ended normally (`park`/`unpark`, keyed by the Cursor session id, folder, structured flag, model picker and helper MCP config) and reuses it for that conversation's next turn: no spawn, `initialize` or `session/load`, and `set_model`/config/`set_mode` only when they differ. Parked connections route updates to no-ops and refuse late permission requests; Stop closes the turn's connection through a per-turn listener; failed or stopped turns and structured one-offs without a session are not kept. At most three stay, each for 10 idle minutes (an idle `cursor-agent acp` tree measured about 270 MB); login, logout and `close()` flush them; quit kills them with every owned process. Projected gain for follow-up turns within 10 minutes: the about 6 s start-up measured without a prompt; first turns are unchanged. Not yet confirmed with real follow-up turns. Tests: `tests/cursor-warm.test.cjs`.
- **Jev routing (2026-10-25):** saved routes since 2026-10-20 show single-call routes at 0.23-0.65 s on both jev-1.13.0 and jev-1.14.0 (4.6k-11k input tokens), so the version was not the cause. Every 2-5 s route took the workspace path: the git scan (1.2-3.1 s then, now 0.3-0.5 s) plus a second classify carrying the file sample (13k-26k tokens over both calls, the calls together 0.4-2.8 s). Shrinking the sample or starting the scan before the first answer trades routing quality or extra work; left to the user.
- **"Unknown provider" after connecting Claude:** Settings asks every provider it connects for its models (`loadDiscoveredModels`), and `providerModels` (`src/runner-commands.cjs`) had no `claude-cli` case. It now returns Claude's models (`tests/runner-commands.test.cjs`).
- **Reply suggestion "you got cut off":** `replySuggestion` sent each message clipped to its first 1,500 characters with no marker, so a longer answer looked unfinished and the suggestion asked the bot to finish. Long messages now keep their first 500 and last 1,000 characters with the omission marked.
- Test cases: `tests/cursor-diff-compaction.test.cjs` (compaction edge cases, load-time migration with backup, scan order and staged/unstaged text) and `tests/state-throttle.test.cjs` (updates and bounded saves during a 20 ms stream, one journal handle across saves, fleet pushes at least 100 ms apart ending on the final state); `tests/reply-suggestions.test.cjs` covers the long-answer excerpt.
- **Freezes with real providers (fixed 2026-10-28, user: "when agents replying or start replying or connecting… the harness is freezing for a bit"):**
  - **Bench:** `.scratch/freeze-bench.cjs` (ignored by git). It boots `createRunner` (real controllers, Agents, providers, connectors, Jev), the `trimFleet` relay and the real UI, on a sanitized copy of the user's data:
    - no jobs, workers, queues or provider session ids; browser access off;
    - every path into the live data folder rewritten to the copy, because old job chats keep workspaces in `Phasma Harness\jobs`;
    - kills of any process that existed before the run are refused;
    - tool approvals are declined automatically.
  - **Scenarios:** `runner.initialize()` as `main.cjs` does, then Claude CLI (haiku) in a new chat and in a copy of the user's 605-item PhasmaEngine chat, then Cursor. The bench records 5 ms main-process and window gap probes plus CPU profiles per phase. Run with `node_modules/electron/dist/electron.exe .scratch/freeze-bench.cjs`, `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` unset, `BENCH_OUT=<json>`, `BENCH_SCENARIOS=claude,claude-bigchat,cursor`.
  - **Cause, by CPU profile:** every window update builds every bot's snapshot (about 20). Each snapshot rebuilt the provider catalog once per preset, because `presets` called `planPreset()` for each one and, on a free or Go ChatGPT plan, every call builds the catalog (83 builds per snapshot). The same snapshot also:
    - hashed the 236 KB benchmark table (`id`) and re-filtered it per model (`summary`);
    - checked each API key file on disk (`JevKey.configured`; `Providers.key()` makes a new key object per call).
  - **Start-up:** each bot searched PATH for the native CLIs and re-read the Codex skill folders while its own Codex app-server started (21 at once).
  - **Fixes:**
    - `snapshot()` builds the catalog and plan check once (`planPreset(catalog)`).
    - `BenchmarkStore` caches `id` and a lookup index until `replace()`.
    - Key-file existence is remembered per file for the process; `save()` and `remove()` keep it current.
    - `findNativeCLI` (live environment only) and Codex's `withoutPersonalSetup` reuse their answer for 10 s.
    - A controller under Agents no longer builds the snapshot its `state` event used to carry; only standalone controllers' listeners get one.
    - Switching workspace asks `browseWorkspace(..., 'repository')` (one `git rev-parse`) instead of a full `git status --untracked-files=all`.
    - `send()` waits for Codex only for Codex turns, Auto and Hub; a Claude, Cursor or native/API turn starts while Codex connects. Before, sends were refused for 31-63 s after launch in the bench.
  - **Before** (v0.3.17 worktree) **→ after**, two alternating runs each, main process:

    | Phase | Before | After |
    |---|---|---|
    | Claude send → first text | 2.15/2.31 s blocked, 8 blocks >100 ms | 0.93/0.92 s, 0 |
    | Claude streaming | 0.81/1.01 s, 5 blocks | 0.48/0.42 s, 0 |
    | Big-chat streaming | 9.0/6.4 s over 53/45 s, 56/34 blocks | 4.8/3.6 s over 63/53 s, 1/0 |
    | Cursor send | one 3.1/2.9 s block | longest 0.20/0.14 s |
    | initialize → Codex ready | 12.9/9.9 s, 27 blocks, longest 2.3 s | 7.3/5.8 s, 7 blocks, longest 0.71/0.60 s |
    | Switching to a bot | 0.61 s, 2 blocks | 0.18-0.21 s, 0 |

    A third "after" run with the last fixes had 0 blocks over 100 ms while streaming; the longest big-chat block was 74 ms. The window's own gaps stayed under 100 ms while streaming throughout.
  - **Still open:**
    - 21 Codex app-servers start at launch, one per bot: about 1.2 s of process creation, since Windows `spawn` blocks.
    - Start-up before the window shows: about 2 s, including a 1.4-1.6 s block (store reads, backups, the document reader).
    - Each update re-sends the whole changed chat to the window: about 10 ms/s while streaming in a 605-item chat.
    - `withoutImages` walks every chat on each snapshot (about 4 ms/s). A cache keyed on revisions would repeat the stale-snapshot problem of the removed 10-27 cache.
  - **Tests:** `tests/core.test.cjs` "a snapshot builds the model catalog a fixed number of times…" (7 builds now; 83 when the plan check runs per preset) and "a Claude turn does not wait for Codex to connect…" (fails with the old gate). Test pool: `responsiveness`. Full suite: 1077 tests, 1073 passed, 4 skipped; UI smoke passed.

## Concurrent host checks

Verified 2026-10-19: a healthy host check in another controller no longer rejects queued assignments or unrelated foreground chat. Its execution block is exempted from cross-context admission only while the same live gate owns the matching process id, the provider connection is ready and the command still has an active check lease. The checking bot keeps its existing background slot and refuses competing work of its own. Completion, failure, timeout, Stop or loss of the gate ends that lease; persisted or unconfirmed execution still blocks new admission globally. No lease survives restart. The live Phasma run exposed this failure when two game checks caused the third and fourth queued jobs to fail before submission. A deterministic regression now holds a native check, keeps the third job queued, admits it when the second slot frees, allows a foreground question and then proves failed termination reinstates the global fence. Sources: `src/agents.cjs` (`acquireReady`, `updateCapacity`), `src/controller.cjs` (`blockedReason`), `src/controller/task-gate.cjs` (`runCheck`, `runLocalCheck`), `tests/agents.test.cjs`.

Stop windows (verified 2026-10-19): the lease ends before a stopped or timed-out check is confirmed gone (up to about 5 s), and during that window another bot's execution block used to fail every queued background job outright. `acquireReady` and `updateCapacity` now keep background work waiting while another context is blocked and admit it when the block clears; a block in the job's own context, and any block for a foreground send, still refuse. `runGate` also catches an admission failure before checks (for example an unconfirmed allowance lookup) and blocks the task instead of leaving the bot busy forever. Regressions in `tests/agents.test.cjs`: "another bot's stopping check delays queued background work", "a failed admission before checks blocks the task".

## Signed-out worker contexts after startup

Verified 2026-10-19: a four-job live batch starts about ten Codex app-servers at once; two or three execution contexts got `account/read` errors ("workspace routing discovery timed out", codex-cli app-server) and stayed `signed-out` while Hub and others were ready. `pump` skips queued jobs whose context is not `ready`, so those jobs waited forever with no error. `Agents.retryAccount` now re-reads the account every 10 s while a queued job sits in a context that is signed out with a Codex error; a deliberate sign-out (no error) is not retried. Sources: `src/agents.cjs` (`pump`, `retryAccount`, `shutdown`), regression `tests/agents.test.cjs` ("signed out by a transient Codex load error").

## Claude Code authentication

Verified 2026-10-18: Anthropic explicitly permits an end user to sign into its unmodified Claude Code binary with their own subscription, including on a platform that hosts that binary. Hosting it in a product/service carries Commercial Terms conditions: preserve native authentication methods, keep the binary unchanged and bill each user directly through their own credentials. This differs from collecting OAuth tokens or using subscription credentials in another API client, which the documentation prohibits. Harness follows the native-binary pattern: `src/providers/claude.cjs` starts the published CLI, reads `auth status` and leaves authentication inside that client. The programmatic CLI interface is documented separately. This resolves the proposed native Max-login approach; it does not establish approval for account sharing, resale or every hosting arrangement. Sources: [Claude Code legal and authentication conditions](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/legal-and-compliance), [programmatic CLI](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/headless), [Pro/Max setup and billing choices](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/11145838-use-claude-code-with-your-pro-or-max-plan). Provider limits and user-selected billing still apply; no paid inference or account change was made by this review.

Authentication/billing conditions rechecked 2026-10-19 against the official pages above. The [October 7, 2026 headless-use update](https://support.claude.com/en/articles/15036540-use-the-claude-agent-sdk-with-your-claude-plan) says Max/Team plans include monthly API credits covering the SDK, `claude -p` and API use, while subscription-limit use of SDK/headless/third-party apps remains available. Harness preserves the native CLI's selected authentication and billing configuration. A subscription-window report does not prove coverage of a different credit or API billing pool; unreported allowance and cost remain unknown. No account or billing configuration was changed.

## Batch worker visibility

Verified 2026-10-19: Hub marks automatically created batch workers with the validated project copy's `temporaryBatch` ID; helpers created inside that delegated scope inherit it. Explicit user-created and reused permanent bots are unchanged. The sidebar retains pending foreground/background work, queues, input requests and the selected chat. Finished temporary workers remain in Overview; editing and saving promotes one to a regular bot. No conversation, report, workspace or profile is deleted automatically. Failed saves retain the previous profile and its lifetime. Sources: `src/controller/coordinator.cjs`, `src/agents.cjs`, `ui/app.js`; regressions: `tests/coordinator.test.cjs` and `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`, pools `coordinator` and `agents-ui`.

## Plain project folders

Plain-folder batching verified 2026-10-18: `JobWorkspaces.inventory` distinguishes Git's explicit not-a-repository diagnostic from unrelated failures and requires no Git metadata in the source or its ancestors. Broken repositories remain failures. `folderInventory` uses a private temporary Git directory only for ignore matching, then reuses `addInputs` for bounded files, hashes, private-path and link checks. The source and copies acquire no Git metadata or commits. `copy` creates empty destination folders, optional Git heads remain null, and external wiki copies do not create placeholder Git directories. Source capture is verified again before worker admission; later jobs reuse the frozen input. `validateSource` rejects home directories, drive roots and Harness storage ancestors. Helper contexts, access boundaries and remapped checks are unchanged. Cancellation removes only the verified temporary metadata directory. Sources: `src/workspace/job-workspaces.cjs`; regressions: `tests/job-workspaces.test.cjs` and the Git/plain variants of `workspacePreparation` in `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`, pool `job-workspaces`. Tests prove copying and coordination with fake model inference, including original-file preservation; they do not prove game behavior.

## Browser access

Authenticated browser transport implemented 2026-10-19: `src/remote.cjs` serves the existing UI, a bounded JSON command endpoint and reconnecting state streams on a loopback-only listener. A public origin must use HTTPS; requests must match its Host and Origin, and state-changing requests require JSON. Authentication uses a private shared key and revocable signed sessions persisted before admission. Browser tickets stay in tab storage and travel in explicit authorization headers, not cookies shared across loopback ports or URL queries. The copied connection key is removed from the visible URL after startup. Key/config filenames start with `credentials`, reusing existing managed-context, workspace-copy and artifact privacy rules. Logout revokes the stored session, disconnecting closes its streams and pending confirmations, and resetting the link replaces the shared key. Closing a browser does not cancel accepted work.

`src/main.cjs` registers native IPC and authenticated remote calls through the same command dispatcher; native sender/frame validation is retained. A remote allowlist excludes credential changes, installation, operating-system dialogs and microphone access. Request-local confirmation routing uses AsyncLocalStorage, so another view cannot answer or accidentally receive a confirmation. Provider authentication errors do not become browser sign-out errors. Wire conversion preserves omitted positional arguments and document byte arrays. `ui/remote-api.js` provides the existing frontend API and a bounded UTF-8 event reader, including Unicode line separators, with snapshot reconnection and no automatic replay of uncertain commands. Settings exposes local start/stop, copy-link, session revocation and link reset. The narrow layout retains a separate Hub shortcut, accessible bot names, usable composer width and scrolling Settings at 150% text. Local dictation remains a desktop feature.

Artifact downloads verified 2026-10-19: `src/artifact-downloads.cjs` stages immutable bytes before sending them. Published files must retain their SHA-256 and size; folders produce current ZIP snapshots, including empty directories and Unix executable modes, rather than claiming a publication-time digest. Inventory and final metadata checks reject changed inputs, private entries, links and nonportable archive paths. The whole export fails on an invalid entry; nothing is silently omitted. Preparation is bounded to 30 seconds, 512 MiB and 10,000 entries, with two simultaneous snapshots/transfers and conservative free-space reservations. Games should publish portable export folders or archives with required assets/libraries; downloading a lone executable does not discover sibling files.

Authenticated preparation returns a random single-use file capability expiring in 60 seconds. This capability intentionally permits one staged-file GET without an account bearer; it grants no command or filesystem access. Host/Origin checks remain in place and links carry no account/provider credentials. Browser downloads stream to disk through the browser rather than buffering a Blob. Logout, session revocation, Stop and Quit cancel preparation/transfers and remove recognized snapshots. Folder inventory checks cancellation/deadline between children as well as during copying. Idle streams stop after 60 seconds, total transfers after 30 minutes or session expiry. Recognized crash leftovers in the runner's `credentials.artifact-downloads` cache are cleaned at startup; unrecognized contents within a download directory remain for review and block further downloads and updates until cleanup succeeds. A browser cleanup error is logged without skipping runner shutdown. Native actions remain Open folder/Run; browser actions are Download file/ZIP and explicit Run on runner. Remote Open folder is rejected. Compact activity controls appear only while downloading or cleanup needs attention.

Verification: `tests/artifact-downloads.test.cjs`, `tests/agents.test.cjs` and `tests/remote.test.cjs` cover fingerprints, ZIP content/modes, source mutations, saved receipt races, active writers, disk reservations, cancellation, private boundaries, single-use links, expiry and revocation. `tests/remote-live.cjs` runs the actual main process, HTTP UI and document parser against fake model submission; it verifies phone-width interaction, an actual browser ZIP download with assets, download Stop, hidden-desktop continuity, confirmation, theme/layout, logout, reset and Quit. Pools: `remote-access`, `artifacts` and `agents-ui`. This is simulated-device/local transport evidence, not a deployed server or real phone/network acceptance. VPS provisioning and Linux service recovery remain open. Sources: `src/remote.cjs`, `src/artifact-downloads.cjs`, `src/remote-settings.cjs`, `src/agents.cjs`, `src/runner.cjs`, `src/main.cjs`, `src/preload.cjs`, `ui/remote-api.js`, `ui/app.js`, `ui/style.css`.

Review 2026-10-19: the headless server now binds its port before the runner initializes, so a taken port fails startup before any queued job is submitted (systemd restarts no longer submit and kill jobs every 5 s); regression in `tests/server.test.cjs`. Still open before anyone enables browser access: the default workspace is the home folder, so an agent running as the user can reach the runner's data. The desktop key is now sealed with OS key protection and snapshots no longer carry inline images (see Tier-2 review fixes above); the headless server's key remains a 0600 plaintext file.

## Antigravity CLI

Current adapter (verified 2026-10-19): `src/providers/antigravity.cjs` uses the native `agy` executable, its existing authentication and billing configuration, text-only NDJSON input, public response/tool events, explicit conversation resume and confirmed process-tree cleanup. Its worker turns require **Full access**; Ask, Workspace and images fail before creating configuration or submitting a prompt. Since 2026-10-31 it also answers Hub and routing replies, tool-free (see "Every model can run the Hub"). The native headless interface has no interactive approval protocol, and planning mode does not enforce read-only access. A unique workspace custom-agent file assigns the current MCP launcher. Session credentials are inherited through this process tree and never written to that file or global settings. Only the unchanged generated file is removed after cleanup, also when the process tree was not confirmed stopped, and by a startup sweep after a crash; user files and changed configuration are preserved. A cleanup failure after a confirmed result keeps the result. An init mismatch prevents prompt submission. Resumed usage stays unknown because native counters may include older turns. Remaining model allowance is still unknown.

Metadata and fixture evidence: native `/usage` returned a successful CLI-command envelope, and `models` returned six tab-separated model entries. The parser was checked against the captured native output without more model inference. This is setup evidence, not a completed live worker turn. `tests/antigravity.test.cjs`, `tests/native-coordination.test.cjs`, `tests/provider-connection.test.cjs` and pool `native-cli` cover scoped configuration, streaming, generic `call_mcp_tool`, startup cancellation, explicit resume, unsupported access, invalid/foreign events, unknown resumed usage, changed-file preservation and links. The Gemini-owner/Antigravity-worker scenario runs the real Harness adapters and local MCP stdio bridge with simulated native protocol peers; it verifies correlated questions/answers, duplicate suppression, foreign-context rejection and exactly one final owner receipt.

Native integration boundary: one bounded real user-turn attempt in a private project read its assigned file with `view_file` and started the assigned MCP server. The stream used generic `call_mcp_tool`; the probe stopped at its unexpected-tool guard before the helper receipt or terminal result. All owned processes stopped and that private project was removed. A prior zero-input startup had only deferred MCP initialization; it did not prove ignored configuration. Effective restricted-tool enforcement, real helper completion, native resume/cancellation acceptance and real-model token accounting remain open. Sources: `src/providers/antigravity.cjs`, `src/providers/native-cli.cjs`, `src/controller.cjs`, `src/providers/capabilities.cjs`, the tests above, [headless events and usage](https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/), [custom-agent schema](https://antigravity.google/docs/subagents?tab=cli), [native permissions](https://antigravity.google/docs/permissions/). No Python SDK or separate API credential was substituted.

## DeepSeek Harness CLI

Adapter (verified 2026-10-19 against `@deepseek-ai/dsh` 0.2.1-rc.2, a developer preview; no model inference or API key used): `src/providers/deepseek.cjs` runs `dsh --profile acp` over ACP with the SDK Gemini already uses. The real peer was driven through `initialize`, `session/new` and `session/close` only. Observed and read from `@deepseek-ai/dsh-acp` / `dsh-acp-app`:
- `initialize` reports protocol 1, agent name `deepseek-harness-acp` and the bridge's own version (`0.0.1`, not dsh's); capabilities are `sessionCapabilities` close/list/resume, no `loadSession`, no modes, `mcpCapabilities.http`, and image prompts only when the configured route supports them. Resume is `session/resume` (same `cwd`, no history replay).
- `session/new` takes `cwd` and `mcpServers`; stdio servers are started and handshaken before it returns (a stub that never spoke MCP made it hang), so the helper bridge is wired like the other ACP adapters. The model is the `model` config option, grouped by provider, whose values are opaque JSON strings (`["deepseek-official","deepseek-v4-flash"]`) that `session/set_config_option` takes back; `reasoning_effort` is a second option (not exposed by Harness). Keyless, only `deepseek-v4-flash` is listed.
- Access has no ACP mode: the acp profile reads `DSH_PERMISSION_MODE` (`read-only`, `workspace-write`, `danger-full-access`) once at launch, and `danger-full-access` is the only one with approval `never`. Harness sets it per host and replaces the host when the access or `cwd` changes; structured calls always use `read-only`.
- `session/request_permission` carries only `toolCallId` and the choices `allow-once` / `reject-once`. The adapter takes the tool name and arguments from the preceding `tool_call` update; without arguments the request is shown but never remembered for the session. Nothing is auto-approved.
- Usage: only `usage_update` (context used/size) exists, no per-turn tokens, so usage is reported unknown. Sign-in: ACP has no auth state (`authMethods: []`), so Connect succeeds when models list; a missing `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY` or credential fails the first turn. Probing creates a persisted dsh session in `$DSH_HOME/sessions`.
- Stop kills the owned process tree (`stopTree`) and confirms it. Windows installs resolve the npm package script (`@deepseek-ai/dsh/lib/bin.js`), because npm's shim is a `.cmd`, and run it the way the shim would (`findNativeCLI`, 2026-10-25): any npm prefix on PATH first (nvm, a custom prefix), then `%APPDATA%\npm`; the prefix's own `node.exe`, else Node on PATH, and Harness's own Electron (`ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE`) only when no Node exists.
- **Electron 44.9.3 broke Connect (found and fixed 2026-10-25):** dsh 0.2.1-rc.2's native loader (`node-addon-require-builtin`) accepts only the Electron builds it lists ("supported Electron versions: 43.0.0, 44.0.0, 44.7.0, 45.0.0-alpha.6"). v0.3.8 (`d1d088c`, 2026-10-23) moved Harness from Electron 44.4.3 to 44.9.3, so dsh under Harness's runtime quit at start and Connect showed only "ACP connection closed", with no sign-in prompt (dsh has none). Now dsh runs under Node as above; checked live under the checkout's Electron: `C:\Program Files\nodejs\node.exe` found, refresh lists 3 models in 13 s; after a restart the user connected it in the app and it answered a short read-only message, so the stored dsh key works. A start failure also appends dsh's first stderr line (`createHost`, waiting up to 1 s for the closed process unless stopped), checked live by forcing the Electron fallback. Regressions: "DeepSeek is found as the npm package script…" and "a dsh that quits at start says why…" in `tests/deepseek.test.cjs`.
- Model list vs the DeepSeek API provider (checked 2026-10-19, dsh 0.2.1-rc.2, keyed): dsh lists its built-in catalog in `dsh-llm-deepseek` (`deepseek-flash` named "DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash", `deepseek-v4-pro` named "DeepSeek-V4-Pro") plus the acp plugin's shipped default `deepseek-v4-flash` (`dsh --profile acp --dump-config`). The API provider at `https://api.deepseek.com` lists only what `GET /models` returns, `deepseek-flash` and `deepseek-v4-pro`: the same two models under their raw ids. dsh chats go to `https://api.deepseek.com/anthropic`. dsh's web-search helper also defaults to `deepseek-v4-flash`; in `$DSH_HOME/sessions` 55 of its 93 searches returned sources, the last on 2026-09-18, so the API accepted that id then although `/models` no longer lists it (a legacy alias, inferred). dsh returns the three option values `["deepseek-official","<id>"]` in the order v4-flash, flash, v4-pro.
- **Installed app could not connect (found and fixed 2026-10-21):** `@agentclientprotocol/sdk` 1.9.0 declares `zod` only as a peer dependency; npm installs it in the checkout, but electron-builder packages only the app's own dependency tree, so the v0.3.0/v0.3.1 installers lacked `node_modules/zod` and `createHost` failed at once with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND`. `zod` 4.6.7 is now in `dependencies` (the only peer-only package in the lock); a local `npm run dist` ships it. `refresh()` used to store a generic error, and `nativeProviderAction` then called `login()`, whose fixed API-key message hid the cause: `refresh()` now keeps the cause ("DeepSeek Harness could not start: …") and `login()` reports it, keeping the API-key text only when no refresh failed. `#provider-status` moved from below the Models list to right under the provider rows, so connect and install errors show where the click was. The `initialize` deadline in `createHost` (every dsh start: Connect, a chat's first message, an access change) was 15 s; raised to 60 s (`START_MS`) at the user's request the same day, since a healthy start continues at once. Measured 2026-10-21 with `refresh()` on this PC: 8 s under Node, 13 s under the checkout's Electron (`ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE`), 12 s under the installed v0.3.1 exe (with the new build's files), and a timeout at about 16 s in three runs under the freshly built `dist/win-unpacked` exe (likely a first-run scan cost). Harness then ran dsh under its own Electron, so an installed release started it about 3 s inside the limit; since 2026-10-25 it runs under Node (above).
- API key precedence (read 2026-10-20 from `dsh-credentials-local`): `$DSH_HOME/.credentials.yaml` (written by dsh's own set/unset) beats the inherited `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY`, then `<cwd>/.env`, then `$DSH_HOME/.env`; the environment applies only while the file stores no value. On 2026-10-20 every DeepSeek Harness turn failed with DeepSeek's "Authentication Fails, Your api key: ****e47d is invalid": the stored key (saved 2026-09-16) was rejected, while the separate DeepSeek API provider's key still listed models. Setting `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY` cannot override a stored key; replace it in dsh. ACP reports no auth state, so the row still shows "API key" until a turn fails.
- Decision (user, 2026-10-19): list every model dsh reports under dsh's own name (`choice.name || choice.value`), like Muse, Antigravity and Cursor; never hide or rename them. A short-lived change that relabelled them by id and hid `deepseek-v4-flash` was reverted the same day. Keyless (empty `DSH_HOME`) dsh lists only `deepseek-v4-flash`. dsh's `initialize` takes about 7.7 s warm against what was then a 15 s deadline (60 s since 2026-10-21); one cold probe failed before `initialize` returned (cause not confirmed).

Validation: `tests/deepseek.test.cjs` (fake ACP peer; pool entry `native-cli`). Not verified: a real turn, a real permission request, the Harness helper bridge against the real peer, and Linux/macOS installs.

## Routines

Assigned input boundary (verified 2026-10-18): when a live delegated worker cannot read its originating chat because that chat is Full-access or in a different workspace, `Agents.readContext` exposes only the request already assigned to that worker. The response is explicitly labelled `assignment-input`; unrelated source messages, logs and history remain private. The exception ends with the assignment and does not permit other Full-access conversations or filesystem reads. Covered by "a restricted worker can retrieve its long assigned input…" (a plain delegation since 2026-10-20) and existing context-access tests in `tests/coordinator.test.cjs` and `tests/agents.test.cjs`.

Redesigned 2026-10-20 at the user's request ("use text to ask the agent again at a specific time"; verified by tests, no live firing yet): a routine is saved text that Harness sends back to an agent, as a new message in the chat where it was saved, on a schedule, once at a date and time, or on each new local Git commit. One-time routines (added 2026-10-20 at the user's request): trigger `once` with `date` (YYYY-MM-DD), `time` and `timeZone`; `routine-time.once` validates a real date, refuses a local time skipped by a spring clock change and runs a repeated autumn time at its first instant; saving refuses a time that is not in the future; after its run settles, `refreshRun` deletes the record. A one-time run missed while Harness was closed fires once when Harness next checks. Tests: `tests/routine-time.test.cjs` "one-time routines run at a real local date…" and `tests/coordinator.test.cjs` "a one-time routine runs once…" (fails without the self-removal). Records are schema 2 in the primary store (`data.routines`): `agentId` (the agent it runs on; `main` for Hub), `ownerId` (that chat; Clear moves it), `createdBy`, `name` (defaults to the start of `text`), `text`, `schedule`, `access`, `workspace`, `next`/`head`, `enabled`/`error`, `run` and `last`. When due, `Routines.fire` calls the agent's ordinary `send` with a `routineRun` marker, so the message queues behind a busy turn like a typed one; the marker is carried through the queue, failover resend, pending message, committed chat item and Hub's message, and the UI labels it "Routine · <name>". A run lasts while its message is queued or its turn runs (`live`), and the next occurrence waits for it, including across a restart. An agent that is not ready (connecting, blocked, busy in another chat, queue full) keeps the occurrence for a later tick. A run is held (disabled with an error) when its chat's access is now higher than the access approved at save, or a Git routine's chat moved to another folder; saving or resuming from chat re-approves at the lower of the routine chat's and the asking chat's access. A failed run is recorded in `last.error`, not held. Ownership rule (chosen 2026-10-20): a routine dies with the agent it runs on, so routines saved in Hub (main or group chats) persist and Hub can delegate when one fires, while a bot's own routines are deleted with the bot inside `deleteAgent`'s save (rolled back if that save fails).

`src/routine-time.cjs` computes timezone-aware local times and weekdays with Intl data. Spring gaps are skipped; only the first instant of an autumn fold is eligible. Calendar misses coalesce into one admitted occurrence and advance from the current clock. Local Git HEAD triggers capture their initial baseline before acknowledgment and poll at up to 30-second intervals, sharing one read per workspace in a cycle. J5 (user decision, verified 2026-10-19): when a Git routine's run settles (its message left the queue and its turn ended), the HEAD at that moment becomes the new baseline, so the run's own commits never start it again. Any commit made before the settle, the user's own included, is absorbed; only later commits trigger. `refreshRun` saves `rebaseHead` with the finished run (restart-safe), the next cycle reads HEAD without waiting for the 30 s poll, and `save()` (edit/Resume) clears the flag because it reads a fresh HEAD itself. Regression: "a Git routine takes the commit its finished run left as the new baseline…" in `tests/coordinator.test.cjs` (fails without the fix). Git reads use fixed argv, no shell, a five-second timeout and owned cancellation. A paused, edited or closed routine cannot admit a stale async read. An empty scheduler has no timer. Future enabled routines alone do not block updates; active admission and Git reads do. Explicit runner shutdown aborts and waits for owned reads; hiding the window leaves the runner timer intact.

Chat-only control (2026-10-20): the tools `routine` (save, pause, resume, delete; omit `id` to create) and `routines_report` live in `src/tools/agent-tools.cjs`, so Hub and every bot get them; `Agents.tool` routes both to `Routines`. Hub sees and changes every routine; a bot sees and changes only its own. Changes are allowed only in a turn the user typed in that agent's own chat: `send` sets `session.userTurn` only when the message is not an automatic result or mail, a routine run or a wiki proposal; the task gate's correction and wiki-maintenance turns (which start through `submitWorker`, not `send`, and carry check output or wiki content) clear it; `Routines.userTurn` also refuses assignment execution contexts and `session.delegation`. The report is allowed everywhere. `tests/core.test.cjs` "one failed check corrects once…" asserts the correction turn is unmarked. `routines_report` returns id, agent name, name, text (600 chars), trigger in words, next run, state and last run; Hub's per-turn context no longer carries routines (it calls the report for ids). There is no routine UI or IPC: the dialog, `ui/routines.js`, the Options button, the status row, the Repeat button on results, `routineSave`/`routineChange` (preload, runner, phone remote) and the `routines` snapshot field were removed, and the runner's `send` strips any `routineRun` a renderer passes. Archiving or deleting a chat that owns routines is refused with "Ask to remove this chat's routines". Copy check lookup falls back through the registered original project root, so nested copies keep configured checks and remap them into their own workspace.

Sources: `src/routines.cjs`, `src/routine-time.cjs`, `src/tools/agent-tools.cjs`, `src/agents.cjs` (`tool`, `deleteAgent`, `clearConversation`), `src/controller.cjs` (`send`, `archive`, `deleteSession`), `src/controller/coordinator.cjs`, `src/runner-commands.cjs`, `ui/app.js` (Routine label). Regressions: `tests/routine-time.test.cjs`, `tests/coordinator.test.cjs` (eleven routine tests: Hub schedule and coalescing, a one-time run that removes itself, user-turn refusals, a bot's own routines and deletion, waiting runs across restart and unready agents, raised access, J5 baseline, paused Git read and shutdown, a real Git repo, the timer, Clear/archive), `tests/runner.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` ("routines are chat-only": no routine element, Repeat button or IPC). Model inference is fake; a live recurring run remains acceptance work.

## Tips

Built 2026-10-21 at the user's request, reworked the same day after their screenshot. `ui/tips.js` shows the same tip in two places: a card under the suggestion buttons on the new-chat screen (`#tips` in `#welcome`, so never during a conversation) and a popup (`#tips-popup`, fixed, placed above the bulb) from the bulb (`#tips-button`) at the right of the sidebar footer, next to the Settings gear (same 1.6x icon scale). Hovering the bulb shows the popup and leaving hides it (250 ms); a click keeps it open until a second click, a click outside or Escape. Each view has ‹ › arrows (no counter, at the user's request) and "I got it", which retires that tip for this viewer (`localStorage` `harness.tips.gotIt`, all back if cleared); both views move together and are rebuilt only when what they show changes. Order: tips that fit this setup first (`fits`: chat checker off, project without checks), then the rest that apply (`when`: reply-suggestion and compare tips only when those can happen; microphone, phone and zoom tips not in the browser view, `window.router.remote`); each new chat moves on. No Show tips tick (removed at the user's request); Settings → General → Show tips (`showTips`, on unless false, boolean-validated, changeable while agents work) hides the card, the bulb and the popup. Tips are text naming where to go: 50 of them (2026-10-21, at the user's request: using Harness, what Auto does, settings worth knowing, getting better results, staying up to date, diagnostics), each checked against the code that day. Text can depend on the setup: the main chat's own name (it can be renamed; the user's is "Bot"), update tips by `update.installable` (bar on installed builds, "update the source folder and rerun Install Phasma Harness.cmd" on source installs), check-related tips only with checks, Jev routing only with a Jev key, Start at sign-in not on Linux. Corrections found while writing them: `/effort` does not cap Auto (it opens the bot's Edit dialog; the cap is Settings → Routing → Effort cap), Ctrl+N creates a bot, there is no drag-and-drop for files and no Ctrl 0 zoom reset, browser access listens on 127.0.0.1 and needs your own HTTPS proxy for a phone. Stale strings seen and not fixed: the `/effort` command description, the Add provider text "run through Codex", the Jev save message naming "General → Router". Text uses spans: the smoke asserts `#welcome` has no paragraphs. Tests: "tips are on unless turned off…" (`tests/core.test.cjs`); UI smoke step "tips" (new chats until the checker tip shows, the popup on hover matches the card, leaving hides it, a click pins it, the popup's arrows move both views and go round every tip, I got it retires the tip so it never comes round again, a click outside closes, Settings turns both off and on; screenshot `.scratch/tips.png`, taken after two animation frames since a capture right after opening missed the popup).

## Reply suggestions

Built 2026-10-20 at the user's request. Settings → General → Reply suggestions, on unless turned off (on by default since 2026-10-21, when the user asked for everything to work without setup). Live check, `.scratch/reply-suggestion-live.cjs` (2026-10-20):
- Codex `gpt-6.1-sol:low` suggested "Can you do it without using the built-in reverse function?" in 8.1 s, so the grey text appears about that long after an answer.
- A repeat call was served from the cache.
- A Muse route returned nothing, with no call and no warning.

The call goes through `smartRouter.complete`, the router's own path:
- **Codex:** an empty tool list, a read-only sandbox with network off, the router's folder rather than the project, and any tool request fails the call.
- **CLI providers:** take the same path routing already uses for raw user text. After an answer, `Controller.replySuggestion` (`src/controller/suggestions.cjs`) makes one structured call (`smartRouter.complete`, schema `{ suggestion }`, 30 s) to the model of the chat's last route, with the last eight visible messages as data. It is cached per chat by the answer's item id, so re-renders and chat switches never pay twice. It returns nothing while the setting is off, a turn is running, messages are queued, compaction runs, the user spoke last, or the call fails (logged as a warning).

The composer (`renderSuggestion` / `showSuggestion` in `ui/app.js`) lays a fixed, pointer-transparent `#prompt-ghost` over `#prompt` with the textarea's font, padding and box. The draft's own text is transparent in the overlay and the rest is `--muted`. The text stays while the draft matches its start (case-insensitive) and grows the composer up to its max height. Right arrow with the caret at the end appends the rest; the screen reader gets it through `aria-description`. Nothing is sent until the user sends. Changing the setting is allowed while agents work (`Agents.settings` local-key list), and it is copied straight to busy bot controllers, which otherwise sync only when idle. Suggestions are not gated by the Background usage threshold (`Agents.backgroundQuota` covers delegated background work only); a limited provider simply fails the call and shows nothing. The chat settings tool (`src/settings-tools.cjs`) does not list `replySuggestions` yet. Tests: `tests/reply-suggestions.test.cjs`, the "reply suggestions" phase of `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` (captures `.scratch/reply-suggestion*.png`).

Read-only chats get none (2026-10-22, at the user's request): `suggestionTarget` returns nothing while `readOnlyNote()` holds, so a sub-bot's chat never asks for or shows a suggestion, and a bot working on another bot's task gets one only after the result returns and its own chat is back. Smoke: "a read-only chat asks for no suggestion and shows none" in the reply suggestions phase.

## Group conversations

Verified 2026-10-18: `src/group-chats.cjs` stores groups as ordinary sessions in the primary controller, with a member-ID list and Hub as coordinator. `Agents.conversation` and the legacy migration exclude groups, preserving the pinned Hub chat and preventing group-to-bot conversion after restart. A leading `@Name` resolves the longest matching current member name and dispatches one assignment without Hub inference; unknown, ambiguous and immediately repeated mentions fail explicitly. The UI member picker inserts that prefix. Other text goes to Hub. Attachment contents cannot select recipients: routing reads the retained user caption. The original request carries group/message/member identities, and existing delegation, mailbox, ownership, budgets, checks and cancellation remain authoritative. Assigned workers read their originating group through `agent_read_context`, not the pinned Hub history.

Groups preserve separate drafts, histories and queues; primary-controller queues settle across chats when Hub becomes free, without replaying uncertain deliveries. The selected chat controls Stop and activity filtering; owner links reopen the appropriate group. Joining changes no workspace, model or access ceiling. Removing an actively assigned member, archiving or deleting a group with outstanding work fails until that work finishes or stops. Clear archives only the selected group and copies its membership. Group editing and initial dispatch roll back failed durable saves before any worker admission. Sources: `src/agents.cjs`, `src/controller.cjs`, `src/controller/coordinator.cjs`, `src/main.cjs`, `src/preload.cjs`, `src/conversation-export.cjs`, `ui/app.js`, `ui/index.html`, `ui/style.css`. Regressions: `tests/group-chats.test.cjs`, `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`, `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/conversation-export.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` (pools `group-chats`, `agents-ui`). These checks use fake models; they do not establish native-provider response latency.

Verified 2026-10-24: `src/group-chats.cjs` keeps groups as sessions in the primary controller. A leading `@Name` starts that member directly through `Controller.send`, without a delegated assignment, acknowledgement/result card or Hub review. Main Bot may continue another chat independently. Other members stay idle; unmentioned text and `@Main` retain the coordinator path. Attachment text cannot select a recipient. Each member has a private native conversation per group, excluded from its ordinary chat and legacy migration. At turn start it receives at most 40 recent shared messages (48,000 text characters); `agent_read_context main` searches the originating group for older context and refuses once membership is removed. It never falls back to private Main history. The member retains its model, workspace instructions, wiki and permission ceiling. Auto routing sees bounded shared context.

Groups preserve separate drafts, histories and queues. Direct member queues can drain while Main is busy elsewhere; a shared member serializes its own group threads. Stop targets only that group, retries failed interrupts, and leaves unrelated work alone. Archive, Clear and membership changes refuse active replies. Restart marks an unfinished reply interrupted and pauses its queue instead of replaying effects. Worker questions and approvals resolve against the group worker session. Configured checks still run; an empty answer or failed checks is reported honestly. Source: src/group-chats.cjs, src/agents.cjs, src/controller.cjs, src/routing/smart-router.cjs, ui/app.js. Verification: tests/group-chats.test.cjs, tests/coordinator.test.cjs and tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs; pools group-chats and agents-ui. Deterministic tests simulate models.

Review verified 2026-10-24: a direct member reply remains connected while that member waits for delegated helpers, including its final follow-up. Groups sharing the member retain FIFO ordering through this wait. Stop cancels that group and its helper subtree, preserves reports, and discards cancelled-result notifications so the next user question cannot restart them. Reply suggestions cannot reuse an earlier Main route during or after a direct group reply. Sources: src/group-chats.cjs (reply), src/agents.cjs (stop), src/controller/suggestions.cjs. Exact regressions: tests/coordinator.test.cjs, “a group reply remains connected when the addressed bot asks a helper to work” and “stopping a group waiting for a helper stops that helper and suppresses the follow-up”; tests/reply-suggestions.test.cjs, “direct group replies do not reuse an earlier Main Bot route for suggestions”.

Live verification 2026-10-24: node tests/group-chat-live.cjs --run codex cursor-cli passed three isolated runner-command turns: Codex gpt-5.6-terra (12,701 ms), Cursor composer-2.5 (16,589 ms), and a resumed Codex group conversation with new shared context (4,655 ms). Each returned the current group code, the PhasmaHarness.md rule code and the exact active wiki path. Private Main context stayed absent, an unaddressed member stayed idle, no delegated jobs/report cards or Main inference occurred, and shutdown reported no remaining workers. These are individual observations, not comparative performance guarantees. No file reads, edits or helper delegation were requested in these live turns; other providers remain unverified live. Logs: .scratch/group-chat-live.log and .scratch/group-chat-live-report.json. The opt-in test uses provider accounts and never runs from the default test pool.

Who replies (added 2026-10-27 at the user's request, "all bots can chat with each other"): the group editor's **Who replies** list holds `group.replyOrder`, an ordered subset of `members` (`save` validates it; an edit that omits it keeps the saved order minus removed bots; Clear copies it). `directRecipients` decides: a leading `@Name` gives that one bot, an unmentioned message gives the ticked bots in order, and `@Hub`, worker results or an empty order return null, the Hub path. `dispatch` checks every member's workspace and access before accepting, then `round` runs `reply` for each bot in turn under one `groupRuns` entry. `run.agentIds` blocks removing any round bot from the group; `run.agentId`/`worker`/`sessionId` follow the current speaker, so Stop ends the round before the next bot starts. Since 2026-10-27 (user request, after "Stop the group reply before continuing" refused a bot edit mid-round) `requireIdle` locks only the bot replying now, and `reply` re-runs `admission` when each turn starts, so a waiting bot can be edited or deleted and its turn uses the new settings or is noted as unable to reply. Test: tests/coordinator.test.cjs "bots waiting in a Who replies round can be edited, and each turn re-checks access when it starts" (fails with the round-wide lock). Each bot replies once with its own `turnId`; from the second bot on, the turn's context carries "This message goes to A, B, in that order. You are B, 2 of 2." and a labelled "Replies so far, to the request below" block before "Current user request:", and `instructions(session, true)` swaps "addressed to you" for "reply once each, in turn". A bot that fails mid-round gets a `groupFailure` notice ("Gemma could not reply: …") in its place and the next bot still replies (changed 2026-10-27 at the user's request after a live round stopped on "Gemma: net::ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED", LM Studio not running); the round stays completed and the queue keeps running. Only Stop, or a round where nobody replied, ends it as interrupted/failed, naming the last bot in `session.error`; a single `@Name` reply fails as before. Test: tests/coordinator.test.cjs "a bot that fails in a Who replies round is noted and the next bot still replies". A refused pre-check names the bot too. Routine runs skip Who replies (`Routines.live` tracks a run by its marker on Hub's path); `@Name` routines still go direct. Hub cannot be ticked: its coordinator turn adds its own user message and may delegate, so it would duplicate the request mid-round. UI: `renderRepliers`/`replierRow`/`numberRepliers` in `ui/app.js`; row order is the reply order, ↑/↓ buttons (touch and keyboard) and HTML5 drag reorder it. Exact tests: tests/coordinator.test.cjs "Who replies answers an unmentioned message once per ticked bot…" and "stopping a Who replies round…", tests/group-chats.test.cjs "Who replies keeps an ordered subset…", and the Who replies block in tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs (captures `.scratch/mosaic/group-repliers-{light,dark,narrow}.png`; at 390 px the rows fit and the arrows measure 24×29 px); pool `group-chats` (now also `--test-name-pattern=Who`) and `agents-ui`.

Group options (2026-10-27, user request: "Group chat should have their options with right click, not at the top as Group"): the header's **Group** button (`#group-edit`) is gone. Right-clicking a sidebar group row (or Shift+F10 / the ContextMenu key) opens the shared `#agent-menu` with **Open**, **Edit** and **Delete**; `agentContextMenu` builds group items from `entry.dataset.group` instead of the Overview card's buttons. Edit opens the group editor (name, members, Who replies).
- Delete (same day, "remove Archive Group and add a Delete in right click options"): Archive is gone from the editor. Delete is greyed while the group has a `groupTurn`, then confirms in `#delete-dialog` in group mode (`confirmGroupDelete`, `deleteGroup`) and calls `deleteSession`, which removes the chat from the store and refuses while a round, routines or outstanding work remain; the refusal shows in the dialog. Member bots and their chats stay.
- Autosave (same day, "For all right click edits make it auto save like Settings does"): `autosaveEditor(dialog, {read, fill, save})` drives `#group-dialog`, `#agent-dialog` and `#hub-dialog` when they edit something that exists. One queue, one save at a time, skipped when `read()` matches `dataset.saved`. Triggers: the form's `change` (text inputs on Enter or blur, the role textarea on blur so Enter stays a newline), Enter (form submit), the Who replies arrows and `dragend`, and Browse. A refusal shows in the dialog's alert line and `fill()` puts the saved values back. X and Esc commit first and close only after the save succeeds. No `api.bootstrap()` after a save (the runner's state push brings it, as it does on the phone). New agent and New group keep their Create button; the edit-mode Save buttons are hidden and `#hub-save` is gone.
- Brief "Saved" (same day, user reports: "Saved is misleading, a previous saved setting still persists even if i m editing the name", then "saved in not visible in these Edit settings … a popup at bottom right", then "i meant the bottom right of the settigs/edits window"): one `#saved-popup` (`popover="manual"`, `role="status"`) replaces every "Saved" text. `showSaved(dialog)` moves it into the dialog that saved (outside a modal dialog it is inert: no hit tests, no screen-reader announcement), shows it and pins it with `right`/`bottom` to that dialog's bottom-right corner, 28 px from the right edge to clear the scrollbar, so scrolling the dialog cannot hide it. It goes after 2 s, or on the form's next `input` (`clearSaved`), in the Edit dialogs, Settings and Checks. "Saving…" and errors stay in Settings' footer and Checks' status line; the Edit dialogs' footer status lines are gone and their footer (only the Create button) is hidden in edit mode. Smoke: the popup is open, on top and inside the group editor's corner after a save, and inside Settings' corner after a setting changes (`.scratch/mosaic/saved-popup*.png`); typing clears it, and it is gone 2.3 s after a save.
- Close buttons (same day): Settings' Close button (`#settings-close`) is gone; every dialog closes with its X. Every dialog X (`.dialog-heading .close-dialog`, `.confirmation-close`) now uses Settings' 26 px size, its SVG icon and the red hover; the icon has `pointer-events:none` so hit tests reach the button. The update banner's × and the remove buttons on chips are not dialog closes and are unchanged.
- Smoke: `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` checks the menu labels, that the header has no Group button and the editor no Archive, Open/Edit/Delete from the menu, Enter, Esc and X saving the name, the arrows and drag saving Who replies, one size and icon for every X, and the red hover (`.scratch/mosaic/close-hover.png`). Its harness registers `deleteSession` and publishes state by hand where the app relies on the push.

Discussions (2026-10-27, user request: "they dont stop until the goal is reached or i say stop", then "or to require my input after they get to a continuous disagreement"): with two or more ticked bots, `round` repeats rounds of the same order until it ends. Each bot ends every turn with `[more]`, `[done]` or `[stuck]` (told so by `instructions(session, true)`). `[done]` means "I have nothing new to add, whether or not I agree". The first names, `[agree]`/`[continue]`, read as "I agree", so friendly bots ended open chats after two rounds of "I agree with Opus…" (user report 2026-10-27).
