The old names still parse as done/more (`STANCE_NAMES`), and the instructions now ask bots to say what they think, disagree when they see it differently, and not open by agreeing with the previous speaker. `readStance` reads the stance from the last four non-empty lines, with formatting allowed. `mirror` drops the `[task: …]` line from a discussion reply (the "Task pending" chip meant nothing in a chat) and moves the stance into `groupStance`, which the window shows as a chip ("Done", "More to say", "Stuck · asks you"; old messages keep "Agrees"/"Continues"). `context()` passes it to the other bots as `stance`. Discussion turns run with `task: 'off'`, so they open no task, checks or diff evidence; `@Name` replies are still tracked. The discussion ends (revised 2026-10-27 after the user's first real discussion, "you all chat about anything" in the 6-bot "Chat" group, ended on a repo design consensus and spent a whole third round restating it):
- after any turn, when every remaining bot's latest stance is `[done]` (`settled`): notice "Everyone had their say after N rounds." (`groupDiscussion.ending`). This does not wait for a full round, so bots that were already done after the last open point are not asked to repeat themselves;
- after any turn, when more than half of the remaining bots' latest stances are `[stuck]`, or at a round's end after `STALE_ROUNDS` = 3 rounds in a row without more bots done (so 4 rounds with nobody done at the least): notice "The bots still disagree after N rounds and need your decision. Reply to continue the discussion." The chat stays completed with the queue running, so the user's reply starts a new discussion with the full history;
- when only one bot is left: "Only X is left, so the discussion ends after N rounds.";
- on Stop, Esc, or a message that is just `stop` (`/^stop[.!]?$/i`, intercepted in `Controller.send` while a group run is active, never queued);
- when nobody could reply in a round (failed, as before).

A bot that fails sits out the rest of that discussion ("X could not reply: … It sits out the rest of this discussion."); before this, the user's Grok bot failed with "Unknown model selection" in every round. That error itself was a bug, fixed the same day. Every bot controller copies Hub's provider model lists when it starts (`initialize(shared)`, `Agents.refresh`), but Hub loads Cursor's list in the background after startup, and `CursorCLI.discover` replaces the array. A worker opened at startup (`openExecution`) kept an empty list, so `cursor-cli:grok-4.7:xhigh` had no variant there. Grok's own chat worked, likely because opening it loaded the list for that bot (unverified). `Agents.shareProviderModels`, run on every Hub state event, now hands Hub's Cursor, Claude and native-CLI lists to every controller whose copy differs, and never replaces a list with an empty one. Test: tests/coordinator.test.cjs "a bot started before Hub loads Cursor's model list still gets its levels…" (fails without it). Grok's slowness is its own: over the 3 days of trace data (`logs/decisions.jsonl`), median turns ran 131 s at Extra High (25 turns), and "hi" took 26 s of worker time. The user's router fallback `routerPreset` is `cursor-cli:grok-4.7:high`, whose 3 routing calls took a median 135 s (Jev: 1.2 s). Each new message gives every ticked bot a fresh start. The instructions also tell the bots to match the user's request and tone (an open chat is a conversation, not a decision, with no repository research unless asked) and, since the user's "they are writing too much each" (2026-10-27), to reply like small talk by default (a sentence or two, casual, plain text) and write more only when they really have something worth saying or the user asks for detail, with one sentence for an agreement with nothing new. Deliberately a default, not a rule (user, same day: "its not hard rule, they small chat unless they really have something to say"), so there is no word cap; `@Name` replies keep the usual length. `context()` strips the `[task: …]` line from quoted replies. There is no round cap otherwise, so bots that keep saying `[continue]` while agreement grows keep going until stopped. From round 2 a bot gets "Replies since your last turn" (items after its `groupSeenMessageId`, or after the message before its first turn), plus "in round N" in its position line. Images go with round 1 only, and the window shows "Round N · Name is replying…". A single ticked bot or an `@Name` reply is still one turn. Tests: tests/coordinator.test.cjs "a group discussion continues in rounds until every bot agrees…", "a group discussion stops and asks the user when most bots are stuck or agreement stops growing" and "typing stop in a group ends the discussion like the Stop button" (the first two fail when every discussion ends after one round). `tests/group-chat-live.cjs --round-only` now reads each bot's first-round reply and expects a conclusion notice (not re-run live after this change).

`/clear` in a group (fixed 2026-10-27, user report with screenshot): the typed `/clear` stayed in the message box after the fresh group chat opened, because `clearConversation` (`ui/app.js`) sets `selectedId` to the new group chat before `applyState`, so neither the draft switch nor `runCommand`'s same-draft-key check emptied it (a bot's own chat was fine). `clearConversation` now empties the box itself. Test: the `/clear` step in tests/group-chat-ui-check.cjs, which the UI smoke runs (fails without the fix: the box still holds `/clear`).

Live round 2026-10-27: `node tests/group-chat-live.cjs --run --round-only codex cursor-cli` (the flag skips the four mention turns) passed in 24,165 ms. Code bot (Codex gpt-5.6-terra) answered first with word "ember" and previous_word null. Cursor bot (cursor-cli composer-2.5) answered second with word "spark" and previous_word "ember". So the second bot read the first bot's reply, both received the round code, the unticked Idle bot stayed idle, no Hub inference or delegated job ran, and shutdown left no workers. This is one observation, not a latency guarantee. Report: `.scratch/group-chat-live-report.json`; log `.scratch/group-chat-live-round.log`.

## File inputs

Verified 2026-10-18: the composer's + button selects text/code files and PNG/JPEG/WebP images. Clipboard file paste shares the same path. Up to four items attach at once; UTF-8 text/code snapshots are limited to 64 KB total, while existing image limits remain. Invalid encodings, control bytes, unsupported binary types and path-like names reject without discarding the current draft. Text and filename contents are data: providers receive a JSON-quoted, explicitly untrusted snapshot, not a filesystem path or new read/write permission. Metadata retains only the original user text and filenames; full file contents live once in the canonical message text. Sources: `ui/attachments.js`, `ui/app.js`, `ui/index.html`, `src/controller.cjs`, `src/main.cjs`.

Each bot's draft retains its own file chips. Failed sends retain unsent snapshots alongside any newer files; limits remain enforced on Send. Sent snapshots use collapsed, literal file details; Copy and Markdown export retain complete canonical contents. Recall and queued Edit reconstruct the original text and files. Queues, Hub requests, CLI turns and foreground Auto failover preserve attachment display metadata. Native Codex refresh retains the original canonical content/metadata, and history replacement retains file snapshots absent from the native response without duplicating returned user messages or accepting forged local receipts. Compact display is used only when metadata and the canonical snapshot match; otherwise the full original text remains visible. Sources: `src/controller.cjs` (`send`, `resume`, `submitWorker`), `src/controller/coordinator.cjs`, `src/controller/providers.cjs`, `src/controller/turns.cjs`, `ui/app.js`; regressions: `tests/attachments.test.cjs`, `tests/core.test.cjs`, `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`, `tests/native-coordination.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`, pools `file-inputs`, `native-cli`, `agents-ui`. PDF/DOCX extraction is described below. These checks use simulated providers and do not establish live model behavior.

Image size (verified 2026-10-19): Codex replays every image in a thread's history on each turn. On 2026-10-19 a Codex thread whose history held two 1920×1026 near-uncompressed PNG screenshots (about 10.5 MB base64 each) sent a 24.5 MB request; the ChatGPT backend closed the websocket, then returned HTTP 400 for that and every later turn, including compaction. `normalizeImage` (`src/image-input.cjs`) now scales images so the long edge is at most 2048 px before re-encoding. Opaque images are then sent as JPEG q85 when that is smaller (see Tier-2 review fixes above). Regression: `tests/documents.test.cjs` ("large images are downscaled", "opaque photos are sent as JPEG").

Image thumbnails and viewer (2026-10-30, at the user's request):
- **Thumbnails.** Attached images are always framed thumbnails (at most 140×100 px), in the composer tray and in sent messages, where they sit side by side. They used to show at full width, up to 500 px tall. `imageThumb` in `ui/app.js` wraps each image in a button ("View attached image N").
- **Viewer.** `#image-dialog` fills the window and centers the framed image. A larger image shrinks to the window less room for the arrows; a smaller one keeps its actual size.
  - The first version set the dialog to `width:auto`, which stretched it across the window and left small images at the left edge.
  - A click anywhere outside the buttons, or Escape, closes it.
- **Stepping.** The arrow buttons and the Left and Right keys step through, and wrap around, only the images sent or shown together: the composer's, one message's, or one reply's (`viewImage` takes the thumbnail's `.message-body` or `#image-previews`). The counter and arrows hide for a single image.
- **Pictures in replies.** A picture in a reply (`.linked-image`) opens in the viewer too. Its Open file button does what clicking the picture did before.
- **Copy image (2026-10-31, at the user's request).** Right-clicking any image (one opened in the viewer, a thumbnail, a reply's picture) opens a native menu with "Copy image" (`src/text-context-menu.cjs`, `webContents.copyImageAt`). The menu cannot tell which image was clicked, so it is offered on all of them.
  - Checked in `tests/text-context-menu-check.cjs` with a real right-click on a 60×40 image. Electron 44's clipboard is async (`clipboard.read()`; `readImage` is gone), and the copy arrives as `image/png`.
- **Test case.** The `ui-startup-smoke.cjs` step "Framed image thumbnails open a centered viewer…" uses one message with two 1200×800 images, a later message with a 240×160 image, and the same small image in the composer. It checks:
  - four framed thumbnails, at most 140×100 each;
  - the viewer steps 1/2 → 2/2 → 1/2 → 2/2 (button, Right, Left);
  - the large image is centered within 1 px and shrunk, and the small one is centered at 250×170 (240×160 plus frame), with no counter or arrows.
  - The step saves `.scratch/image-thumbnails.png`, `image-viewer.png` and `image-viewer-small.png`. The link step checks the reply picture's viewer and Open file.

## Links in chat

Added 2026-10-28. Web links (`http`/`https`, including bare URLs) open in the default browser through `openLink`. Markdown links and images whose target is a local path become clickable: absolute, workspace-relative or `file:` targets, with an optional `:line[:col]` or `#L` suffix. marked's `link`/`image` renderer overrides in `ui/app.js` put the raw target in `data-path`/`data-image`, because DOMPurify drops `C:\…` and `file:` hrefs. The document click handler sends `data-path` to `openFile`. `renderMarkdown` loads `![alt](path)` images as data URLs through `linkImage`, once per message per path, so streaming re-renders do not re-read them. Web images become links and are never fetched. An inline `code` span that is only a path (`BARE_PATH` in `ui/app.js`) gets the same link. A drive path qualifies, or a relative/POSIX path with a folder and a file extension, so identifiers such as `api.openFile`, commands such as `/clear` and globs stay plain. Spans already inside a link and fenced code blocks are skipped.

`openFile` and `linkImage` are registered in `src/runner-commands.cjs` next to `browseWorkspace`, because every `REMOTE_METHODS` entry must exist there for headless hosts (`tests/runner-commands.test.cjs`). `src/main.cjs` passes `linkShell`, which returns Electron's `shell` for desktop calls and `null` inside a `browserRequest`. `src/workspace/linked-files.cjs` resolves relative targets against the selected chat's workspace, using the same root rule as `browseWorkspace`, and rejects UNC/`//host`/`\\?\` paths so a link cannot reach a network host. Directories and an allowlist of display-only types go to `shell.openPath`, falling back to the folder when no app is associated. Everything else, such as `.js`, `.py`, `.lua`, `.bat`, `.ps1`, `.lnk` or unknown types, only opens its folder with `showItemInFolder`, because agent text can be shaped by web content. Images are capped at 10 MB.

Browser access and headless hosts get no shell. There, `open` never opens anything on the host. It returns `{ path }` for a file inside the workspace, and `api.openFile` in `ui/app.js` shows that file in the Workspace files panel (`showLinkedFile`). Images preview as pictures there through `LINKED_IMAGE`, and other files as text, so a linked `.bat` is only read. Folders and anything outside the workspace are refused, and `linkImage` serves only workspace images. Below 720 px the panel stacks the list above a full-width preview (`ui/style.css`). Known limit: a relative path in a worker result shown in Hub resolves against Hub's workspace.

Verified 2026-10-28: `node --test tests/linked-files.test.cjs` (line suffixes, `file:` URLs, network-path rejection, open-versus-folder routing, image data URLs). `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` ("Chat file links, linked images and web image links passed") renders `<a data-path="%">bad</a> <span data-image="%"></span> [main](src/main.cjs:114) [doc](<C:/a b/c.md>) [web](https://example.com) ![shot](C:\tmp\shot.png) ![remote](https://example.com/i.png)` twice. It checks the click targets, a single image read and that the web image renders as a link. It also checks that malformed raw `data-*` values are dropped without breaking rendering (`linkTarget` guards every decode), and makes a preload → IPC → `linked-files.cjs` round trip. The smoke registers its own IPC handlers. "Browser-access file links open in Workspace files, images inside the workspace only passed" covers the phone UI flow. `tests/remote-live.cjs` phase "linked files" runs the real `src/main.cjs` over real browser access. It checks that the phone gets `{ path: 'linked.md' }`, and that a workspace image loads while an image or file outside the workspace is refused. It also checks that the desktop is not limited to the workspace. As of 2026-10-28 that live test fails later at "the phone composer retains usable space", which is unrelated: it fails identically on an untouched HEAD export. "Bare paths in inline code open as file links passed" checks that `ui/app.js:2146`, `C:\a b\c.md` and a linked `src/x.js` open once each. It also checks that `api.openFile`, `/clear`, `tests/*.test.cjs` and a fenced `src/main.cjs` stay plain, and that no links are nested.

## Document and voice input

Document extraction verified 2026-10-18: `src/documents.cjs` owns one separate Node process, a 15-second parsing timeout (plus a separate bounded 60-second Windows memory-limiter startup wait, verified 2026-10-20), an OS memory cap (R5 under Tier-2 review fixes), stripped environment and confirmed cleanup. `src/document-worker.cjs` uses PDF.js 6.4.299 for PDF text and fflate 0.8.3 for selected Word XML parts. Source documents are limited to 10 MB, PDFs to 200 pages, and all extracted text shares the 64 KB attachment limit. ZIP inflation is bounded at each step and at 2 MB total, including forged archive size headers. Main Word text, headers, footers and footnotes/endnotes are retained; `ui/documents.js` rejects DTDs/entities, malformed XML and excessive formatting before converting Word text, tabs and paragraphs. It excludes deleted text and field instructions. Layout, images and annotations are not included, and document chips/summaries say extracted text. Empty/scanned, protected, invalid or oversized input rejects without truncation or replacing existing drafts. Original bytes are not persisted or copied to worker workspaces. Reading remains local and does not execute document actions or contact an OCR service. Runtime sources also include `ui/attachments.js`, `ui/app.js`, `src/main.cjs`, `src/preload.cjs` and `src/runner.cjs`; checks are in `tests/documents.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` and pool `documents`. Actual PDF/DOCX parsing, Unicode text and provider-input Send passed in Electron with fake model inference. Text extraction can lose layout-dependent meaning; OCR, legacy DOC, spreadsheet and presentation input are outside this slice. Parser API references: [PDF.js](https://mozilla.github.io/pdf.js/api/draft/module-pdfjsLib.html), [fflate streaming ZIP](https://github.com/101arrowz/fflate/blob/master/docs/classes/Unzip.md).

Whisper since 2026-10-21 (user report: Windows' `System.Speech` desktop recognizer, English-only and untrained, turned speech into mumbling; it and `src/dictation.ps1` are gone, with no fallback). The page records and a local Whisper transcribes; nothing is sent to a transcription service. `src/dictation.cjs` (`Dictation`): `start` opens a recording (`listening`; `recording` is what the microphone permission checks) and warms the engine meanwhile; `transcribe(id, audio)` validates the open recording, an even byte length and the five-minute cap (`MAX_BYTES`) before anything reaches native code, skips silence (`voiced`: at least 0.5 s with 0.2 s of 20 ms frames above about -36 dBFS; Whisper turns near-silence into `[BLANK_AUDIO]` or invented glyphs and noise once took 61 s on the CPU), strips tags and non-Latin/Greek noise (`cleanText`) and emits one `text` event (sequence 1); `stop` asks the page for its audio (`stopping`, ended without words after `stopMs`), `stop(id, { discard })` ends it at once. Engine: `src/whisper-worker.cjs`, forked with `gameEnvironment()` and `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` like the document worker, loads `@fugood/node-whisper-<platform>-x64-vulkan` (GPU) else the CPU build (optionalDependencies 1.1.3 exact, prebuilt N-API, no install scripts; not the `@fugood/whisper.node` wrapper, which pulls 125-200 MB CUDA builds), warms on two seconds of silence and calls a second warm second under 1 s a GPU. Models (`MODELS`, Hugging Face `ggerganov/whisper.cpp`, size and SHA-256 pinned, `.part` then rename, in `<data>/speech-models`): base-q5_1 (60 MB) on first use with progress in the mic status; a GPU machine then downloads small-q5_1 (190 MB) and switches; small already on disk loads directly. Requests are queued so a model switch never releases a transcribing context; the worker exits after 10 idle minutes. Language is auto-detected (`language: 'auto'`): the user dictates in both English and Greek (decided 2026-10-21), at the cost of about twice the CPU time and an occasional accented-English clip written in Greek letters. `src/main.cjs`: `microphoneAllowed` (unit-tested) gates both the permission request and check handlers: media, audio only, the main window at `rendererURL`, while recording; other checks keep Electron's default; requests other than the microphone stay denied. Hide, blur and minimize ask for the audio; close and renderer exit discard. The remote browser view still has no voice.

`ui/dictation.js` records after `voiceStart` (getUserMedia audio only, `AudioContext` at the device rate, `ui/pcm-capture.js` worklet averaging to 16 kHz 16-bit PCM; a 16 kHz context fed by a 48 kHz device is avoided). Status: "Starting…" until the microphone really records, "Listening…", "Writing it down…", or the model download percent. Stop, Send, a chat switch, a dialog or the five-minute limit end the capture and send the audio (`voiceTranscribe`); words land in the original chat's draft before Send continues. The capture opens after the button's busy period, since chat switching is ignored while `submitting`; a capture that opens after Stop closes at once. Measured 2026-10-21 (i9-14900KS, 9.2 s English TTS clip, `.scratch/dictation-live.cjs` with the real worker): words 0.5 s after Stop when the warm-up overlapped the speaking, 0.2 s on the next recording (small, GPU); CPU builds: base 2.5-3 s with a forced language, about twice with auto, small 11-22 s; the real download took 3.7 s. Tests: `tests/dictation.test.cjs` (fake worker and fetch), UI smoke "dictation checks" (a tone through the real worklet: 0.4 s recorded as 12,800 bytes), pools `dictation`, `agents-ui`. Not verified: a live microphone, Greek accuracy, Linux (the Linux builds are in the lockfile but untested).

## Background allowance guard

Native Claude allowance capability verified 2026-10-18: the installed published CLI accepts the control request `{subtype: "get_usage", skip_behaviors: true}` after initialization, without a user prompt or model response. The published SDK 0.3.302 declares structured subscription windows with percentages and ISO reset times; `skip_behaviors` avoids scanning seven days of local transcripts. A successful local metadata request returned `rate_limits_available: false` and no windows, so current availability remains unknown. This verifies the transport, not a measured Max allowance. Consume a validated report through the native CLI when available; do not extract OAuth tokens or call private account endpoints from Harness. The current native CLI is signed out; actual Max-window acceptance remains open. Sources: [published SDK definitions](https://unpkg.com/@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk@0.3.302/sdk.d.ts), native CLI metadata request, `src/providers/claude.cjs` for the existing initialization transport.

Since 2026-10-22 (the user removed the setting): background work holds only on a window reported full (100%); there is no Settings threshold, and `backgroundGuard` is called with 100 (`Agents.backgroundQuota`). A full ChatGPT window was already a usage limit (`codexUsage` → `limits.mark`, until its reset or `UNKNOWN_RESET_MS`); a full Claude window is caught only by the guard. Verified 2026-10-18, still true: valid ChatGPT primary/secondary usage reports are saved in `providerQuotas`, shared across all controllers and exposed as last-reported values. Missing/malformed reports do not fabricate remaining allowance or erase a valid reserve; reports above 100% still hold. A known reset expires its window; a window without a reset remains held until a valid lower report. Credits do not bypass this reserve. Other providers' remaining allowances stay unknown. Sources: `src/providers/limits.cjs` (`setUsage`, `backgroundGuard`), `src/agents.cjs` (`backgroundQuota`, `sync`).

Queued assignments retain their accepted IDs and content with a durable `quotaPause`, before routing or incrementing execution attempts. A versioned never-submitted queue recovers with its reserve intact. Existing turn admission/capacity waits also hold subsequent background submissions, release unused slots, and remain cancellable. Direct chat bypasses the background reserve. Auto waits on known usage limits rather than selecting another account to bypass them; an explicit provider selection can choose a different account. Reported reset times schedule a bounded wake without polling. A lower report or changed threshold can release a reserve; actual provider-limit failures during a worker/router turn keep the job interrupted for review and pause its queue, since execution may be partial. They do not fail over or replay automatically. Owner tools and UI activity/details show the pause reason. Sources: `src/agents.cjs` (`backgroundQuota`, `recordQuotaPause`, `quotaWake`, `acquire`, `updateCapacity`, `pump`, `tool`), `src/controller.cjs` (`send`, `submitWorker`), `src/controller/providers.cjs` (`providerOutcome`); regressions: `tests/limits.test.cjs`, `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`, pools `background-usage`, `local-agents`, `agents-ui`.

This is admission control using the last supported provider report, not a hard spending cap. Already admitted native turns can finish and consume additional usage; unreported subscription allowances cannot be guarded. It does not establish per-job cost or token totals, time budgets or live-provider behavior. Direct foreground Auto retains its existing enabled-provider failover policy. No paid API or real-model calls were used to validate this slice.

Claude guard integration verified 2026-10-19: `ClaudeCLI.readUsage` owns a bounded metadata process, initializes the documented control protocol and requests `get_usage` with `skip_behaviors: true`. It submits no user prompt; tools/MCP/hooks are disabled and native authentication stays in the CLI. Cleanup is confirmed before accepting a report, with an owned retry handle on uncertainty. `accountKey` hashes native auth metadata without retaining credentials. `ProviderLimits.setUsage` keeps only validated allowance windows, labels and reset times; it discards session/transcript details and binds saved windows to that account key. Five-hour, weekly and CLI/SDK windows apply across models; Opus/Sonnet windows keep their family scope. Unmapped model scopes protect Claude background work conservatively and explain the hold. Classification uses the full native model label; only its displayed caption is shortened. A complete valid model-scoped list replaces that account's earlier scoped entries, including an empty list. Missing or malformed lists retain known windows; neither invents a zero.

`Agents.acquire` queries verified, enabled Claude accounts before a background model request, then rechecks stop, revision, owner context and sign-in state. Same-account requests share a thirty-second cache; native usage events invalidate it. Stale held queues refresh immediately, while continuing holds recheck at most five minutes later using the existing quota wake timer. Rechecks are metadata-only and stop when there is no matching held work. A changed allowance or threshold can release the wait; direct chat stays independent. Existing native hard-limit errors retain their own expiry/review behavior. Settings renders only matching-account quota windows. Sources: `src/providers/claude.cjs`, `src/providers/limits.cjs`, `src/agents.cjs`, `src/controller.cjs`, `src/main.cjs`, `ui/app.js`; tests: `tests/claude-usage.test.cjs`, `tests/limits.test.cjs`, `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`; pool `claude-allowance`. The actual installed getter returned no windows with confirmed cleanup, and `claude auth status` reported signed out. Actual Max limits remain unverified; no model inference ran in these native checks.

## Foreground and execution contexts

Verified 2026-10-18: each bot retains its original public chat controller and one optional execution controller for delegated work. `Agents.execution` creates and registers the execution store at `workers/<agentId>/sessions.json` (Hub uses `main`). Registration is durable before initialization; a failed registration can retry an empty store, but an unregistered nonempty store is retained untouched for review. Startup opens only registered contexts. The sidebar still shows one bot. Legacy public-context assignments keep their original IDs and owners. `allControllers` includes both contexts in capacity, owned-job lookup, approvals, Stop, cleanup, updates and restart reconciliation. Workspace/access changes, clearing and deletion check both contexts. Sources: `src/agents.cjs` (`openExecution`, `execution`, `allControllers`, `requireIdle`, `settings`, `snapshot`); regressions: `tests/agents.test.cjs`, pool `local-agents`.

Mail IDs include the source conversation, so foreground and execution exchanges from the same bot cannot collide. Scoped job messages and answers return to the actual assignment/source context. Retry deduplication searches both recipient contexts, retaining the original saved receipt even after the job finishes or routing changes. Context reads use the corresponding source/owned job and retain existing permission/workspace limits. Result ownership remains a conversation identity, not whichever bot chat happens to be open. Sources: `src/agents.cjs` (`sendMessage`, `readContext`, `tool`); regressions: `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`.

The UI keeps foreground Send available during delegated work. Activity exposes actual owner, Details, Stop and explicit Review for a background approval. Details is a nonmodal side panel containing a bounded saved transcript, actual error/access/revision, and a correlated update form. Escape/outside dismissal preserves the foreground draft; the panel reserves space so it cannot cover Send. Background approvals stay pending until Review and can be deferred without losing the request; replies carry the exact bot/context identity. Update retries retain their amendment ID and expected revision; Received and Applied come from saved state. Sources: `src/main.cjs`, `src/preload.cjs`, `ui/app.js`, `ui/index.html`, `ui/style.css`; regressions: `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`, pool `agents-ui`, including 840px width at 150% font. Phone layouts remain part of the later remote/phone phase.

These checks use isolated fake providers, not live inference. Separate controller admission does not guarantee immediate model responses: the existing single local endpoint still serializes inference (see Local model memory and context below). The isolated Electron load fixture records UI latency separately in `.scratch/async-chat-latency.json`; native live latency remains unmeasured.

## Runner recovery and rollback

Linux service lifetime verified 2026-10-19: `src/runner-containment.cjs` delegates to `src/runner-linux.cjs` on Linux. Recovery is safe only inside the service named from the canonical data-directory hash, with matching actual MainPID, InvocationID and ControlGroup. Live systemd properties must enforce control-group termination, a 20-second stop deadline followed by SIGKILL, main-process exit tracking, no delegation and the supplied privilege/cgroup protections. An environment variable alone never enables recovery; ordinary Linux desktops remain unsupported by this service guarantee. The actual user service test in WSL/systemd 255 forcibly killed its Node runner, removed detached descendants that ignored SIGTERM and admitted a replacement only afterward. Fixtures use no provider authentication or model calls. This proves process lifetime, not a security sandbox or full hosted-job recovery. The headless entry and service setup are implemented; [Linux hosting](hosting.md) records their scope and remaining acceptance. Sources: `src/runner-linux.cjs`, `tests/runner-linux.test.cjs`, `tests/runner-linux-live.cjs`, [systemd cleanup](https://www.freedesktop.org/software/systemd/man/latest/systemd.kill.html), [service exit tracking](https://www.freedesktop.org/software/systemd/man/260/systemd.service.html).

Shared commands verified 2026-10-19: `src/runner-commands.cjs` registers the existing chat, group, artifact, confirmation and file operations without Electron imports. Native IPC keeps its sender/frame checks; browser dispatch keeps its authentication and allowlist. Both runtimes now use owned image processing, with shared size/type and trusted attachment-metadata checks. Stop cancels matching validation before it can admit a delayed message; unrelated chats continue. Closing admission and controller ownership are checked again afterward. `tests/runner-commands.test.cjs` covers the command contract, validation, cancellation, normalized image bounds and removed-owner race. The actual HTTP/Electron smoke includes image input; the real Node service fixture covers the same bindings without a display. See [Linux hosting](hosting.md) for setup and exact verification limits.

Verified 2026-10-18: before constructing controllers, `createRunner` backs up existing Hub, named-agent and registered execution JSON stores beside each file as `sessions.json.pre-runner-recovery-v1.bak`. Registry entries must be unique known bot IDs or `main`; paths stay fixed under `workers`. The backup materializes the checkpoint and committed journal into standalone JSON and is verified as a regular file; later launches preserve it. Invalid agent paths or an unverifiable backup stop startup before recovery rewrites the stores. Provider credential files are not copied or migrated. Additive job fields remain readable by the prior storage format; checkpoint journals before opening stores with an older build. New contained Windows assignments record a recovery version, canonical workspace and submission-attempt count. The attempt count is saved before provider submission. After the previous lifetime job is empty, restart resumes only never-submitted queues whose source, result owner, workspace, parent revision and permission ceilings still match. Legacy, attempted, paused, missing-owner and incompatible work remains undelivered as Needs review. Interrupted turns pause automatic replies too, so a late result cannot silently resume uncertain execution. Sources: `src/runner.cjs`, `src/agents.cjs`, `src/controller.cjs`; regressions: `tests/runner.test.cjs`, `tests/agents.test.cjs`, pools `background-runner` and `local-agents`.

Stop records the affected assignment IDs in the request's own controller store before traversing children or delivering cancellation reports. Startup reconciles these records across all stores before considering any queued recovery. A crash between the source save and child cancellation therefore cannot resurrect an acknowledged Stop; a failed result delivery cannot undo it. If cancellation storage fails, Stop still interrupts every selected active worker and returns the storage error; unrelated work behind a cancelled queued job remains active. Records retain original ancestry across result-owner handoffs and are pruned against retained pending jobs when another Stop is recorded. Updates also wait for deferred reviews, pending revisions, background connections and browser sign-in completion. Completed cached initialization promises are not pending work. Failed unsent drafts (failed session, message error, no submission) are also not pending work: their retry content stays saved, while active or unconfirmed submissions remain update blockers (verified 2026-10-20). Sources: `src/agents.cjs`, `src/runner.cjs` (`hasPendingWork`); regressions: `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/runner.test.cjs`.

For a manual rollback, Quit runner first. Keep copies of the current JSON files and their adjacent `.journal` files. Move the live `.journal` files aside before restoring the desired standalone `.pre-runner-recovery-v1.bak` files over their matching `sessions.json` files; then relaunch a compatible build. A stale journal must never accompany a restored snapshot. These copies preserve the first recovery snapshot, not every later conversation change; restoring one replaces newer history in that store. Never restore provider credential files or replay an uncertain assignment merely because its backup says Running. Windows lifetime containment is described below.

Restart decisions (verified 2026-10-19 with fake providers): an assignment held as Needs review after a restart changes only through the user's decision in its job details (`reconcileJob` in `src/agents.cjs`; command `reconcileJob`, desktop and browser). Agent tools, Hub actions, routines and late receipts cannot reach it.
- **Return as interrupted** works for any held job. It delivers the job to its owner with status `interrupted`, its saved evidence and a note that it was not run again. The owner and its batch then settle. A failed owner save keeps the job held; once the owner has saved the result, a retry finds that delivery instead of adding a second one.
- **Start now** only appears for work that was queued and never started (`recovery.kind: not-started`) on a contained runner (`recoveryVersion: 1`). It reruns the original source, owner, permission, parent-revision and workspace checks; only the owner-queue pause that held it is waived. It refuses while the budget is paused, while an earlier job for that bot is undecided, and while that bot has saved messages from before the restart. The decision is saved before the job is queued, and the existing attempt counter still prevents a second automatic start after another crash.
- Work that may have run (`uncertain`) has no rerun path. Jobs saved by an uncontained or older runner only offer Return.
- `restartRecovery` keeps the first restart's record until the job resumes. A later restart of resumed work records that run as uncertain instead of keeping a stale "not started".
- Regressions in `tests/agents.test.cjs`: "returns to its owner only by the user decision", "never-started assignment held after restart", "second restart after resumed work"; UI smoke phase "restart reconciliation".
- Still open: a live forced restart with real providers, and hosted/server game recovery.

Windows process lifetime (verified 2026-10-18): `src/runner-containment.cjs` waits for a private named-pipe handshake before any controller starts. A hidden PowerShell helper creates a Windows job with `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`, opens the runner handle, verifies the pipe client's PID and challenges the live parent before assigning it. The pipe ACL permits only the current Windows account. The globally named job hashes the canonical user-data path. A replacement runner checks its active-process count before joining; while an earlier job remains occupied it closes its probe handle between bounded startup retries, allowing the old monitor's last-handle cleanup to finish. An overlapping live runner is refused without terminating the owner. The monitor is outside the job and holds its lifetime handle; runner death or helper failure therefore stops normal inherited child trees, including detached descendants. Startup failure does not start workers. This avoids PID-reuse guesses and continuous process scans. Primary references: [Microsoft job objects](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/procthread/job-objects), [named job creation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winbase/nf-winbase-createjobobjecta).

The two static `runner-job*.ps1` files ship as ordinary files through the existing `src/**/*` packaging rule (`asar: false`). The short bootstrap starts the monitor outside [libuv's direct-child job](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/libuv/libuv/v1.x/src/win/process.c); otherwise an update installer would inherit the old runner's lifetime. Losing the private control connection allows five seconds for runner exit, then terminates only the already-verified runner handle. An accepted update waits for the old job to empty and close before launching its verified installer outside it. Isolated tests cover overlap refusal, restart cleanup, runner crash, graceful exit, monitor crash, control-lease loss and installer survival, using exact process identities and private fixtures; `tests/runner-live.cjs` checks Electron Quit. Existing per-task Stop and verified process cleanup remain in place. This controls process lifetime, not permissions or filesystem access; external service/COM brokers can create processes outside an inherited child tree. Linux/server containment and older uncontained runs remain outside this Windows guarantee.

## Hub coordination

Planning access and live reliability (2026-10-23): Hub's tool-free planning process uses a read-only native sandbox even when its user chat has Full access. A real Codex `gpt-5.6-terra:low` restart acceptance run incorrectly refused to dispatch writable workers because the planning input omitted the chat permission ceiling. `src/controller/coordinator.cjs` now supplies `access: session.access` on every decision and explicitly distinguishes the planner's sandbox from worker permissions. Worker admission still enforces the source chat and recipient ceilings. `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`, case `Hub planning receives the current chat access independently of its read-only runtime`, fails before the fix and checks Full, Workspace and Ask after it; pool `coordinator` includes it.

The corrected Windows live run dispatched three real Codex jobs into private project copies: two commands were active and one job waited for a slot. Killing only the private runner removed both command processes. Restart on the same stores submitted the never-started job once; the two previously active jobs stayed interrupted/Needs review without replay. Stop of the recovered job produced exactly one cancelled receipt, and shutdown reported no remaining processes. A separate real parent/helper run verified that stopping the parent cancels both command processes and returns one parent receipt, while unrelated queued work starts normally and cancelled work stays stopped. These cases use `.scratch/restart-live.cjs` and `.scratch/reliability-nested-live.cjs`; deterministic coverage remains in `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/shutdown.test.cjs` and `tests/runner-containment.test.cjs`. This verifies Windows/Codex recovery and cancellation, not every provider or hosted-game recovery.

Model choice before dispatch (2026-10-20, after one live batch failed with "Sent 0/4 agent tasks. Model unavailable"):
- **What Hub sends:** six replays of that batch's first Hub decision (`.scratch/hub-dispatch-replay.cjs`, tool call intercepted) all sent `mode: codex:gpt-6.1-sol:medium` with `effort: low`, which resolves to `codex:gpt-6.1-sol:low` and was available. So the failed run either sent a mode that did not resolve, or briefly had no Codex models. Its log (synchronous `appendFileSync`) had no disconnect warning.
- **Logging:** `refreshAccount` used to drop the account and every Codex model on any failed call without a trace; it now logs a warning. `Agents.agentMode` logs the requested mode, the resolved id, `enabled`, the Codex model count and whether an account exists whenever a requested model is unavailable. The next occurrence names its cause in `harness.log`.
- **Check before reserving:** `agent_work` for new workers resolves the model and effort with `agentMode` (shared with `agent_create`) before checking capacity or reserving batch copies. A rejected batch reserves nothing.
- **One retry:** model and effort errors are marked `retryable` (nothing was created or sent). The Hub step loop returns the first one to Hub as a tool result with the `agent_models` choices. A second one ends the turn with "… Nothing was sent." instead of failing it. Any error after dispatch starts still fails the turn, so no work is sent twice.
- **Tests:** in `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`, "Hub honors explicit worker model and effort" (correction, then a second rejection) and "a batch with an unavailable model reserves nothing". Both fail on the previous code.

Hub context cost (measured live 2026-10-19, `node .scratch/hub-context-measure.cjs <label>`: fresh Hub, gpt-6.1-sol low, four short chat messages; reports in `.scratch/hub-context-{before,after}.json`):
- **Before:** each Hub call started a new ephemeral Codex thread, so nothing was cached: about 9.2K input tokens per call, 0% cached, 37,219 input in total. The conversation was under 2K characters; the instructions were 14,392 characters, about 10.5K of them a JSON dump of every tool's argument schema that the response schema already carries.
- **Change:** `SmartRouter.completeOnce` keeps one subscribed Codex thread per Hub chat (`conversation: { key: session.id, context }` from `sendCoordinator`). Later calls send `conversationDelta()`: new messages (including the reply the user saw), new tool results, and changed or removed fields; images once per request. A different model, effort or instructions, a new app-server connection, a chat that no longer extends what the thread saw, or a failed turn start each start a fresh thread with the full prompt. The instructions list each tool once by name and description (9,873 characters). Completion checks, summaries and non-Codex providers keep the one-shot path.
- **After:** 33,632 input, 72% cached; full-price input fell from 37,219 to 9,312 (-75%). The fourth turn still answered from the first, so context carried over.
- **Regressions:** "conversation deltas carry…" and "a Hub chat keeps one Codex thread…" in `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`.

Hub rename (verified 2026-10-19): `Agents.renameHub` saves `primary.data.hubName` (1-60 characters, no other bot's name, not "main"). `hubName()`/`agentName(id)` replace every former `'Hub'` fallback in `src/agents.cjs`, the snapshot's first agent row, group members and owner, and the Hub prompt (`INSTRUCTIONS(name)`: "You are <name>, the Hub: …"). `reservedName()` keeps "hub", "main" and the current name away from bots. Role labels (Hub Auto, "Hub stopped.") keep the word Hub. UI (updated 2026-10-20): **Edit** on the Hub Overview card and its context menu opens `#hub-dialog` for name and model, saved together (autosaved since 2026-10-27). Default name (2026-10-27, user request "Default rename Main Bot as Assistant"): an unset `hubName` now reads "Assistant" (was "Hub"), in `hubName()` and the UI fallbacks in `ui/app.js` and `ui/tips.js`; "hub" stays reserved so `@Hub` still reaches it. A store with a saved `hubName` (the user's had "Main Bot") keeps it; the default only applies when none is saved. The main bot's Edit dialog sends `mode` only when the model changed, so a rename does not fail on an unavailable model. Regressions in `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/coordinator.test.cjs` and the UI smoke.

Every model can run the Hub, Auto routing, the chat checker, reflection and reply suggestions (2026-10-31, the user: "no weird rules or blocks… because we didn't have access to them we can't say they don't work").
- **What was wrong.** From v0.2.0 (2026-10-19) Muse and Antigravity were marked `structured: false`, which the wiki called "refuse a schema". In fact Harness's own adapters threw before asking either CLI: "Muse structured routing is not enabled until tool isolation is verified" (`muse.cjs`) and "Antigravity is worker-only until tool-free routing is verified" (`antigravity.cjs`). That throw is what a Hub set to `muse-spark-1.3-contributor` hit on every turn. The check was never done.
- **Now.** The flag, both throws and the eight `structured` checks are gone: the Hub menu (`presetGroups`), `coordinatorChoice` and Hub Auto, `renameHub`, the chat checker, reflection and reply suggestions. Muse and Antigravity models are `router: true`.
- **The rule they share with every provider.** A Hub or routing reply is tool-free.
  - Each adapter adds "Return only a JSON object matching this schema. No tools are available." and the schema, unless a resumed conversation already has it.
  - A tool call during the step rejects the reply as `INVALID_STRUCTURED_RESPONSE`, which gets the router's one retry (Cursor `cursor.cjs`, Muse `item()`, Antigravity tool `step_update`).
  - Muse and DeepSeek run the step read-only. Antigravity starts it without `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, so a tool it reaches for asks instead of running, and the adapter expects init `permission_mode: "request-review"` (worker turns: `always-proceed`, Full access only). agy's `--mode plan` is no option: agy 2026-10-31 warns it "has no effect while slash command expansion is disabled".
- **Tests.** "Muse answers a Hub or routing reply…" (`tests/muse.test.cjs`; the fixture's new `toolCall` option), "Antigravity answers a Hub or routing reply…" (`tests/antigravity.test.cjs`; the fixture now reports the permission mode its flags give), "the Hub runs on any model…" (`tests/coordinator.test.cjs`), "…a Muse chat gets one like any other" (`tests/reply-suggestions.test.cjs`), and the UI smoke "Hub model menu offers every model…". Pool entry `hub-on-any-model`.
- **Live.** Antigravity, 2026-10-31, the real `AntigravityCLI.run` as `smart-router.cjs` calls it, with a Hub-shaped schema: the first call (22.2 s) returned `{"reply":"Checking which bots are running.","action":{"tool":"agents_list","arguments":{}}}`, the resumed one (9.2 s, same conversation) `{"reply":"ok","action":null}`, both passing `structuredResponse`. A schema call told to read `README.md` with its file tool was rejected as `INVALID_STRUCTURED_RESPONSE` after 13.2 s (a `tool` step, no hang, no unknown event; the file unchanged). Known limit shared with DeepSeek and Muse: one adapter instance per controller runs one turn at a time, so the same native model as both Hub and chat checker can refuse the second call ("already has an active process"). Muse cannot be run on this account (Meta billing 402, and the user won't subscribe, `docs/implementation-plan.md`).
- **Pitfall still true.** A model's `router` flag is not a capability: legacy Codex presets and API models carry `router: false` and answer structured replies.

Verified 2026-10-10: `agent_work.count` creates one to ten independent workers for an explicitly requested batch, preserving the same task, requested model/effort, workspace, images and access (`src/controller/coordinator.cjs`). Counts and queue capacity are checked before creation; partial failures report the actual number dispatched. `Agents.checkDelegationCapacity` shares the ten-task Hub budget and global pending limit across single and batch calls; named agents retain their three-task limit. Each batch member is a separate assignment; current execution uses the two background slots described above. The four-worker fixture runs in two waves. Workspace isolation remains pending (`src/agents.cjs`).

Each batch assignment carries its group id, count and index alongside the original request. Workers are told to perform one full instance, leaving peer creation to Hub. Completion checks assess that assignment; result-message metadata persists each verified outcome in visible history, with bounded follow-ups replacing only the relevant worker result. Hub reports progress until every dispatched member returns, then supplies all reports to the final synthesis. Cancellation, exhausted budgets and failed/undispatched members prevent a full-completion claim. The original message stores dispatch progress so partial creation failures cannot leave Hub waiting for nonexistent workers. `tests/coordinator.test.cjs` covers four-worker dispatch at an exact model/effort, persistence, aggregation, capacity rejection, partial failures, cancellation and bounded continuations (pool `coordinator`). These tests use isolated provider fixtures, not paid model calls.

Verified 2026-10-10. The permanent sidebar agent is named **Hub**; its internal id remains `main`, so existing conversations, references and deletion protection survive the rename. `Controller.send` routes Hub through `src/controller/coordinator.cjs`. Hub answers chat and planning with a pinned model; a manual model selection takes precedence, while Auto chooses an available chat model once and stores `hubMode`. Hub Auto calls `SmartRouter.chooseHub` once, using the selected Jev or Smart routing mode. Both receive the same coordination policy and available benchmark evidence/model descriptions: select a balanced mid-tier model relative to the enabled catalog, with Low preferred when adequate. There is no model-name/family allowlist. Before either router chooses, `chooseHub` groups comparable general-index measurements by model (not provider or effort row), using each model's best observed eligible-effort result (Low, Medium or provider default) only to determine its tier. With at least three measured models, it excludes the bottom/top quarters (rounded down, at least one at each end); boundary ties are kept. Individual offered rows retain their actual effort-specific measurements. Unknown measurements remain eligible, and fewer than three measured models keep all options. A live test caught Jev choosing a measured top-tier model at Low effort, so this catalog boundary is enforced in code, not only in a prompt. Models with configurable effort offer only explicitly supported `low`/`medium` choices; models with no configurable effort remain eligible at provider default. `hubEffortAllowed` in `src/routing/effort-cap.cjs` applies the same rule to candidate filtering and pin reuse. Disabled and rate-limited candidates are excluded. A sole eligible choice is selected directly without a classifier call, so LM Studio alone does not need Jev or a cloud router. Selection bypasses worker task continuity, workspace inspection and uncertainty escalation. Jev failure falls back to an available Smart router with the same policy; Stop never retries. The selected id and `hubModeSource: auto` are persisted, so subsequent chat and compaction reuse the choice. New route records distinguish Hub Auto from manual selection, including reused pins; the transcript omits the redundant Model/Auto-selected/manual-selection paragraphs (updated 2026-10-20), while reply provider/model badges remain visible. Previously saved route labels remain unchanged because their original selection source cannot be recovered reliably. Older pins without that marker are reselected once. Manual overrides are retained; returning to Auto reruns selection. A successful Hub choice also persists the effective composer mode. Worker-result continuations use the Hub pin and its Auto/manual origin rather than the queued composer mode, so old results carrying a removed local-model id can be resumed safely. Higher configurable efforts require a manual choice. Provider-default choices retain their null/absent effort through inference and use the existing local-model preparation/context path. With no eligible effort, Hub stops before inference with an actionable error. Selection runs inside the busy/cancellation lifecycle, and a late response after Stop cannot save a model or begin chat. Worker catalogs and routing remain unchanged. An Auto pin is reused only while it remains available, enabled and outside known usage limits. If its provider is disconnected, the model is removed/disabled or a usage limit becomes active, the next chat, worker-result continuation or compaction reselects through the same mid-tier effort policy across the currently eligible providers. The replacement is validated again before saving and becomes the new Auto pin; the conversation and summaries are retained. If no eligible model exists, selection fails without replacing the stored pin or starting inference. Explicit manual choices still report unavailability rather than silently changing models. Offline regressions in `tests/coordinator.test.cjs` cover switching from a cloud pin to LM Studio alone, retaining history and the default-effort pin, both routers selecting fixed-effort models, and local inference preparation without an invented reasoning level. Provider-switch regressions in tests/coordinator.test.cjs cover Claude to OpenAI, removed/rate-limited pins, persistence, retained history and manual-choice protection (verified 2026-10-10).

Execution is delegated through the existing agent manager. Hub's `agent_work` action reuses a selected worker or creates one, defaulting to Auto and inheriting current access. The optional workspace is validated as an existing absolute path and must match the requested repository; other workspaces require Full access before any agent is created. A reused agent in the wrong workspace is left unchanged and a worker is created in the target workspace instead. When a manually pinned candidate is replaced by Auto, its workspace is retained unless the task specifies another one. This prevents routing a PhasmaEngine review using evidence from Hub’s unrelated benchmark-refresh folder. Explicit requested model/effort still uses the existing validated catalog. Hub exposes agent_work as its only dispatch action; the generic agent_delegate remains available to worker agents. Automatic dispatch reuses Auto workers; a manually pinned candidate is left unchanged and a new Auto worker is created instead. An explicitly requested existing agent is selected by passing its exact roster mode. Hub records hubWorkerMode so follow-ups retain an explicit choice; old hubWorker references alone cannot authorize reuse of a manual pin. The worker runs normal Jev/Smart routing over all enabled available providers, including Claude, and retains the normal worker effort cap. Attachments travel with the delegated task; the full original conversation remains accessible through bounded `agent_read_context`. Follow-ups can reuse the previous worker. Actual dispatch produces a local acknowledgement naming the recipient; worker results return through the existing durable delivery queue. Stop, deletion, access ceilings, pending-work guards and circular-task limits remain enforced.

Hub uses the existing isolated structured-inference runner (`SmartRouter.complete` in `src/routing/smart-router.cjs`) for Codex/API, Claude and Cursor. It receives only an application-validated list of agent actions; its inference has no worker MCP, shell, browsing or workspace execution. Calls use the router's isolated directory, with tool denial and read-only configuration. Hub can chat and manage agents while their independent turns run, including overlapping workspaces and Full access. A turn makes at most six management decisions and ends immediately after single or batch dispatch. Cancellation is checked again after inference and before dispatch, so a late result cannot create work.

Creating several agents (verified 2026-10-19; the confirmation removed 2026-10-21).
- **Before:** "create 10 agents" took ten single `agent_create` actions. That hit the six-decision limit after six agents, with a misleading "action limit" error.
- **Now:** Hub's `agent_create` also takes `names` (two to ten).
  - Since 2026-10-21 the request is the approval (the user's rule: no approvals unless needed): `createAgents` creates the whole list at once and replies with a code-built, accurate result: "Created N agents: …" or "Created X of N agents: … Not created: name (reason)". The earlier "Create N agents: …? Reply yes" step and `session.pendingAgents` are gone.
  - A single `name` creates at once too.
- **Schema limit:** `structured.cjs` validates Hub's response schema and rejects `minItems`/`maxItems`, so the 2–10 bound lives in code.
- **Evidence:**
  - A live run with Codex `gpt-6.1-sol` low proposed ten invented names, then created all ten on "yes" (`.scratch/hub-create-live.cjs`, throwaway profile).
  - Regression in `tests/coordinator.test.cjs` ("Hub asks once before creating several agents"). It also runs Hub's real schema through `structuredResponse`, and fails on the `minItems` schema that broke live.
- **Sources:** `src/controller/coordinator.cjs` (`HUB_CREATE`, `createAgents`, `sendCoordinator`, `actionArguments`).

Hub responses contain `reply` and either `action: null` or a typed `{tool, arguments}` object. The nested action union is derived from the existing tool schemas, binding each tool to its argument fields; arguments are never JSON encoded inside a string. This removes the second parse that rejected Windows paths even when the outer response passed schema enforcement. Optional arguments are nullable for strict providers and nulls are removed before dispatch, preserving Auto model selection and inherited access. Runtime checks reject unknown actions/fields, missing required values, invalid types, bounds and enums before side effects. Verified 2026-10-10 against `src/controller/coordinator.cjs` and `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`: regressions preserve Windows/UNC paths, quoted pasted text and Unicode across real local handoffs, and reject legacy or malformed action strings. An isolated live Gemma run passed greeting, idle-agent creation, Windows-path review delegation, simulated worker-result return, compaction and requested deletion. Worker execution was simulated; real agents, conversations and PE files were not used.

Hub builds context from visible exchanges and stored summaries, keeping tool logs in workers. Older history is summarized in bounded chunks when its text reaches 24,000 characters; the latest exchanges remain verbatim, with long messages linked back to the retained original. `/compact` summarizes all remaining exchanges without deleting the visible history. `/clear` retains its existing confirmation/archive behavior. Summaries are saved only after successful responses; failure never removes original messages. Model usage is reported when supplied by the provider; no context-window percentage is invented. Tests: `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`, `tests/routing-calls.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`; test pool `coordinator`. Native worker bridge/lease tests in `tests/agents.test.cjs` explicitly use execution-capable controller fixtures; Hub's separate runtime is exercised by the coordinator tests.

Verified 2026-10-10: Hub now snapshots the original user message id and exact text into `session.delegation.originalRequest` (`src/controller/coordinator.cjs`, `src/agents.cjs`). Hub's generated task is supplementary guidance, not a replacement. The worker receives the original request before those notes, with review-specific instructions to inspect actual changes, include untracked files and report concrete evidence and coverage. Long requests include a marked excerpt and a reference to retrieve the retained full message. The original text drives every worker routing path and the saved task goal (`src/controller.cjs`). A new request clears previous task-model retention; automatic follow-ups keep the original request id and its selected worker (`src/controller/turns.cjs`). The result queue carries the same snapshot and worker identity, so intervening Hub chat or compaction cannot substitute a different request.

Worker returns undergo a separate structured completion assessment against the original request before Hub writes its reply. A completed turn or task marker does not establish completion. For reviews, summaries without code inspection and findings/coverage fail the assessment. Missing work returns to the same worker, preserving original scope, images, access and configured model; each worker assignment is limited to three dispatches including follow-ups, and Hub can send at most ten tasks per request. Invalid assessments fail closed, cancellation cannot restart work, and failed workers are reported as unconfirmed rather than retried automatically. Original requests or reports exceeding the 16,000-character assessment window are explicitly unverified, without retrying execution to resolve a context limit. This is a model-based check of reported evidence, not independent proof that every file was inspected (`src/controller/coordinator.cjs`, `src/agents.cjs`).

Validation: `tests/coordinator.test.cjs` covers misleading Hub notes, saved snapshots, unrelated later chat, fresh/reused worker task identity, images, incomplete reviews, bounded continuations, cancellation, invalid assessments, oversized requests/reports and failed workers. `tests/routing-calls.test.cjs` rejects stale `sameTask` flags when no current job is eligible for retention. Both are registered in the `coordinator` and `worker-routing` test-pool entries. An isolated live local check on 2026-10-10 replayed the original PE review request and the actual worker report admitting it was not a code review. With the enabled cross-provider catalog, routing selected Opus 5.5 at High effort; the actual Gemma Hub model rejected that report as incomplete. These are observed choices, not model constants. This check used only local inference, did not execute an Opus worker, and did not repeat the full PE review.

Ownership handoffs at restart (verified 2026-10-19): `Agents` re-commits the last owner handoff of every undelivered job while it is constructed. If the new owner's chat or its receipt was missing, that threw and the runner failed on every launch. The reconcile is now guarded: the job is held for review (`interrupted`) and startup continues. `Controller.deleteSession` also refuses to delete a chat that is the source or current owner of any undelivered job, which closes the way that state was reached. Sources: `src/agents.cjs` (constructor), `src/controller.cjs` (`deleteSession`); regression `tests/agents.test.cjs` ("a lost owner chat holds it for review").

## Worker difficulty and model selection

Verified 2026-10-10 against `src/routing/smart-router.cjs`, `src/routing/benchmarks.cjs`, `src/providers/jev.cjs`, `src/controller/turns.cjs` and `tests/routing-calls.test.cjs`. Both Jev and Smart explicitly classify reasoning difficulty as Low, Medium, High or Xhigh, independently of risk, uncertainty and choice confidence. Shared criteria distinguish simple retrieval, bounded feature explanations, difficult interacting implementation/debugging, and broad investigations spanning multiple difficult concerns. Missing source evidence alone must not inflate difficulty. Both classifier boundaries reject missing or invalid difficulty; the assessment is retained in routes and decision traces.

**Sizing only, code picks (2026-10-25).** Neither router picks the worker any more. Jev's questions are `QUESTIONS` in `src/providers/jev.cjs`, with no `preset`. Quick answers (a `direct` and a `quickAnswer` question) were removed on 2026-10-29. Smart's `DECISION_SCHEMA` has no `preset`, and its instructions carry no worker catalog.

`finalChoice` first runs `pickPreset(decision, session, available)`:
- High/Xhigh: `strongest()` at matching effort.
- Low/Medium: the chat's current model (last non-direct route) when adequate.
- Otherwise: the adequate effort-matched worker with the lowest aa-index `taskCostUsd`. Adequate means `ADEQUATE` = 0.6 (low) or 0.75 (medium) of the best effort-matched score.

Further rules:
- Unscored workers are not ranked; unknown cost sorts after known cost.
- **Model for simple messages (2026-10-26, at the user's request).** `settings.autoLowModel` is `'auto'` (the default; was `'off'` until the user renamed it the same day, never released) or any worker preset id. Only read when a message is routed on Auto (`chosenMode === 'auto'` in `Controller.send`); a hand-picked model never reads it.
  - Shown in Settings → Routing as two pickers side by side (`.model-effort-field`), like the Edit dialogs:
    - `#settings-low-model`: Auto plus the available models grouped by provider (`presetGroups`/`groupedOptions`).
    - `#settings-low-effort`: that model's efforts (`showDialogEfforts`).
    - `lowModelChoice` saves one preset id; a newly picked model starts at its lowest effort.
    - `saveSetting` re-renders both after saving, because they hold a group key and a level rather than the id.
    - A hint below suggests a cheap model at low effort (user's request, 2026-10-26). Validated in `Controller.settings`; a local key in `Agents.settings`, so changing it never waits for bots to stop.
  - It reaches `pickPreset` through the routing context in `Controller.send` and the Jev comparison context. For low difficulty it wins while it is in the offered catalog (effort cap, usage limits, enabled), even over the chat's current model. `userPick` skips `provenChoice`; a continued task still keeps its worker.
  - Why: API-key and native models often have no AA score, so Harness cannot rank them. The user asked for the select itself to be the switch, rather than a default chosen for them.
  - Tests: picker cases in `tests/routing-calls.test.cjs`, the proven-setup case in `tests/setups.test.cjs`, validation in `tests/core.test.cjs` (chat checker test), and the UI smoke step that picks a model and then Off (screenshot `.scratch/low-model-setting.png`).
- An effort with no score of its own counts at its model's best score at a lower offered effort (a lower bound), with unknown cost.
  - Found 2026-10-26: checking the catalog without Claude and Codex, Grok 4.7 medium has no AA run, so medium work fell to the "no measured scores" fallback and went to Antigravity Gemini Flash high.
  - Now that catalog gives Grok low/medium/high/xhigh, and Claude only gives Sonnet low/medium and Opus high/xhigh.
  - With only unmeasured models (Antigravity, DeepSeek, LM Studio), the first offered runs. Antigravity puts the level in the model id (`gemini-3.8-flash-high`, effort null), so low work can get Flash high.
- With nothing scored, the current or first offered model runs.
- `difficultyChoice` is gone. `escalatedFrom` came back on 2026-10-29 with a different meaning: it marks a cascade repair's route (see Cascade repair and the chat checker's judge), not a pick made before dispatch. `pickPreset` skips those routes when it keeps "this chat's model", so a repair by a strong model does not become the model for the chat's next low or medium task.
- `routerPick` now means Harness's own pick when a kept task or proven setup overrides it.

Calibration: `.scratch/pick-replay.cjs` replays the 110 logged Auto picks against the live catalog. With gpt-6.1-sol offered, low picks it at any share from 0.5 to 0.9, as Jev did on 16 of 28 low turns. On the 2026-10-25 catalog (no Sol), medium picks Sonnet 5.5 medium and low picks gpt-5.6-terra low. Behaviour change: Haiku 5.5 has no AA cost and Antigravity Gemini, DeepSeek, LM Studio Gemma and Grok medium have no AA score, so they are no longer picked on Low/Medium while a measured, costed worker is adequate. In the decision log that changes 7 fresh low/medium Jev picks; of their 18 other turns, 14 were same-task continuations (which keep their worker as before) and 4 predate logged difficulty.

Measured 2026-10-25 (`.scratch/jev-route-probe.cjs`, real `choose()`, three messages × 2):
- one-call route: 1,814 input tokens, $0.000076, 219-449 ms (was 12,182 tokens and $0.000512);
- two-call route with workspace scan: 3,706 tokens, 0.9-1.1 s, including a 0.45-0.75 s scan.
- the above ran with quick answers off (removed 2026-10-29). With them on (`jev-route-probe.cjs quick`, one run each):
  - "Does the text mention rain? Text: Rain is expected tomorrow." got Jev's direct "Yes." in 375 ms (2,179 tokens, $0.000092).
  - "Rename the variable count to total in src/a.cjs and run the tests." got no direct answer and went to gpt-5.6-terra low (the saved answers are the second call's, so the first call's `direct` choice is not recorded): two calls, 5,350 tokens, 1,576 ms including a 1,168 ms scan.
- Smart router on Cursor grok-4.7 xhigh (`.scratch/route-timing-probe.cjs xhigh`, the ATH "which Python command" message, two calls): the prompts took 35.4 s and 43.6 s, and the route 96.1 s, against 103.8 s, 113.3 s and 230.5 s with the catalog. The pick, Sonnet 5.5 low, was the same.

Tests: the picker test and the reworked difficulty tests in `tests/routing-calls.test.cjs`.

**Jev sees attached images (2026-10-30).** Jev 1.13+ reads PNG/JPEG images. TypeSafe calls this beta ("Dreamer-ready" in docs.typesafe.ai/images.md). Each request opts in with the `TypeSafe-Danger-Zone-Images: true` header.
- **Path.** `Controller.send` and `compareWithJev` put `attachedImages` in the routing context. `JevClient.classify(context, signal, images)` sends them as `assets`, each under a `randomUUID()` reference listed in `state.attachments.images`. The Smart router still gets only the count and its "Images go to the worker" note.
- **Sizes.** Jev rejects any side outside 280-1568 px; it does not resize (live: "Invalid image dimensions." for 1920×1080). `jevImage` in `src/image-input.cjs` passes an image within limits through as is; otherwise it shrinks, grows toward the minimum, pads, and encodes the copy as JPEG q85. Jev bills by pixels, so JPEG costs nothing in quality terms; measured 2026-10-30 on a 1920×1080 PNG screenshot: 33 ms and 237 KB, against 61 ms and 413 KB as PNG.
  - It decodes in the runner, outside the document worker's memory cap, so it skips any image over 2048 px (larger than `normalizeImage` makes). A skipped image stays in `imageCount`.
  - Each message's images are fitted once: a `WeakMap` keyed on the array is shared by both routing passes and the comparison.
- **Fallback.** A 400 or 413 on a request with images is retried once with text only. The reason then ends "Jev could not read the attached images, so it judged the text alone."
- **Live, 2026-10-30, jev-1.14.0.** Probe: Electron, a temp copy of `Local State` and `jev-key.enc`. A 1920×1080 red screenshot with an error line, routed with "what is wrong in this screenshot?":
  - 200 in 337 ms, 5,417 input tokens ($0.00023), against 1,805 tokens for the text alone;
  - uncertainty low with the image, high without it;
  - a `noul` question "The image is mostly red" scored 0.98.
- **Tests.** "Jev routing sees attached images fitted to its 280-1568 px limits…" in `tests/routing-calls.test.cjs`; pool entry `jev-images`.
- **Setting.** Routing sends images only while Jev image assist is on (next paragraph).

**Jev image assist (2026-10-30/31, the user's design: "all images go through Jev first, model should avoid reading for token savings, unless it needs to read it (not a yes/no question)").**
- **What it does.** While it is on, no model is sent the images of its chat. Every model, the Hub included, gets their numbers. It asks Jev yes/no questions (`image_ask`) and accepts the answers Jev is sure of. It reads an image itself (`image_read`) only when Jev is unsure or what it needs is not a yes/no answer.
  - A model that cannot take images works the same way, without `image_read`.
  - Before this, such a model failed with "Enable image support for this model…" (workers) or "Choose an image-capable model for Hub before sending images." (Hub; the user saw it).
- **Setting.** `jevImages`, Settings → Routing → "Jev image assist", per bot like the other Routing settings.
  - On unless turned off, while a Jev key is saved and Jev is enabled (`Controller.jevImageAssist`). Off and locked without a key.
  - Off: models that can see images get them inline again, the two old errors come back, and Jev routing gets no images.
- **Tools.** `src/tools/image-tools.cjs` defines `image_list`, `image_ask` and `image_read`, appended to `agent-tools.cjs` `TOOLS`. That one list reaches the Hub (`HUB_TOOLS`), Codex and CLIs (the `phasma_harness` MCP: `SHARED_TOOLS`, `MCP_TOOLS`, Claude's allowed list) and API workers (the bridge tools). All run through `Agents.tool`.
  - Image N is the Nth image part of the chat's user messages, oldest first. An unavailable image keeps its number (`chatImages`).
  - `image_ask` takes `image` (default: the latest) and 1-8 `questions`. It sends one Jev request with a `noul` question each, the image under a `randomUUID()` reference.
    - Answers: probability ≥ 0.9 is yes, ≤ 0.1 is no, anything between is unsure, with a note to use `image_read`.
    - Each image is fitted once by `jevImage` (cache of 16, keyed by message id and index).
  - `image_read` returns MCP content `[image, text "Image N"]` with the original image, without Jev. It refuses when the turn's route has `images: false`.
    - The image comes first because Codex's code mode forwards an MCP image as `image(result.content[0])`.
- **How `image_read` reaches each model.**
  - **MCP (Codex, Claude, Cursor, native CLIs):** `router-mcp.cjs` passes `image_read`'s content through, as it does `connector_call`'s.
  - **API workers:** a tool message carries only text, so `ApiAgent.tool` returns the text, and `step` adds a user message with the images after the tool results.
  - **Hub:** its tool results are JSON text, so the read image joins the images of its next planning call and the result says "Image N is attached to this request." An image tool's error is a result, not a failed Hub turn (`coordinatorTool`).
- **The text a model gets.** `Controller.forModel(session, route, text, images)` returns the text plus a note, and no images; the chat keeps the images.
  - A model that can see images: "[Image N is attached but not shown, to save tokens. Ask Jev yes/no questions about it with image_ask (image: N); read one with image_read only when Jev is unsure or you need more than a yes/no answer.]"
  - A model that cannot: "[Image N is attached, but you cannot see images. Ask Jev …]".
  - Applied at `submitWorker` (sends, corrections, delegations), at live input and Codex `turn/steer` (against the turn's route), and for the Hub (`images: []` and `context.attachedImages`; its workers get the images and apply their own assist).
- **Visible steps (2026-10-31, the user: "show to the user what is going on exactly", reported "in the same way other calls are reported").** `Controller.recordStep` saves a `toolStep` chat item `{ title, lines }`. It shows between the message and the reply as the usual folded activity row (the same `details.activity` as "Ran · command"), and is exported as "## Step".
  - Image steps are recorded once at `Agents.tool`, for the Hub and every bot (`imageStep`):
    - "Jev reads image 1", opening to "Is there an error message visible…? → Jev: yes (0.99)" and "Asked by Grok 4.7 · Extra High · 238 ms".
    - "claude-sonnet-5-5 · low reads image 1".
    - "… lists the chat's images".
    - "… · image_read failed", with the error.
  - The Hub's other tool calls ("Grok 4.7 · Extra High · agents_list", opening to the result) are recorded in its planning loop. Delegations and comparisons keep their cards.
  - A worker's MCP call also keeps its existing `mcpToolCall` row.
- **Cursor models see images (2026-10-31, at the user's word).** Grok 4.7 takes images, and the Cursor adapter sends them when the CLI advertises `promptCapabilities.image`; otherwise it refuses with "Cursor CLI did not advertise image support."
  - Harness enabled a discovered Cursor model with `images: value.images === true` and had no switch for it, so every saved Cursor entry held `false`. With assist on, Grok got "you cannot see images" and its `image_read` was refused; with assist off the Hub failed with "Choose an image-capable model for Hub".
  - Cursor's model list says nothing per model, so `providerWorkers()` now gives every Cursor worker `images: true`, at each reasoning level. Saved entries are left as they were.
  - Test: "every Cursor model counts as able to see images…" (`tests/core.test.cjs`).
- **Hub history names models and steps (2026-10-31, from the live demo).** After the demo switched the Hub from Grok to Sonnet, Sonnet said "My earlier 'Yes' wasn't based on reading the image". The history listed Grok's reply as plain `assistant` and left out Jev's answer.
  - `messages()` now gives each Hub reply `model` (its `routeLabel`) and includes `toolStep` entries (`role: 'step'`). The Hub instructions say the Hub may change models and must not take an earlier model's reply for its own.
- **Hub tools are actions, not callable tools (2026-10-31, from the live demo).** Sonnet's transcript (`~/.claude/projects/…router-workspace/*.jsonl`) showed `tool_use image_read` → "No such tool available: image_read". It then gave up in its structured reply, without ever returning the action.
  - The instructions now say every tool, `image_ask` and `image_read` included, runs only as `action={tool, arguments}`, and that an `image_read` image comes with the next request.
- **History names earlier images.** The Hub's history (`messages(view, imageNumbers(session))`) and a CLI's cross-backend handoff (`handoff(items, max, imageNumbers(session))`) add "[Image N attached]", so a later turn can ask about an earlier image.
