Without assist, the handoff keeps "[Earlier image attachment]".
- **Pitfall, found live 2026-10-31.** The Hub's reply schema is built from every tool's `inputSchema` (`SCHEMA` in `coordinator.cjs`), and `structured.cjs` throws "Unsupported structured response schema." for any word outside its list.
  - `minItems`/`maxItems` on `image_ask` failed the user's Grok 4.7 Hub when it called `image_ask` (it sent the strict form `image: null`).
  - The validator walks only the branches a reply uses, so a fixture without `image` never reached `questions`.
  - Fixed by dropping both words (`imageTool` checks 1-8). The coordinator tests' fake model now runs action replies through `structuredResponse` and fails on unsupported words. Its image fixtures use the strict shape.
  - Strict schemas send omitted optional arguments as `null`; `imageTool` treats `image: null` as the latest image.
- **Live, 2026-10-30, jev-1.14.0.** One `image_ask` with five questions about a 1920×1080 red screenshot showing "ERROR: shader compile failed" took 488 ms including fitting. "Is there an error message?", "Is the background red?" and "Does the text mention a shader?" came back yes at 0.99; "Is the background blue?" and "Is there a cat in the picture?" came back no at 0.01.
- **Codex, verified 2026-10-31 with the installed Codex and a local Responses mock.** Code mode lists only some MCP tools up front; `image_read` is reached through `ALL_TOOLS`. `const r = await tools.mcp__phasma_harness__image_read({}); image(r.content[0])` put the PNG in the next request's `custom_tool_call_output` as `input_image`. Now checked by `tests/agent-tools-smoke.cjs`.
  - Claude, Cursor, DeepSeek and Muse get the same MCP image content (tested at the MCP layer). They were not run end to end with an image.
- **Speed and cost.** No Jev call is added to a send. A question is one Jev request, about 0.3-0.5 s, plus the model's tool step. An `image_read` costs another model step and the image tokens, which a direct send would have spent anyway.
- **Tests.**
  - `tests/image-tools.test.cjs`: the tools, plus the real bridge and MCP child passing `image_read` as image content.
  - "with Jev image assist no model is sent images…" (`tests/core.test.cjs`).
  - "with Jev image assist the Hub gets image numbers…" (`tests/coordinator.test.cjs`).
  - "an image a Harness tool returns reaches the API model…" (`tests/api-agent.test.cjs`).
  - `tests/agent-tools-smoke.cjs` (real Codex) and the UI smoke's Jev image assist row.
  - Pool entries `jev-image-assist` and `agent-tools-smoke`.

Earlier design, kept for history: Low/Medium kept the router's model choice and matched its effort, High/Xhigh escalated to the strongest measured model with no model-family allowlist, and with no comparable scores the router's model was retained while effort still adapted. Effort selection is unchanged: the lowest offered level meeting the difficulty, or the highest offered level if capped. Models with fixed/default effort remain supported. Confidence alone neither promotes a bounded explanation nor lets a difficult task stay at Medium. Existing task continuity and Hub's separate mid-tier policy remain intact. Offline tests cover both routers, cross-provider selection, low confidence on bounded work, high confidence on hard work, caps, absent candidates, unknown benchmarks, fixed-effort models and invalid classifications. The authorized live prompt-and-catalog-only check classified the four examples Low/Medium/High/Xhigh and selected Sonnet 5.5 Low, Sonnet 5.5 Medium, Opus 5.5 High and Opus 5.5 Xhigh respectively; these are observed catalog-dependent outcomes, not routing constants. No source excerpts or tasks were executed in that live check.

`DIFFICULTY_POLICY` and `TASK_KINDS` in `src/routing/benchmarks.cjs` are the shared Smart/Jev contract. First assess the requested deliverable and scope: explaining an existing flow is Medium; investigating one difficult failure mode across components is High; a broad correctness review coupling several distinct hard concerns is Xhigh. Task kind is descriptive and never overrides assessed difficulty. The former `audit => xhigh` override was removed because it amplified classification mistakes. Unrelated dirty files, read-only permissions, confidence and missing source excerpts do not change the requested scope. Model selection and effort caps apply once, in `SmartRouter.finalChoice` (`pickPreset`, 2026-10-25). Hub selection uses only the common catalog evidence guidance plus its coordination policy, without inheriting worker difficulty rules. Verified 2026-10-10; offline regressions and pool entry `worker-routing` cover these boundaries with arbitrary model names.

The four fixed difficulty prompts are in `tests/fixtures/routing-difficulty.json`. The opt-in `tests/routing-decisions-live.cjs` evaluator accepts explicit `--router=<catalog-id>` arguments (discover IDs with `--list`; `--router=jev` selects configured/enabled Jev), uses a frozen enabled catalog capped at Xhigh and production deadlines, and records raw and applied choices, retries, errors and expected difficulty/effort matches. It sends prompts/catalog only, reuses the classification when no new workspace evidence exists, and starts no workers. It is excluded from unattended tests; running it consumes model usage and requires explicit authorization. Its entry guard recognizes Electron's script argument because `require.main` alone did not start the evaluator on Windows; an offline regression covers that launch path. An authorized repeat on 2026-10-10 used the exact previous prompts, 33-preset catalog and benchmark snapshot: Sol 6.1 Medium, Sonnet 5.5 Medium and Grok 4.7 Medium each matched all four difficulty/effort targets; Gemma at provider default matched three, still overclassifying the Medium flow explanation as High. All 16 returned valid output on the first attempt. Sonnet's High raw Astra selection was adjusted to Opus High by the measured-capability rule; the other raw selections were unchanged. This is one sample per prompt/router, not evidence of general reliability. Detailed local artifact: `.scratch/router-matrix-FPY3JU/results.json` (ignored, not distributed).

`SmartRouter.complete` validates every provider's whole JSON response against the supplied schema using `src/providers/structured.cjs`. It accepts plain JSON or a whole JSON fence, never extracts actions from prose or repairs malformed escapes. Malformed JSON/schema gets at most one fresh format retry with the original input; no rejected response can dispatch an action. Attempts share one cancellation/deadline budget and usage is summed. Quota, transport errors, incomplete local responses and Stop are not retried. The default structured-call deadline is 180 seconds (Hub calls with explicit 120-second limits retain those limits), covering startup and both attempts. Persistent invalid output returns a controlled error without model text. `tests/structured-inference.test.cjs` covers CLI, local and Codex paths, schema validation, usage, limits, deadlines and Stop. This improves failure handling, not a guarantee that a model always classifies correctly.

## Provider connection progress

Verified 2026-10-18. The header shows a small Connecting indicator (hover for provider names), and Settings > Providers shows progress beside the affected provider. Startup CLI checks, sign-in, API model discovery and Jev's connection test use transient progress; overlapping requests keep the indicator until all finish. ChatGPT remains pending while browser sign-in awaits its completion notification, including the final account refresh, and clears on failure or disconnection. Progress is not saved to the session store. The renderer combines all agents' pending providers, prevents duplicate plug clicks and respects reduced-motion preferences. Enabling an API provider only changes configuration; the indicator appears when a request actually runs. Local model loading remains separate. Sources: `src/controller.cjs`, `src/controller/providers.cjs`, `src/controller/turns.cjs`, `src/main.cjs`, `ui/app.js`; regressions: `tests/core.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`, pool entries `provider-connections` and `agents-ui`.

Parallel startup (verified 2026-10-19):
- **Before:** `Controller.initialize` checked Claude, Cursor and every enabled native CLI one after another, then connected Codex. Codex took about 17 s to arrive (Antigravity's check alone is about 11 s). Meanwhile Settings showed API providers as "Needs Codex" and an enabled, installed Antigravity as "not installed" (its default status before the check).
- **Now:** every native CLI gets a file check first, and the slow checks run in parallel. Codex connects right after the Claude check, which the legacy-router migration still reads first.
- **Labels:** CLI providers are labelled "Claude CLI", "Cursor CLI", "Muse Code CLI", "Antigravity CLI", "DeepSeek Harness CLI" and "Codex CLI" in the UI (the Codex row shows the ChatGPT plan and email as its detail; the model-group title is "Codex CLI / ChatGPT"). Usage-limit messages keep the account names ("Claude", "ChatGPT (Codex)").
- **Regression:** "provider connecting status follows parallel startup checks" in `tests/core.test.cjs`.

## Lessons from failed checks

Built 2026-10-19 (idea from google-research/harness2's experience reflector; fixtures and gate tests, no live run yet):
- **When:** `saveLesson` in `src/controller/task-gate.cjs` runs when a tracked task settles, before wiki upkeep snapshots the wiki. It saves only with check evidence: checks failed and a correction fixed them (`checks-passed` with corrections), or they still fail after one (`needs-you`, "checks failed after one correction"). Never for blocked, cancelled, worker failed, not-checked or router-skipped checks.
- **What (`src/lessons.cjs`, no model call):** task id, date, goal, failing check name and exit code, a redacted excerpt (`redact` from `src/log.cjs`, 400 characters), the outcome, and on a fix the files whose `git diff --stat` line changed between the failing and passing attempts (the stat is cumulative). The same check failing with the same first error line updates its record (`seen`); since 2026-10-21 digit runs are ignored in that comparison (`shape`, so counts, line numbers and ports do not split a lesson, while `a1.test.js` and `b2.test.js` stay apart). Keys saved before that still find their lesson (`sameLesson`), and records that now share a key merge into the newest, keeping the older one's `suggestedAt`, `recurred` and `reflection` and adding up `seen` (`mergeDuplicates`). At most 50 records per project; a lesson unseen for 90 days expires, except the newest 10, so a project left alone for months keeps its latest ones (`fresh`, applied on read and write).
- **Where:** `lessons.json` plus a generated `lessons.md` in the project's wiki root (`WikiStore`, Harness-managed or the user's chosen folder). Batch copies are keyed to their source project (`lessonWorkspace`).
- **Use:** `openTask` attaches `task.lessonBlock` for a new task (not `[Router]` corrections): up to 3 lessons sharing words with the request or concerning a check that will run again, within 1,500 characters, framed as quoted check output. `withTaskLessons` in `src/controller.cjs` puts the block in front of that task's first worker message on both turn paths (Codex `turn/start`, CLI/API `sendCLI`). The visible message and `workerInstructions` stay unchanged, so worker caches survive.
- **Regressions:** `tests/lessons.test.cjs`, "a corrected check failure becomes a lesson…" in `tests/core.test.cjs`, pool entry `task-lessons`.

Richer records (2026-10-20, harness2 item 2; fixtures only, no model calls). Each new record also has these fields:
- **`situation`:** the failing checks, the first error line, and the failing attempt's setup hash, provider and model (from the setup fingerprint).
- **`change`:**
  - the files whose stat line changed, each with its `git diff --stat` text;
  - `setupChanged`: whether provider, model, effort, access, instructions, skills, tools or checks differ between the failing and final attempts. Lessons are not compared, since only a task's first run carries them; attempts without a setup count as unknown, not changed;
  - `amended`: whether the assignment was amended in between. Attempts now save the amendment count (`runGate`). Older attempts have none, so any amendment on the task counts.
- **`evidence`:** the checks passing and failing after the correction, plus regressions. A regression is a check that passed before and fails after; a check that did not run says nothing.
- **`verdict`** (harness2's four) judges this task's correction, not whether offering the lesson helps later tasks; that needs the two-run comparison (item 3):
  - **hurt:** a regression;
  - **confounded:** the setup or assignment also changed, or a different error remains;
  - **helped:** the checks were fixed;
  - **no observed effect:** the same error repeats.
- **`confidence`:**
  - **high:** helped at least twice (the per-record `verdicts` counts survive merges);
  - **medium:** helped once with a known code change;
  - **low:** helped without a visible change (plain folders have no git diff), and every other verdict.
- **No record** when the failure has neither output nor an exit code (thin evidence). A fix whose stat lines look unchanged is still recorded.
- **Offering:** confounded records are kept on the page but not offered (a guess, as harness2 puts it). Records from before verdicts are still offered and show only their outcome. The offered line ends with "· verdict, confidence".
- **Tests:** four tests in `tests/lessons.test.cjs` cover the verdicts, thin evidence, merges and old records. The gate test "a corrected check failure becomes a lesson…" (`tests/core.test.cjs`) asserts the new fields through the real task gate: per-attempt setup and amendment count, `situation`, verdict helped, and the verdict in the offered line.
- **Live (2026-10-20, `.scratch/lessons-live.cjs`, Codex `gpt-5.6-terra:low`, about 70 s):**
  - A scratch git project's check had a requirement the task did not mention, so the first attempt failed ("greet("") returned "Hello, !"").
  - After one correction it passed. Both attempts had setup `53d85db058aba89b`.
  - `lessons.json` and `lessons.md` were written with verdict helped, and the next task's first input carried the lesson. That task passed first time.
  - The run's only log warning was the script stopping background wiki upkeep at shutdown.
- **Found and fixed:** the fix changed the same single line, so `greet.js`'s `--stat` line ("2 +-") was identical before and after. The lesson said "files changed by the fix: none detected", with low confidence.
  - `captureEvidence` now also saves `evidence.files`: a 16-hex hash of each changed file's own `git diff` (no content kept, private paths skipped, at most 200 files, 4 s timeout).
  - `changedBetween` in `lessons.cjs` compares those hashes and falls back to `--stat` lines for older attempts.
  - Live rerun: `changed: ["greet.js"]` (stat still "2 +-"), medium confidence.
  - Test: "per-file diff hashes catch a fix that keeps the same --stat line".

## Two-run comparison

Built 2026-10-20 (harness2 item 3; design approved by the user that day: instructions now, skills later; one pair by default, up to three on request; 20% threshold; blind pick optional).
- **Start (automatic since 2026-10-21):** only the Hub has the `compare` tool (`COMPARE` in `src/controller/coordinator.cjs`); workers never get it, so a comparison cannot start another. The request is the approval: `compareTool` plans and starts at once, with no confirmation turn. `planComparison` (`src/controller/comparison.cjs`) refuses a project without configured checks, a second running comparison in the same project (one older than a day stops blocking), and too many jobs. Without a named model, `comparisonMode` calls the router once for the request and pins its choice for every run, so the runs never differ in model. Spending is bounded by the existing background usage limit: comparison runs are ordinary delegated jobs and pass through `backgroundQuota` in `pump`.
- **Runs:** `startComparison` computes the lesson block once, plans the pairs (`planRuns`: labels A/B, which one carries the change is random per pair) and dispatches one batch of 2 × pairs workers named "Compare N" from one snapshot, with the pinned mode. Workers get only the task text: the user's compare message names the change, so neither the verbatim request nor the `agent_read_context` pointer is passed on.
- **The change:**
  - `bot`: the changed run's worker instructions get the change appended (`coordinatorTool`).
  - `project`: `prepareCopy` (called from `pump` in `src/agents.cjs` once a copy is ready, before its checks are mapped) writes the snapshot's `PhasmaHarness.md`, or the starter when there is none, into every copy, and the base plus the change into the changed run's copy. `git update-index --skip-worktree` hides the edit from `git diff`, so the diffs shown do not tell the runs apart.
- **Kept identical:** the copy record carries `comparisonId`, and `comparisonOf(session)` gates the task gate while the comparison runs:
  - `openTask` uses the frozen lesson block;
  - `saveLesson` records nothing;
  - `maintainWiki` does not run;
  - `runGate` restores check files from the batch input snapshot after capturing the diff and before the checks run (`restoreCheckFiles`; saved per attempt as `restored`). Only files named in a check command are protected; a test runner's own suite (`npm test`) is not.
- **Results:** a returned run skips the Hub's completion check and follow-ups, which would give one run extra turns. `collectComparison` measures it from the delivered result item (`runResult`): check statuses, corrections, every attempt's setup (`deliver` adds `corrections` and `attempts` for comparison jobs), complete tokens, dollars only when complete, active time. No model call.
- **Verdict per pair (`pairVerdict`):**
  - **confounded:** a run not completed, checks that did not run cleanly, a setup that changed between attempts (lessons ignored, since corrections run without them), first-attempt setups that differ beyond the changed instructions, or a change that did not reach its run;
  - checks decide first: a check lost is **hurt**, a check gained is **helped**, both is **no observed effect**, and both failing the same checks is no observed effect;
  - then fewer corrections is **helped** (more corrections is no observed effect, as designed);
  - then tokens: at least 20% fewer is **cheaper**, not helped, since cost says nothing about quality; anything else is no observed effect. Active time decides only when either run did not report its tokens completely; it is noisier (provider load, network, check duration). Chosen by the user on 2026-10-20 after the live run below.
- **Overall (`overallVerdict`):** a strict majority of the comparable pairs; one pair, a split or any confounded pair is low confidence, two agreeing pairs medium, three high.
- **Chat layout (2026-10-21, after the user's first real run was "a mess": card far above, six per-run items, results in finishing order):** the card lives under Bot's newest comparison message (`comparisonCard` moves it): "Started the comparison… The card below updates as they finish", then "Both runs are back. Which did better? Pick below, or reveal." A run returning before the last only updates the card ("Running · 1 of 2 back"; `quiet` replies are not pushed). Comparison runs' result receipts (`deliver`) and handoffs (`sendCoordinator`) are `internal`, so the chat and its export skip them; the confirming message carries `comparisonId`, so its batch report is hidden in the chat and the activity bar. The card keeps A and B in fixed order and asks "Which run did better? The change is revealed after your pick." The `compare` description says Harness runs the checks itself, so "workers must not run checks" no longer makes the Hub stop to ask.
- **Bot names and cleanup (2026-10-21, after the user saw "Compare 1 3"):** run bots are named "Comparison N · A" / "· B" (with the pair number when there are several pairs, e.g. "· 2A"), from a counter in Harness data (`comparisonCount`), which also titles the card. The old names reused "Compare 1"/"Compare 2", so leftover bots made the name loop add " 2", " 3". `removeComparisonBots` (`src/controller/comparison.cjs`) deletes the idle bots of comparisons that are over (revealed, or no longer in Harness data) after a reveal and once at startup (`Agents.initialize`); a busy bot is kept and logged. `deleteAgent` leaves project copies, so Apply still works; the card hides a removed run's Details.
- **Results (automatic since 2026-10-21; the blind pick was removed at the user's request for no approvals):** the last returned run settles everything in `collectComparison`: `finish` sets the verdict and shows which run had the change, `applyBestRun` applies the run that passed every check with the fewest corrections, then fewest tokens (a full tie goes to the current setup; nothing when no run passed) through `JobWorkspaces.changes`/`apply`, and `adoptRule` appends a project rule whose verdict is helped (not cheaper) to `PhasmaHarness.md`. A conflict applies nothing and the card offers Apply by hand. The card (`renderComparison`, `comparisonOutcome` in `ui/app.js`) shows each with an Undo (`undoComparison`, also on Remote Control): `JobWorkspaces.undo` restores the applied files from the batch snapshot and refuses if one changed since; the rule Undo removes the appended rule. The bots are removed at once; `data.comparisons` keeps the record only while it runs and stays out of the renderer snapshot.
- **Saved:** `comparisons.json` plus a generated `comparisons.md` in the project's wiki root (beside the lessons), written when the comparison finishes, with what was applied and added. Older records are copied into later batch snapshots with the wiki.
- **Tests:** `tests/comparisons.test.cjs` (verdicts, confounders, majority and confidence, check restore, `prepareCopy`, the run that lands, card and page). End to end with fake workers in `tests/coordinator.test.cjs` ("Hub compare starts on request…", both targets): no confirmation turn, the router's model pinned, mapping absent from the snapshot while running, change only in the changed run, frozen lessons in both first inputs, no compare message in worker inputs, a worker's edited check restored and judged by the original, no completion-check calls, then the verdict at once, the better run applied, a project rule kept, and both Undos. UI smoke step "comparison card" (`.scratch/comparison-finished.png`): verdict without a pick, Apply only when nothing landed, Undo buttons.
- **Live (2026-10-20, `.scratch/compare-live.cjs`, Codex `gpt-5.6-terra:low`, one pair, bot change naming the check's hidden empty-name requirement; the task text forbade running checks):**
  - The mechanics held: each run kept one setup across its two attempts (`979e289703898d16` changed, `f133502c676af0ed` current), the two differed only in `instructions.bot`, no check file needed restoring, and the two corrected tasks wrote no `lessons.json`. The page and records were written; the log had no warnings.
  - The expected outcome did not happen: both runs failed once and passed after one correction. The instruction did arrive: the changed run's Codex rollout (`~/.codex/sessions/2026/10/20/…01a15afb-9f1c…`) carries it in the developer instructions, and the other run's rollout does not. So Terra low received it and ignored it on the first attempt.
  - The verdict was helped, low confidence, from 28% less active time (21 s against 30 s); tokens were 18% lower (127,512 against 155,070), under the threshold. Both runs started at 23:23:22, so order did not skew the time; one pair is still the weak evidence its confidence says. That verdict led to the tokens-first rule above; under it this pair is no observed effect (tokens within 20%).
  - The fingerprint hashes the saved instruction text, not what the provider received; delivery is covered by the end-to-end test, which reads each worker's `thread/start` developer instructions.
  - A real Hub turn (`.scratch/hub-schema-live.cjs`, Terra low) accepted the strict response schema with `compare` in it and replied normally.

## Suggested comparisons

Built 2026-10-21 (plan item 5's "tuning suggestions from recorded outcomes"); made fully automatic the same day at the user's request ("anything we do should be done automatically, no more settings or approvals unless it is needed"):
- **When a lesson did not stick:** `openTask` saves which lessons a task's first message carried (`task.lessonKeys`, the keys of the records that fit in the 1,500-character block, `lessonsOffer` in `src/lessons.cjs`). When that task then fails the same check with the same first error line, `saveLesson` passes `carried` and the task's route, and `recordLesson` flags the record `recurred: { at, mode }`. A failure on a task that did not carry the lesson flags nothing.
- **When it starts:** in `sendCoordinator`, before a fresh user request's single `agent_work` is dispatched (not worker results, mail, routines, group chats or a delegated Hub), `suggestedComparison` looks for a recurred lesson not yet tested whose words overlap the request. Configured check names are left out of the match, or every task in the project would match. Harness decides, not the Hub model, and starts the comparison on that task with no question; if a comparison could not start (no checks, one already running, no model) the task is just dispatched. The model is the one the user asked Bot for, else the one the lesson failed on. Each lesson is tested once (`suggestedAt`).
- **The rule** is a template: `"<check>" has failed more than once with: <first error line> Make sure your change handles this before you finish.` It goes into `PhasmaHarness.md` in one run of a project-target comparison, while both runs keep the normal lesson block. Bot says why it is running the task twice, then the comparison settles like any other (results above): the better run lands in the project and the rule is kept if it helped.
- **Pitfall found by the smoke:** `ui/app.js` already had a `renderSuggestion()` for reply suggestions; a second top-level function of that name silently replaced the first (the later declaration wins), so the question buttons never rendered. Check for an existing name before adding a top-level function there.
- **Tests:** "a lesson that fails again…" in `tests/lessons.test.cjs`; the gate test "a corrected check failure becomes a lesson…" (`tests/core.test.cjs`) fails a second task that carried the lesson and checks `recurred.mode`; "a lesson that did not stick tests a permanent rule…" in `tests/coordinator.test.cjs` (an unrelated request is just dispatched, a matching one starts the comparison with no question and no extra Hub call, pinned to the lesson's model, on the original request, once per lesson). The comparison end-to-end test ("Hub compare starts on request…") covers the automatic results: one Hub call, the Auto model pinned, the better run applied, the project rule kept, both Undos; `tests/job-workspaces.test.cjs` "Undo puts applied files back…"; UI smoke step "comparison card" (`.scratch/comparison-finished.png`).

## Reflection on failed checks

Built 2026-10-21 (self-improvement plan step 1, after harness2's reflector; automatic, no setting):
- **When:** `saveLesson` (`src/controller/task-gate.cjs`) records a lesson (a task needed a correction or still fails its checks; never for comparison runs), then calls `reflectOnLesson` (`src/controller/reflection.cjs`) in the background.
- **Cost:** one structured call on the user's router model (`effectiveRouter` of the primary), only while background work is not paused at the usage limit (`agents.backgroundQuota`), cached by a hash of the exact input, so the same run is never asked twice. Aborted when its controller closes.
- **Input (`reflectionInput` in `src/reflection.cjs`):** the goal, up to three failing checks with redacted output, the task's own run from `task.startIndex` (requests, worker messages, commands with exit codes and output tails, file changes; private paths such as `.env` left out; everything through `redact`; the first entry plus the newest work within 10,000 characters), and the project's current `PhasmaHarness.md`.
- **Answer:** "rule" (one line, at most 300 characters) or "keep as is". `checkReflection` keeps a rule only when the quoted `moment` appears verbatim (whitespace and case aside) in the text that was sent, and the rule has no URL, code fence, credential words, or anything about skipping, editing or weakening checks or tests. The rule and moment are redacted again; a failed answer is stored as `rejected` with its reason.
- **Stored:** on the lesson record as `reflection` (`saveReflection` in `src/lessons.cjs` re-reads `lessons.json` right before writing, so writes made during the call survive) and on `lessons.md` as "Proposed rule: … (from: "…")" or "Reflection: keep as is".
- **Used:** a grounded rule goes on trial (see Trial rules on real work); a lesson that did not stick tests the reflected rule in its suggested comparison instead of the template; a reflection that chose "keep as is" means no comparison for that lesson (harness2's KEEP_BASE).
- **Tests:** `tests/reflection.test.cjs` (redaction and private files, newest work kept, grounding, guard, keep, cache, a write during the call surviving, no call past the background limit); the gate test "a corrected check failure becomes a lesson…" (`tests/core.test.cjs`) gets a grounded rule on the lesson and its page; "a reflected rule is what a lesson that did not stick tests…" (`tests/coordinator.test.cjs`).
- **Live (2026-10-21, `.scratch/reflection-live.cjs`, one call):** router Terra low answered in 9.6 s with "When modifying input-dependent behavior, run the relevant checks and handle documented or test-exposed empty-input cases before declaring completion.", quoting the check output `greet("") returned "Hello, !", expected "Hello, stranger!"`. A token planted in the run never reached the stored files; the log had no warnings. The rule is generic; whether such rules help is for trials (plan step 2) to show.

## Trial rules on real work

Built 2026-10-21 (self-improvement plan step 2; automatic, no setting). `src/trials.cjs` (records, matching, outcomes) and `src/controller/trials.cjs` (task-gate wiring, notices, Undo):
- **Entry:** a grounded reflection rule goes on trial (`trialFromReflection` in `src/controller/reflection.cjs`) only when its failure was seen at least twice (harness2: a repeated failure is evidence, a one-off is not; added 2026-10-21 after review, since three first-time passes of a usually-passing check prove little), and not while its lesson is `recurred` and untested by a comparison; then the suggested comparison tests it first. A suggested comparison whose verdict is helped puts its rule on trial (`adoptRule`), not into `PhasmaHarness.md`; a comparison the user asked for still adopts directly. The card's Undo marks that rule removed and, if it was kept since, takes its line out of `PhasmaHarness.md` too (`undoComparison`, added 2026-10-21 so no untracked line stays behind). At most five rules are on trial per project; the oldest makes room. Rule text that was taken out before (rolled back, dropped by a hit, removed by Undo or deleted by hand) never goes on trial again (`retired`; `startTrial` returns null, and a suggested comparison falls back to the template rule or skips); a rule that only made room may.
- **Carried:** `openTask` adds trial rules that are about a check the task runs and share words with the request (`matchingTrials`, at most three) as their own block ahead of the lessons (`trialBlock`, 800 characters), so the lesson cut never drops them. `task.trialRuleIds` records what fit; their lesson keys join `task.lessonKeys`. Kept rules are carried through `PhasmaHarness.md`, so `task.keptRuleIds` lists those about a check the task runs whose exact line is in the `PhasmaHarness.md` that task's worker reads (`carriedKept`; a batch copy reads its own copy), and their lesson keys join too. A task that started before a rule was kept never carried it. Comparison runs carry none (their frozen block comes from `lessonsFor`).
- **Decided** in `runGate`, before `saveLesson`, by `recordOutcomes` (re-reads `rules.json` first), once per task:
  - **clean:** the rule's check ran on the first attempt and passed;
  - **hit:** any attempt failed that check with the rule's target first line (matched per failure, like lessons: `failureLine`);
  - **none:** anything else, including skipped checks.

  Three clean keep the rule: one dated line under `## Rules Harness learned` in `PhasmaHarness.md` (`keepInInstructions`, created once, 64 KiB guard). One hit drops it, and the same pass records the lesson as carried, so it becomes `recurred` and the paid comparison takes over.
- **Always-on cap (plan step 4, built 2026-10-21):** at most 10 kept rules have a line in `PhasmaHarness.md` (`MAX_KEPT`). A rule that wins with 10 there takes the place of the unpinned one tasks matched least recently (`lastMatchedAt`, stamped when a task that carried the rule shares words with its goal and error, after that task's keeps are decided; ties go to the oldest `keptAt`). The word match is the signal because in a one-check project every task carries every kept rule. The displaced rule is marked `onDemand` (its old line in `movedLine`) before its line leaves the file, so `forgetDeletedKept` never mistakes it for a hand deletion; the kept notice names it, and Undo of that keep moves it back. With all 10 pinned, the winner itself is kept on demand.
- **On demand:** kept rules without a line ride the tasks they match like trials (check runs + shared words, `matchingOnDemand`), at most 2, in their own block after the trial block ("Project rules learned from earlier failures here…"), so they never take trial slots. They count as carried (`keptRuleIds`), so a hit rolls them back too (notice "Stopped adding the rule…", Undo pins them on demand). At most 30 (`MAX_ON_DEMAND`); past that the least recently matched unpinned one is dropped with `room: true`, so its text may come back (`retired` now keys on that flag; the five-trial cap sets it too). Not built: skills with trigger lines, demoting a rule whose check left the project, merging near-duplicate rule texts.
- **Rollback (plan step 3, built 2026-10-21):** a hit on a task that carried a kept rule takes its line out of the project's `PhasmaHarness.md` (`recordOutcomes` sets `rolled back`, then `rollBackRule`), with a notice and Undo. If the line is already gone, the rule is marked removed with no notice. The lesson is recorded as carried, so it becomes `recurred`: its next reflected rule goes to the paid comparison if none ran for that lesson yet, otherwise on trial (a lesson that reached `kept` through a suggested comparison already has `suggestedAt`). Undo of a rollback puts the same line back and pins the rule (`pinned`), so it is never rolled back automatically again, even past `MAX_KEPT`. A kept line the user deletes by hand is found at the next task's `openTask` (`forgetDeletedKept`, against the source project's file) and marked removed, which frees its place under the cap. Not built: a comparison that tests removing a kept rule.
- **Visible:** each kept, dropped or rolled-back rule adds a `notice` item to the chat the user follows (the owner's chat for a delegated job). A kept rule's notice has Undo (`undoRule`, also on Remote Control), which removes the exact line (and the heading once nothing is left under it, `removeKeptLine`) and marks the rule removed; a rolled-back rule's Undo ("Put the rule back") restores and pins it. Exports include notices as "Harness" sections. `rules.md` beside the lessons lists every rule with its state and count.
- **Tests:** `tests/trials.test.cjs` (clean, hit, none; keep after three; drop on a hit; dedupe by task; matching, block cap, the five-rule cap; the PhasmaHarness.md section; rollback only when carried, pinned rules survive, hand-deleted lines removed, taken-out text refused; the always-on cap, on-demand matching and its cap); `tests/lessons.test.cjs` covers digit-blind keys, merges and expiry; the gate test "a corrected check failure becomes a lesson…" (`tests/core.test.cjs`) shows a one-off failure gets no trial and a second one waits for the comparison; "a carried trial rule whose target failure comes back…" drops it with a notice and makes its lesson `recurred`; "a trial rule carried by three matching tasks…" keeps it with a notice and Undo; "a kept rule whose target failure comes back…" rolls it back with a notice, makes its lesson `recurred`, restores and pins it on Undo, and marks a hand-deleted line removed at the next task; "a rule kept with ten rules already in PhasmaHarness.md…" moves out the least recently matched one, carries it on demand, and moves it back on Undo; "an on-demand rule whose target failure comes back…" rolls it back and Undo pins it on demand; "a suggested comparison that helped puts its rule on trial…" (`tests/coordinator.test.cjs`); UI smoke step "rule notices" (kept, dropped and rolled back, with both Undo buttons). Checked through the real gate with a fake model; no paid run was needed.

## Proven setups

Built 2026-10-21 (self-improvement plan step 6). `src/setups.cjs` (records, derivation, rollback; `setups.json` and a generated `setups.md` beside the lessons) and `src/controller/setups.cjs` (wiring):
- **Evidence:** `runGate` calls `recordSetup` after `saveLesson` for a task Auto routed (`openTask` saves `task.kind`/`task.difficulty` from `selected.assessment`; manual picks carry none) whose checks ran: `clean` (passed, no corrections of its own: `task.startCorrections` holds those carried from a delegation), `corrected`, or `failed` (still failing); blocked, cancelled and not-checked say nothing to the gate. One record per task (`taskId`). Never comparison runs. Tokens are the sum of its attempts' `turnUsage` (null when any is unreported). At most 300 records.
- **Chat-judged evidence (2026-10-21):** a task that settled `not-checked` (no checks, or the router skipped them) gets its record from the chat checker instead: `SCAN_SCHEMA.outcome` is accepted (builds on the result, thanks, moves on), corrected (corrects, redirects, repeats the ask) or unclear; `checkScan` maps them to `clean` / `corrected` / nothing. `runScan` calls `recordSetup(exchange.taskSession, task, { outcome, noticeIn })`: the record goes to the project of the chat that ran the task (in Bot's chat, the worker's), the notices to the chat the user wrote in. Records carry `source: 'chat'`; same-task follow-ups (`task.sameTask`, saved by `openTask` from the route) are skipped, since they would score a fix of the earlier mistake as right first time, and so are routine runs (the task's first item has `routineRun`), which any next message would "accept". Only while a chat checker model is picked (Off by default).
- **Proven:** per kind, over its last 20 low and medium records, presets with at least 3 runs are ranked by first-time passes, then failures; tokens break a tie only within one provider (providers count them differently) and never when unreported. A preference needs two such presets and a strict winner with at least 3 clean runs, is derived only while the kind has none, and never for a (kind, preset) the user undid (`blocked`).
- **Applied:** `send` passes `provenSetups` (`provenSetupsFor`) in the router context; `finalChoice` applies `provenChoice` after `pickPreset` on the non-kept path, for low and medium difficulty, when the preset is offered (enabled, effort cap, usage limits), keeping `routerPick` and a "Proven setup for <kind> work in this project" reason. Every Auto path ends in `finalChoice`, Jev's included (its other exit is quick direct answers).
- **Rollback:** the preferred preset not passing a low or medium task of its kind first time drops the preference (notice `setupDropped`); Auto then chooses freely and gathers evidence again. The `setupPreferred` notice's Undo ("Stop preferring this model") calls `dropPreference`.
- **Limits:** without checks, learns only while the chat checker is on, and judges from one reply (a correction that comes two messages later is missed); learns only where Auto already used several presets for the same kind; no exploration while a preference holds; no per-difficulty preferences.
- **Tests:** `tests/setups.test.cjs` (outcomes, thresholds, ties across providers, unreported tokens, hard tasks ignored, Undo block, rollback; Smart and a Jev-shaped decision through `SmartRouter.choose`); gate test "a preset that passes a kind of work's checks first time…" and "without checks, the chat checker reading your replies…" (`tests/core.test.cjs`); `tests/chat-scan.test.cjs` (outcome parsing, which tasks the scanner records); UI smoke step "rule notices".

## Cascade repair and the chat checker's judge

Built 2026-10-29 at the user's request, after reviewing GitHub Copilot Fusion's Single/Cascade/Critique patterns (changelog 2026-10-28). Reworked the same day to the user's design: one cheap checker model (the Chat checker picked in Settings) is both Fusion's cascade judge and its critic, on every response, with or without checks, and escalation goes to the better worker of any provider. A first version reviewed only after passed checks, on another provider's strongest model; the user rejected tying it to checks and to a second provider. Later that day the user asked for manual picks to escalate too and for a switch per function in its Settings tab (see Switches below).

**Cascade (repair).** In `runGate` (`src/controller/task-gate.cjs`), a task goes once to `repairPreset` (`src/controller/chat-scan.cjs`) when its checks still fail after its one correction, or when the checker escalates it. `repairPreset` is the Escalation model as set (`escalationPick`; see Switches), else, on Auto or while that pick is unavailable, at a usage limit or without image support a task with images needs, the strongest measured worker at the task's Auto difficulty effort, or a manual pick's own effort (`route.effort`, else medium), within the effort cap and usage limits.
- **Not for:** comparison runs, a worker already on that model, no measured scores on Auto, or a provider over `backgroundQuota`. Admission is the same as for a correction (`agents.acquire`: job budgets).
- **Message:** `repairText` (`src/tasks.cjs`) says why (the checker's findings, else the failed checks) and carries the original request. A switch inside one chat does not give a Codex thread the CLI turns: `sendCLI` hands off chat items, but `loadThread` only resumes Codex's own thread.
- **Records:**
  - `task.repair` = `{ from, route, label, at }`; the repair route carries `escalatedFrom`, which the turn trace logs.
  - `setups.taskOutcome` returns `failed` for the original preset whatever the repair did (checks passed, none, or Needs you), also when the chat scan later reads the user's reply as accepting it, so proven setups never credit the cheap model with the strong model's fix.
  - `session.job.worker` becomes the repair model, so a follow-up on that task continues there.
  - After failed checks the task reason stays "checks failed after one correction" (lessons key on it). The summary reads "Handing the task to X", then "Needs you: checks still failed after X took over".
- **Not built: savings.** The cascade alone saves nothing; savings need a cheaper *first* pick. Only High/Xhigh go straight to the strongest model, and Harness has no record of cheaper models on such tasks. Proven setups ignore hard tasks, and getting that evidence means deliberately routing some hard tasks to cheaper models. That trades spend for quality, so it is the user's decision.

**Switches (2026-10-29).** Each is on unless turned off, validated as a boolean in `Controller.settings`, read from the primary's settings when it runs, and in `Agents.settings`'s local keys, so changing it never waits for agents to stop. UI: `ON_SWITCHES` in `ui/app.js`.
- General, under "Chat checker, reviser, escalator and rule finder model" (`#settings-chat-scan`, the model they all use):
  - Check each response (`checkResponses`): off means no look and no snapshot (`responseChecker`, `send`).
  - Revise: send problems back (`checkerRevise`) and Escalate: hand to the escalation model (`checkerEscalate`): the two steps of the judge's ladder. With both off, a look only reports.
  - Find rules from your replies (`scanReplies`): off means `runScan` reads no exchange, so no rules, notes or chat-judged setup records.
- Checks tab: Escalate failed checks (`escalateFailedChecks`): off means checks that still fail after one correction stop at Needs you.
- Escalation model (`escalationModel`, Auto or a worker preset, validated like Model for simple messages): who takes over a task (both triggers) and writes the chat checker's rules and notes (`strongestPreset`, when the pick answers in a schema). A pick is used as set, above the effort cap too; Auto, or a pick that is disabled or at its usage limit, means the strongest measured model within the cap. Added 2026-10-29 because the user found the take-over model, decided by the cap and switches in three tabs, confusing. One picker (`#settings-escalation-model` + `#settings-escalation-effort`) in General, under the checker's switches. Routing and Checks show only its name and a link, "General → Chat checker, reviser, escalator and rule finder model" (`p.setting-link` with `data-setting="settings-chat-scan"`), which opens General with that section's first row at the top, focuses its picker and outlines the row for 1.6 s (`.setting-flash`; a still outline under reduced motion). The user asked for links, not copies, and for the landing to show the setting the link names rather than centring the Escalation model further down (2026-10-29).
- Routing tab: "Effort cap for Auto" is now "Effort cap", and its hint (`capHint`) says it caps Smart and Jev routing, the escalation model while it is on Auto and comparison runs; a model picked by hand (for a bot, the escalation model, the chat checker or routing) keeps its own effort. "Routing model (Auto)" is now "Routing model", and the tab's intro says it picks for messages on Auto and for the escalation model on Auto.

**The checker's judge (`checkResponse`).** When a worker's turn on a tracked task settles with its checks passed or with none, the chat checker (`chatScanner()`, Off by default, picked by hand; the same model scans the exchange after the user's next message) answers one structured call with no tools: `accept`, `revise` or `escalate`.
- **When:** every tracked task (task tracking is on unless set to Off), Auto or manual, any difficulty. Not for comparison runs, a task with a job budget (the call is not counted there) or a provider over `backgroundQuota`. With no checker, `responseChecker` returns null without an await. Found 2026-10-29: an unconditional `await checkResponse()` kept the gate one tick longer and broke "delayed completion of an older turn does not finish the current turn" and "compaction finishes through thread/compacted…" (`tests/core.test.cjs`).
- **Verdicts:**
  - accept: nothing changes.
  - revise: the findings go back as a Router message (`revisionText`) to the worker that wrote the work (`lastWorker`: the repair model after a repair). It fixes the real ones, says why any is not, and the checks run again.
  - escalate: the task, a manual pick included, goes to `repairPreset` with the findings (`escalationText`).
  - Bounds: one revise and one escalation per task, each followed by another look, so at most three looks (`LOOKS`). A second revise escalates. An escalation that is switched off or cannot run (no scores, quota, already the strongest model) becomes the revise, and a revise that is switched off escalates. Problems left after both are only reported ("X still found N problem(s): review the result"); the checks still decide the state.
- **Snapshot:** `send` starts `taskBaseline` for tracked tasks while a checker is picked (Auto routes meanwhile) and awaits it before `submitWorker`, so the checker sees only the task's own changes.
  - Cost: a manual pick, which has no routing to hide behind, starts that much later, as does any send whose route finishes first. A Jev one-call route measured 219-449 ms.
  - Measured 2026-10-29 on PhasmaEngine (35 changed entries): `stash create` and `ls-files --others` run in parallel took 412-490 ms over three runs. Run one after the other they took 0.59 s + 0.15 s. Not measured inside the desktop app.
  - A plain folder (no `.git` there or above it) starts no Git process and is judged on its reply alone. Found 2026-10-29: the git spawn in a non-Git test folder delayed the usage-limit failover's re-send past the tick-bounded `flush()` in "usage limits: Stop is never overridden by the failover…" (`tests/core.test.cjs`).
  - It is `git stash create`, which changes neither the files nor the stash list (HEAD on a clean tree), plus `git ls-files --others`. Each run leaves an unreferenced commit object that Git's gc prunes.
  - Snapshots live in memory for the last 20 tasks, so a restart only costs that task's changes (its reply is still judged).
- **Input (`src/critique.cjs`):**
  - The redacted request, the worker's reply without its status line (8,000 characters), which checks passed or "none ran", and `git diff -U10 <snapshot>` per file plus files the task created (text, at most 64 KiB).
  - Private paths (`PRIVATE_PATH`) are left out. Each file is capped at 12,000 characters and the whole at 60,000.
  - Changes the user made before the task are not shown.
- **Grounding (`checkCritique`):** a finding counts only when its quote (10-300 characters) appears verbatim, with diff markers, whitespace and case aside, in the source it names: a shown file or "reply". Findings asking to skip or weaken tests are dropped, and at most five are kept. A revise or escalate left with none is an accept, so a cheap checker cannot call the strong model without evidence.
- **Records:** `task.critique` = `{ model, state, looks, revised, findings, dropped }`. A revision counts as `corrected` in proven setups, whatever the checks or the user's reply said.
- **Visible:** "Checking the response with X" while it runs, "Sending X's findings back" while a revision is sent. Afterwards: "· checked by X, no problems found", "· X accepted it after one revision", "· X accepted it after Y took over" or "· X still found N problem(s): review the result", after "Configured checks passed", "No checks configured" or "Checks skipped by router". A failed look reads "coverage not assessed" as before.
- **Limits:**
  - The checker cannot read files beyond the diff context.
  - A file the task changed that was already untracked before it is missed.
  - Edits the user makes while the task runs are judged as the task's.
  - Its calls' tokens are not added to the chat's usage; the checker also reads each exchange again in the chat scan after the user's next message.
  - The busy slot stays held while it looks, up to its 120 s inactivity limit.
  - There is no rollback to the pre-revision draft: a revision that breaks the checks goes through the normal correction.
  - Not measured: what the looks cost and how often a cheap checker's findings are real.

**Tests:** `tests/core.test.cjs`:
- "checks still failing after one correction hand an Auto task once…"
- "the cascade runs once, a manual pick escalates at its own effort, it can be turned off…"
- "a repair takes the Escalation model as set, else the best measured model…"
- "the chat checker reads a response without checks…" (a real Git repo with a change made before the task, which must not be shown)
- "the chat checker's escalate hands an Auto task to a stronger worker…"
- "the chat checker looks at most three times…" (manual pick escalation included)
- "each chat checker switch turns its step off…"
- "the chat checker is off until a model is picked…" (Find rules from your replies off)
- "with the checker off, failing or quoting nothing it was shown…" (plain folder included)

Also `tests/critique.test.cjs` (private paths, new and binary files, size cap, grounding on the reply and files, ungrounded escalate, the five-finding cap), `tests/setups.test.cjs` (revision is corrected, escalation is failed) and the escalated-route case in `tests/routing-calls.test.cjs`. Pool entry: `cascade-and-critique`. Offline only; no paid model call was made.

## Chat scanner

Built 2026-10-21 (self-improvement plan step 5, requested by the user: "a second low model that always scans the chat and tasks"; runs once a model is picked as the chat checker, Off by default). `src/chat-scan.cjs` (inputs, answers, scans.json, notes.json) and `src/controller/chat-scan.cjs` (wiring):
- **When:** `send` calls `scanAfterUserTurn` for a real user turn (`session.userTurn`), never for `[Router]` corrections, failover re-sends, delegated jobs or comparison runs. The exchange is taken at once, before the new message opens its own task, and scanned on the next tick; the hook never throws or delays the send. Worker chat: the last task's items from `startIndex`. Bot chat: the last real request plus the run of the one job it delegated (`agentResult` items, worker task by `assignmentId`, project = the result's workspace); no job or several jobs are skipped.
- **Scan:** the chat checker (`chatScanner`): Settings > General > Chat checker model, any enabled model that answers in a schema (`chatScanChoices`, snapshot `chatScanPresets`, setting `chatScanModel`, added 2026-10-21 at the user's request); **Off by default** (no `chatScanModel`, or `off`): the user picks the model by hand, and only that model checks; while it is unavailable nothing is scanned. There is no Auto and no Jev option (decided 2026-10-21: the user judged Jev unfit for checking; it can only pick among options, so it could not quote the exchange). Changing it never waits for agents to stop. Within the background usage limit, once per exchange (`claimScan`, before the call, so a failure is not retried). Shown, redacted: the request, the newest work that fits (6,000 characters), the task's outcome, the user's next message, the project's `PhasmaHarness.md` and riding rules, and the chat rules the task carried. Answer: `worthy`, `problem`, a verbatim `moment` (no quote, not worthy), per carried chat rule `recurred` / `avoided` / `not relevant`, and `outcome` (accepted / corrected / unclear), which decides proven setups for tasks without checks (see Proven setups).
- **Also the judge:** the same model checks each response as it ends (see Cascade repair and the chat checker's judge).
- **Escalation:** a worthy exchange goes to `strongestPreset` (best measured worker that answers in a schema, medium effort; without measured scores the router's model), at most 5 calls per project per day, counted when made (`claimEscalation`). It answers `rule`, `note` or `nothing` through the reflection guard. A rule goes on trial with `check: null`, `error` = the problem, `goal` = the request, `source` = "the chat" (retired text refused); a note joins `notes.json` (30 at most, older than 90 days expire except the newest 10). Each posts a notice with Undo (`trialStarted`, `noteAdded`).
- **Chat rules on trial:** carried only by tasks sharing two words with the request they came from (`chatMatches`), one per task, so they never ride everything. `recordOutcomes` leaves them alone (no check to decide them); the scanner's verdict at the next exchange does (`recordChatVerdicts`): recurred = hit (dropped, or rolled back once kept), avoided = clean (three keep it), not relevant = none. Kept chat rules count as carried from `PhasmaHarness.md` only on a close match, which also stamps `lastMatchedAt`.
- **Notes:** offered to tasks sharing two words with the note or its request, at most 2, as context after the rules ("Notes Harness took from earlier chats…"). `notes.md` lists them.
- **Tests:** `tests/chat-scan.test.cjs` (redaction, grounding, verdict filtering, escalation guard, once per exchange, 5 a day, notes, Bot attribution, never scanned cases); `tests/trials.test.cjs` (two-word matching, one chat rule per task, checks never decide them, verdicts keep/drop/roll back); gate tests "a correction in the chat becomes a trial rule…" and "a note from the chat rides matching tasks…" (`tests/core.test.cjs`); UI smoke step "rule notices".
- **Live (2026-10-21, `.scratch/chat-scan-live.cjs`, three calls, before the setting existed):** the router (Terra low) flagged a file-rename exchange the user corrected ("keep the exported name") in 10.9 s with a verbatim quote, and passed over an ordinary follow-up in 6.0 s; the strongest pick (Astra medium) wrote a grounded rule in 7.2 s; no log warnings. Pitfall found there: catalog presets carry no measured scores until `smartRouter.benchmarks.catalog()` joins them, so `strongest()` on the raw catalog always fell back to the router; `strongestPreset` joins them now (`.scratch/strongest-probe.cjs` prints the pick without a model call).
- **Not built:** a middle model, a setting for the rule-writing model, scanning an exchange nobody replies to (failed checks already reach lessons and reflection).

## Apply a project copy's changes

Built 2026-10-21 (first half of "suggested comparisons", requested by the user so a comparison or batch run's result can reach the real project; tests and smoke only, no live run yet):
- **Where:** a finished comparison applies its better run by itself (see Two-run comparison). By hand: an "Apply changes to my project…" button in job details (once the job is delivered; choosing among several batch copies stays the user's call) and an Apply button per run on a comparison card when nothing landed by itself. Both call the `applyCopy` runner command, which is also on Remote Control.
- **What counts as a change (`JobWorkspaces.changes`):** the copy's current files (`inventory`, so ignore rules apply) against its batch snapshot manifest (`input.json`, content hashes of every working file, git and plain folders alike). So a file that was already dirty at snapshot time is not a change, and ignored build outputs are never applied. Left out: the copied external wiki (`.phasma-job-wiki/`), an added `PhasmaHarness.md` equal to the starter Harness writes into every writable workspace, and `PhasmaHarness.md` in a comparison copy (a comparison's rule is adopted separately).
- **Conflicts:** a change applies only where the project's file still equals the snapshot (or is absent for an addition). Any other difference refuses the whole apply and names the files. A file the project already has with the copy's content is skipped. A different project file edited since the snapshot is no conflict.
- **Safety:**
  - the dialog lists up to 30 files and says the changes land uncommitted (nothing staged or committed), that ignored files are not applied, and where the previous versions stay (the batch snapshot's `input` folder);
  - `apply` re-runs the preview after the dialog and writes nothing unless it matches exactly;
  - each file is checked again just before its own write; deletions go first (a case-only rename is one file on Windows);
  - writes go to a temp file renamed into place;
  - paths follow `scoped()`'s rules (`resolveInside`), and an existing link on the way is refused;
  - refused while a bot still works in the copy (an undelivered job with this copy or scope, or a running chat turn there).
- **Target:** the snapshot's `source`, checked against the copy record; a copy of a copy applies into its parent copy.
- **Tests:** four "Apply …" tests in `tests/job-workspaces.test.cjs` (git and plain folder: edits, additions in new folders, deletions, rerun finds nothing, dirty and ignored files left out, conflicts, identical additions, Harness files, busy copy, copy changed after preview, a junction in the project); "Apply shows the files…" in `tests/runner-commands.test.cjs` (dialog text, 30-file cap, Cancel and conflicts write nothing, exactly the preview is applied); the UI smoke checks the job-details button (hidden while running) and the card's Apply buttons (only after reveal).

## Provider panel and Options menu UI

Verified 2026-10-19 in the UI smoke (screenshots in `.scratch/`):
- **Spinners:** `withProviderAction(id, label, run)` in `ui/app.js` marks a provider row busy while an action runs, and `renderProviderConnections` shows the spinner with the label ("Connecting…", "Installing…", "Updating…", "Saving key…", "Removing…"). Used for account connect/disconnect, Install/Update, API-provider enable/key/remove and Jev key save/remove. Model fetches ("Fetching … models…") and the LM Studio load button also show the spinner. Install is hidden while a row is busy.
- **Chat bubbles (verified 2026-10-20):** user messages align right and agent replies/commentary align left; all use content-sized bubbles with an 88% maximum transcript width. Bubble colours are sampled from the supplied references: agents use #efefef in light mode and #292929 in dark mode; users use #1c1c1c in light mode and #191919 in dark mode, with white text in both. User links inherit white and inline/fenced code keeps dark backgrounds for contrast. Bubble padding is 5.5px vertically and 13px horizontally, reducing a single-line bubble's height by about one third without shrinking text; long content continues to wrap. Manual and Hub Auto routes no longer create redundant Model paragraphs or empty routing rows. Provider/model reply badges, pending/error status and expandable worker routing details remain. Sources: `ui/style.css`, `ui/app.js`; UI smoke verifies short/long bubble dimensions, alignment, wrapping, neutral/darker user colours in both themes, agent/commentary bubbles and absence of redundant rows. Metadata has no You heading; provider/model badges follow the timestamp and copy button below replies. Timestamps show elapsed time (Just now, minutes, hours, days, months or years ago), refreshing every 30 seconds while visible and on returning to the window; their tooltip and datetime retain the exact send time. Rows align left for agents and right for users, and reveal on hover, bubble click or keyboard focus. Opacity preserves row geometry so revealing it does not move messages; message margins are 8px and action-row spacing is 2px. Task-status badges remain visible inside their bubbles. UI smoke covers click/focus reveal, ordering, alignment, reduced margins and task-status visibility.
- **Composer context indicator (verified 2026-10-20):** the gear disclosure and its Commands/Clear toolbar are removed. The provider-reported context ring sits directly to the left of the microphone, matching the other control sizes and visible gaps. Hover or keyboard focus shows context percentage/counts and session token totals; unsupported/missing usage remains unknown. The button remains focusable when compaction is unavailable (`aria-disabled` blocks its click action), so the report is still accessible during work. Compact uses the existing command when supported and idle. `/help`, `/clear`, `/context` and `/compact` remain available. Model configuration remains in Edit; hidden model state controls retain existing send/queued-edit behavior. Sources: `ui/index.html`, `ui/app.js`, `ui/style.css`; UI smoke checks known usage, tooltip bounds, active-turn clicks and whole-interface zoom.
- **Sending rolls to the end (2026-10-21):** the chat follows new messages only within 100 px of the bottom (`renderMessages`), so a message sent from a scrolled-up chat appeared out of view. The composer's submit handler now calls `scrollChatToEnd`: an eased roll that takes the same 300 ms (`SEND_SCROLL_MS`) from any distance and reads the end every frame, so a message rendered meanwhile is included; with reduced motion it jumps. Pitfalls:
  - `#conversation` has `scroll-behavior: smooth`, so each frame uses `scrollTo({ behavior: 'instant' })`; a plain `scrollTop` assignment would animate.
  - `renderMessages`' own follow (a smooth `scrollTop = scrollHeight` near the bottom) took over the roll's frames in Chromium, ending it early. It is skipped while `rollingToEnd` is set.
  - UI smoke step "send scrolls to the newest message": without the fix the sent message stayed about 1,300 px below the view; with it, 1,403 px and 281 px away both took 283–284 ms.
- **Model pickers wait for providers (2026-10-21, user report):** Settings, the agent and Hub dialogs and the Benchmarks worker filled their model lists once, when opened, so a list opened while providers were still connecting kept only the early ones (API keys, LM Studio) until reopened. Now every model picker (composer `#preset`, `#settings-router-preset`, `#settings-chat-scan`, `#agent-model`, `#hub-model`, `#benchmark-worker`) shows only a disabled "Connecting…" while `providersConnecting()` is active (the same set as the header indicator: `connectingProviders` of every agent, Codex's own connection, model fetches, provider actions, a Jev key test), and `renderProviderConnections` → `renderModelPickers` refills open ones in place when it clears (`fillPicker` rebuilds only on a changed list, keeping an unsaved dialog choice; Settings pickers show the saved value except while their own save runs, `dataset.saving`). While connecting, `currentMode()` sends with the saved model, and the agent and Hub Save buttons wait, so a blank model is never saved. Opening Settings → Providers renews Cursor's model list, which counts as connecting until it answers. UI smoke: "while any provider connects, every model picker shows only Connecting…" (screenshot `.scratch/pickers-connecting.png`); its fake model server now answers later Cursor fetches at once (`holdCursorModels`).
- **Model groups:** each provider's models in Settings → Providers sit in a `<details class="provider-model-section">`; a collapsed group stays collapsed across re-renders for the session (`collapsedModelGroups`).
- **Smoke timing:** each smoke phase has a 30 s deadline; the provider steps run in their own "provider settings" phase.

## CLI install and update

Verified 2026-10-19 on Windows (live lookups and fixtures; no real update run yet):
- **Button:** a missing CLI shows **Install**; an installed CLI whose official latest release is newer shows **Update** (title "installed → latest"). `src/main.cjs` sets `controller.cliUpdateCheck` and runs `refreshCliUpdates()` in the background after startup; Renew runs it again. Tests and the headless server never set it, so they make no network calls.
- **Versions (`src/providers/install.cjs`):** installed = the longest version token of `<cli> --version`; latest = npm `/<pkg>/latest` (Codex, dsh), `downloads.claude.ai/claude-code-releases/latest` (Claude), the version embedded in Cursor's install script, Muse's `muse-code/channels/muse-stable`, Antigravity's `manifests/<platform>.json`. `newer()` compares the numeric core, then a release beats its own prerelease, then suffix numbers (dsh publishes rc builds as npm latest). A failed lookup offers nothing. Muse on Windows (changed 2026-10-27): the installed version is read from `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\muse\.muse-version`, not `muse --version`. `muse.cmd` starts its unsigned `.muse-launcher.ps1` with `-ExecutionPolicy Bypass`, and Bitdefender's `Gen:Suspicious.Cloud` heuristic blocked that every time Harness checked for updates (03:27 and 03:37 that day; nothing quarantined). Muse turns already run `muse-bin-*.exe` directly (`findNativeCLI`); only the user's own Update still uses the launcher. Test: the Muse part of "CLIs install the newest release…" in tests/providers-optional.test.cjs (fails without the change).
- **Update commands:** npm `@latest`; Claude and Cursor rerun their installers (both always fetch the newest; Cursor's deletes `%LOCALAPPDATA%\cursor-agent` first); Antigravity's installer exits when `agy.exe` exists, so it uses `agy update`; Muse's installer only replaces the launcher, so it runs the launcher with `MUSE_SYNC_UPDATE=1`.
- **Several at once (2026-10-21, at the user's request):** `inTurn` in `src/providers/install.cjs` lets each CLI install on its own while others run; a second click on the same CLI is refused, and the npm-based ones (Codex, DeepSeek: commands starting `npm `) take turns, since two global npm installs at once can break each other. A waiting npm install shows "Updating…" on its row and checks that no agent is busy when its turn starts. `refreshCliUpdates` keeps only the newest-started check (an older one finishing late would bring back a finished Update button) and returns each caller its own answer. The Settings status line clears only its own message. Limit: a Claude or Cursor install that finishes while Codex is updating refreshes accounts while Codex is closed, so ChatGPT shows disconnected until Codex reconnects a moment later. Tests: "CLIs install side by side…" and "overlapping update checks…" in `tests/providers-optional.test.cjs`; no live parallel update run yet.
- **Codex:** no longer pinned (`@latest`); the machine already ran 0.163.0. Updating closes every context's Codex client and router first (Windows cannot replace a running binary) and reconnects them after, success or failure. `MEASURED_REAP` in `src/tasks.cjs` still trusts parent-exit cleanup only for the measured 0.156.1; other versions need confirmed process-tree termination.
- **Regression:** "CLIs install the newest release, and Update is offered only for an older installed version" in `tests/providers-optional.test.cjs`; "Update button for a newer CLI release" in the UI smoke.
- **Not verified:** a real update run for each CLI (whether `agy update` or Muse's synchronous check ever prompts), and the Linux and macOS latest sources. Correction (2026-10-19): the Cursor Update clicked that day ran in a Harness started from the agent's sandboxed shell, so its result (2026.10.16, signed in) existed only in the sandbox view (see "Launching Harness from an agent session"). The real install came from rerunning the official installer through Explorer. A real in-Harness Update run is still unverified, and whether a real cursor-agent existed before that day is unknown. The installer warned "Git for Windows (with Git Bash) is required to run a private worker": Git is installed and C:Program FilesGitinash.exe exists, but PATH holds only Gitcmd and `bash` resolves to WSL's System32ash.exe.
- **PSModulePath pitfall (verified 2026-10-19):** Harness launched from PowerShell 7 inherits pwsh's module folders (`Documents\PowerShell\Modules`, `Program Files\PowerShell\…`). Every `powershell.exe` below Harness then loads PowerShell 7 modules and loses built-ins; the Claude installer failed with "Get-FileHash … CommandNotFoundException". pwsh cleans the path only when it starts `powershell.exe` directly, so a direct test hides it; going through `cmd` reproduces it. `cleanPowerShellModulePath()` in `src/process-tree.cjs` drops entries containing `\PowerShell\` at startup (`src/main.cjs`, `src/server.cjs`). Regression in `tests/providers-optional.test.cjs` (Windows probe: missing before, found after).

Gemini CLI was removed on 2026-10-19 (Antigravity CLI serves Gemini models). A saved `native:*` selection whose CLI no longer exists gets the CLI capability and is refused by `sendCLI` ("This CLI provider is unavailable."), never sent to Codex.

## Launching Harness from an agent session (sandbox pitfall)

Verified 2026-10-19 on this Windows machine: the coding agent's shell runs in a sandbox that redirects writes to `%LOCALAPPDATA%` (and the `%APPDATA%` root). A file written there from the agent's shell is invisible to Explorer and to anything Explorer starts, while the agent keeps seeing it. `%APPDATA%\Phasma Harness`, `%USERPROFILE%` (`~/.cursor`, `~/.local`), the repository and `%TEMP%` were real in a probe the same day.
- **What went wrong:** Harness restarted with `Start-Process` from the agent's shell ran inside that sandbox. A Cursor CLI Update clicked there replaced `%LOCALAPPDATA%\cursor-agent` only in the redirected view. The user's own Harness, launched from Explorer, then correctly reported "Cursor CLI · not installed" and logged `Provider check failed … Cursor CLI is not installed` (the `launchCursor` pre-check found no `cursor-agent.ps1`).
- **Rule:** start Harness, installers and any user-facing app from an agent with `explorer.exe "<path>\Launch Phasma Harness.cmd"`. Explorer starts it outside the sandbox. To check what the real system sees, run a `.cmd` through `explorer.exe` that writes its findings to `%TEMP%`.
- **Repair done:** the official Cursor installer was rerun through Explorer (2026.10.16-e3cb207, still signed in), and Harness was relaunched the same way; its startup log shows no Cursor check failure.
- **Diagnostics:** `Controller.initialize` now logs `Provider check failed` with the reason for Claude and Cursor; the UI only says "not installed" or "not connected".

## Native CLI accounts on the provider rows

Verified 2026-10-19 (live check, addresses masked): native rows show `account · version` instead of `Connected · version` when the CLI names its credential.
- **Muse:** `account/read` returns `{ state, label }`; `accountLogin` shows the label (the Meta account address), and `apiKey`/`envKey` show "API key". This account used `accountLogin`.
- **Antigravity:** `googleAccount()` reads `active` from `~/.gemini/google_accounts.json`; agy's own `cli.log` logs "OAuth: authenticated successfully as" that same address.
- **DeepSeek Harness:** "API key" (dsh runs on `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY`; its ACP surface reports no user).

Muse auto-updates (pitfall, 2026-10-19): its launcher installed 1.4.11-R6667.1 on its own, and Harness's pinned `@muse-code/sdk` 1.4.10 then refused the new schema fingerprint ("Unsupported Muse Code protocol"). Bumping the SDK to 1.4.11 restored it (5 models, account read). Expect this after each Muse release; the pin is deliberate, so bump the SDK and rerun `tests/muse.test.cjs` rather than skipping the fingerprint check.

## Muse Code CLI turn errors

Verified 2026-10-19 live: a Muse turn that ends `failed` carries the reason in `turn/completed` (`params.error.message`, `params.reason`); this account returned "API error 402 … Billing verification failed. Please check your payment method. (billing_error)" for every turn. `src/providers/muse.cjs` now shows that reason ("Muse Code CLI could not complete this turn: …"), not a generic failure. The 402 is the Muse account's billing, not Harness. Regression: `tests/muse.test.cjs` (`turnError` fixture option).
Free plan, headless (observed 2026-10-19, Muse 1.4.10-R6427.1, account without a monthly plan): the interactive TUI answers on `muse-spark-1.3-contributor`, but Muse's own headless `muse exec` returns the same 402 in the same folder with the same model and effort, and with `--contributor-mode on` or `off`. Harness drives Muse through `muse serve` (headless), so Muse needs billing on the account there. Driving the TUI instead would sidestep Meta's billing gate; not done. Environment ruled out (same day): `muse exec` returned 402 both with the agent shell's environment and with a clean environment rebuilt from the machine and user registry (what a new cmd window gets); no `META_API_KEY` is set. The user also saw the interactive TUI return the same 402 at times until cmd was restarted, so the account's billing state on Meta's side is in play; Harness cannot change it.
Profiled catalog and hidden sign-in (verified live 2026-10-19): Muse began returning `model/list` under provider profile `tbh` (the catalog's `profileId` and every row's). `MuseCLI.refresh` dropped every row with a `profileId`, found zero models, and marked Muse signed out. The plug then ran `muse login` with its output discarded, so its browser code never showed; it waited the 10-minute deadline, stuck on "Connecting…", while the user's own Muse CLI stopped answering. Now rows matching the catalog's own profile count, `session/setModel` passes that `profileId` (MSP `ModelSelection`), and `login(open)` opens the `https://*.meta.com` link Muse prints, as Claude's sign-in does (`src/main.cjs` passes `shell.openExternal`). A profile does not avoid the headless 402. Regressions in `tests/muse.test.cjs`.

## API providers: Harness's own agent loop

Pitfall (verified 2026-10-19): `SmartRouter.completeOnce` calls `providers.structured()` for every non-CLI choice, Codex included. When `structured()` stopped returning null for non-local providers, every Hub turn on a Codex model failed with "Provider is disabled or missing." `structured()` now returns null unless `capabilities(provider).api`. Regression: "Codex and CLI choices skip the API structured path…" in `tests/api-agent.test.cjs`. A refused model listing now names the provider and URL, and the Settings group shows that error once and waits for Renew instead of refetching on every redraw.

Verified 2026-10-19. API providers (LM Studio, DeepSeek API, any OpenAI-compatible endpoint) no longer run through Codex. Codex may be missing from the PC and from the provider list.

**How it runs.** `capabilities()` gives provider ids without `:` an `api` capability, so `sendCLI` runs them through `ApiAgent` (`src/providers/api-agent.cjs`).
- The loop streams `/chat/completions`, asking for usage in the final chunk.
- Its tools are `read_file`, `list_files`, `search` (bundled ripgrep), `write_file`, `edit_file` and `run_command` (PowerShell or bash), plus the Harness agent tools and `project_context` through `bridgeTool`.
- It emits Claude-style events, so items, Stop, usage and budgets work as for the native CLIs.
- Each chat's history is saved in `api-sessions/<id>.json` beside the agent's store and resumed with the CLI session id. DeepSeek `reasoning_content` is sent back only within its turn.

**Approvals** follow the Claude rules through Harness prompts:
- Reads inside the workspace run.
- Edits inside it run with Workspace access.
- Commands and anything outside the workspace ask.
- Full access runs everything.
- Containment is judged on real paths, so a link inside the workspace cannot reach outside it.
- An allow-for-session covers one exact command or file.

**Routing and availability.**
- Routing and Hub calls use `Providers.structured` directly for every API provider: JSON mode for hosted APIs, schema mode for local servers.
- `available()` no longer requires a Codex connection.

**Codex proxy removed (2026-10-19).** The local Responses proxy that once carried API providers into Codex (`Providers.start`/`forward`/`close` and the API branch of `config`) is deleted. `Providers.config()` now returns only Codex's OpenAI default, and `modelRequest` serves only `/chat/completions`.

**Limits:**
- No OS sandbox (Codex had one); safety comes from the approval prompts.
- No compaction or live correction: the `api` capability has `compact` and `steer` set to false.
- At most 100 tool steps per turn.

**Regressions:**
- `tests/api-agent.test.cjs`: tool loop, approvals by access level, outside reads, saved history.
- `tests/core.test.cjs`: "API providers run in Harness's own loop without Codex" (no Codex calls, chat continues, Stop).
- `tests/local-models.test.cjs`: JSON mode for hosted providers; direct `modelRequest` checks for image bytes, the context-limit error and Ollama cloud models.

## Local model memory and context

Verified 2026-10-10. `src/providers/local-models.cjs` discovers model-management APIs at the configured provider endpoint (preserving reverse-proxy prefixes and authentication).
