LM Studio supports load/unload and context sizing; Ollama uses a Harness-named preset derived from the downloaded model with `num_ctx` set, because OpenAI-compatible requests do not retain a one-off native context override. These small preset definitions reuse weights; the original model is unchanged. llama.cpp router mode supports load/unload, but context size is server configuration. Unsupported endpoints and hosted providers keep their existing inference path. No model downloads are started.

Workers and the routing classifier prepare local models before inference. The default is 32,768 tokens, capped by model limits and preserving larger loaded contexts. Both local inference paths share `Providers.modelRequest`, which rechecks after eviction, estimates request room (excluding image data), and can grow adjustable contexts up to 262,144 tokens or the model maximum. An HTTP context rejection reporting the required tokens gets one larger-context retry before the response is returned. This estimate is not a tokenizer; streaming errors, unavailable memory, server-fixed context and intrinsically oversized prompts can still require a smaller request or different model (`src/providers/api-agent.cjs`, `src/providers/providers.cjs`).

Structured calls from Hub (chat, coordination and compaction) and Smart routing use `Providers.structured` for detected local servers. They send system instructions, a bounded user prompt, optional images and an explicit JSON schema to `/v1/chat/completions`, without execution tools. LM Studio/Ollama receive `response_format.json_schema`; llama.cpp receives its `response_format.schema` form. The local Responses endpoint is not relied on for schema enforcement. JSON parsing and caller validation still fail closed: plain text, incomplete output and invalid Hub actions cannot become actions. The existing provider lock covers loading and the complete response, including cancellation. Unknown/hosted providers and subscription backends retain their existing paths. Verified 2026-10-10: the running LM Studio Gemma model returned valid JSON via the documented chat endpoint, and an isolated full Hub send of `hello` completed with no agent actions or cloud calls. This live check used synthetic history only. Offline tests cover schema formats, malformed/truncated replies, context growth/retry, Stop and hosted fallback. See [LM Studio structured output](https://lmstudio.ai/docs/developer/openai-compat/structured-output), [Ollama compatibility](https://docs.ollama.com/api/openai-compatibility) and [llama.cpp server](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/blob/master/tools/server/README.md).

All agents share a provider-level lock across model management and streaming inference so a reload cannot interrupt another Harness response. Local inference for one endpoint is serialized. Stop prevents sending after preparation; a reload already started finishes under the lock, since canceling HTTP cannot reliably cancel server allocation. Manual load/unload is disabled while any agent is busy. Unload frees model memory and preserves downloads and conversations; a later message loads again. The composer shows Load, Loaded (context in the tooltip) and Unload for a supported selected model, including the last local worker chosen by Auto. Status reads never load a model, refresh on focus and every ten seconds while visible, and show retry on failure (`ui/app.js`, `src/main.cjs`). Ollama's managed presets remain on disk, and its five-minute idle lifetime is retained.

Validation: `tests/local-models.test.cjs`, the local-context regression in `tests/core.test.cjs`, and the local model controls in `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`; pool entries `local-models` and `agents-ui`. API references: [LM Studio models](https://lmstudio.ai/docs/developer/rest/list), [LM Studio loading](https://lmstudio.ai/docs/developer/rest/load), [Ollama context compatibility](https://docs.ollama.com/api/openai-compatibility#setting-the-local-context-size), [Ollama preset creation](https://docs.ollama.com/api/create), and [llama.cpp server](https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/blob/master/tools/server/README.md).

Desktop exit preference (verified 2026-10-20): General > On Exit offers Close App or Stay on Tray (default). `src/runner.cjs` persists the desktop-only preference in `runner-window.json`; Close App invokes the existing main-process shutdown path, while tray mode hides the window and keeps work running. Browser settings hide this desktop-only control. The sidebar Settings gear uses an intrinsic-width hit area with padding (`ui/style.css`). Covered by `tests/runner.test.cjs` and `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`.

Settings layout (verified 2026-10-20): benchmark evidence and its existing import/apply/reset/refresh controls live in a dedicated Benchmarks tab, sharing the settings scroll area. Standard single-line inputs and selects reduce vertical padding to make their original line-plus-20px-padding-plus-border height 25% shorter without shrinking text; the usage threshold has a 12px label gap. Sources: `ui/index.html`, `ui/app.js`, `ui/style.css`; UI smoke checks tab loading, fit, and the numeric height ratio.

General settings spacing (verified 2026-10-20): Browser access labels and fields have a 12px gap, with 12px vertical spacing around its action buttons. (2026-10-22: the user removed Settings' Find project context button and dialog, the workspace and access fields, and the background usage threshold. Bots' own project-search tool and the Project search ranking setting stay.)


Send now and live input (verified 2026-10-20): queued-message actions use Send now for every provider. Codex keeps native turn steering; direct Claude turns accept same-mode user follow-ups through their open stream-json stdin with `--replay-user-messages` and UUID acknowledgement. The saved queue is retained until acknowledgement, and interrupted/uncertain delivery is not automatically resent. Automatic failover is disabled once a live correction is submitted, avoiding a replay of only the original prompt. Additional queued messages drain in order; model changes, internal agent results and delegated assignments remain outside this automatic path. Delegated changes retain the existing revision workflow. Other direct providers and Hub use Stop, wait for the old turn to finish, then submit with conversation history and existing cleanup/admission checks. A later Stop cancels this restart; unconfirmed cleanup leaves the message queued with an error. This is a stop-and-resume fallback, not a claim that those providers expose native steering. Composer sending is disabled while delivery is pending, during compaction, or when another non-Hub conversation is busy. Sources: `src/controller.cjs`, `src/providers/claude.cjs`, `ui/app.js`; tests: `tests/core.test.cjs`, `tests/claude-permissions.test.cjs`, `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`, `tests/agents.test.cjs`, `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs`. Installed Claude help confirms the replay/input flags; provider timing was tested with scripted streams, not paid live inference.


Provider live-input audit (2026-10-20; documentation/source inspection, no live inference):
- Codex: native `turn/steer` is already wired in `src/controller.cjs`.
- Claude: streaming input supports multiple messages and interruption; Harness now forwards direct follow-ups, but live timing has not been measured. Reference: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/streaming-vs-single-mode .
- Muse: **native queue/steer/replace is available**, not just stop/restart. Installed `@muse-code/sdk` 1.4.11 declares `IfBusy = "queue" | "steer" | "replace"` in `dist/src/msp.d.ts`; our `src/providers/muse.cjs` sends one turn then tears down its host. Reference: https://meta-models.github.io/muse-code-sdk/next/generated/msp/methods/turn-start/ .
- Cursor: local TypeScript SDK `run.steer(text)` returns `complete_delivered` or `revert_to_followup`; cloud/detached handles fall back. Our `src/providers/cursor.cjs` uses ACP, whose documented interface exposes `session/cancel`; do not assume SDK steering is available through that adapter or silently switch authentication/billing. References: https://cursor.com/docs/sdk/typescript and https://prod.cursor.com/docs/cli/acp .
- DeepSeek Harness: its published ACP contract allows one in-flight prompt per session and supports `session/cancel`, then subsequent prompts. A persistent cancel/re-prompt path can improve our process-kill fallback, but native mid-turn injection is not established through this interface. Reference: https://github.com/deepseek-ai/deepseek-harness/blob/master/packages/acp/acp/README.md .
- Antigravity: documented stdin stream-json accepts multiple sequential prompts in one process, one result per turn. Our adapter already uses this transport but ends after one result. Native mid-turn injection is not established by those docs. Reference: https://antigravity.google/docs/cli/headless/ .
- API-backed models (including OpenRouter/local servers): `src/providers/api-agent.cjs` owns the tool/model loop. Corrections can be queued between model calls; changing an in-flight chat-completions request is not part of that endpoint. This is an implementation opportunity, not current native steering. Jev is routing/auxiliary inference in this integration, rather than an independent worker transport.
The previous universal stop-and-resume fallback is therefore not the best available implementation for Muse, Cursor SDK, persistent Antigravity sessions, or Harness-owned API loops. This audit changes documentation only.

Native Send now per provider (built 2026-10-20 by Claude from the audit above; fixture-tested, no live inference). `src/providers/capabilities.cjs` declares `liveQueue` (the running turn accepts follow-ups into the provider's own queue) and `liveSteer` (Send now applies within the running turn); `Controller.sendCLI` hands the run an input callback for either, and `queuedMessage` passes `{ steer }` so Send now steers where the provider can. `flushLiveQueue` feeds ordinary queued messages only to `liveQueue` providers, so a steer-only provider is never interrupted by them. A message counts as delivered only when the provider acknowledges it; otherwise it stays queued (with `uncertain` only for timeouts and disconnects).

| Provider | Follow-ups while working | Send now | Delivered when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Codex | sent after the turn | native `turn/steer` | Codex accepts the steer |
| Claude | its stream-json queue (answered after the current step; the row reads "Waiting") | send-now interrupt: the current step ends, background tasks keep running | replay acknowledgement (UUID) |
| API / local models | next model call | next model call | the message goes into a model request; one still waiting when the turn ends returns to the Harness queue |
| Muse | `turn/start` `ifBusy: queue` (a new turn the run also waits for) | `ifBusy: steer` (same turn id) | steer: `turn/start` acknowledged; queued: its turn completed (a run that fails first leaves it queued as uncertain: "closed before the queued message ran") |
| DeepSeek Harness | after the turn (one prompt at a time) | `session/cancel`, open approvals answered cancelled, the cancelled prompt's commands swept by `stopStartedSince`, then a new prompt in the same session and process | the replacement prompt is sent |
| Cursor | after the turn | same as DeepSeek over its ACP connection | the replacement prompt is sent |
| Antigravity | after the turn | stop and resend with history (fallback) | — |

- Scope: direct chats. Delegated assignments keep the revision workflow (`Agents.revise`: Codex `turn/steer`, otherwise stop and replace) with its ownership, budget and cleanup guarantees; `flushLiveQueue` and the live path skip delegated sessions.
- Antigravity keeps the fallback because its stream has no event that confirms a second prompt was received; a write followed by a crash would leave the message's fate unknown.
- Risk to check live: the DeepSeek/Cursor sweep stops every process the host started after the cancelled prompt began. A long-lived worker that dsh or cursor-agent first starts during a tool call would be stopped too (fixtures have no real process tree, so they take the no-sweep path). If a live run shows that, narrow the sweep to the step's command processes.
- Queue list (2026-10-21, at the user's request): pressing Send now sets `message.sending`, and the queue list hides that message and its buttons at once, though it stays in `session.queue` until the provider takes it (`Controller.queuedMessage`). The flag is cleared in `finally`, so a failed send shows again as Paused with its error; a restart clears it, and a send cut off by the app closing shows as unconfirmed. Claude finishing its turn without live input keeps the message queued (sent next), so it shows again there. Tests: "live delivery keeps a queued correction…" and "a message the app closed on while sending…" (`tests/core.test.cjs`), smoke "A sent queued message leaves the queue list".
- A cancelled step that does not end within 15 s refuses Send now with a plain error (`stepStopped` in `src/providers/native-cli.cjs`); unconfirmed cleanup does the same. Neither starts a second writer.
- Each replacement prompt gets its own approval signal, so an Allow answered after Send now cannot approve the new prompt's tools (DeepSeek captures the signal per request; Cursor checks it after the answer).
- The Send now tooltip states what happens (`sendNowTitle` in `ui/app.js`); the UI now applies the API capabilities to provider ids without a colon (`providerCapabilities.api`), which previously fell back to Codex's.
- Tests: `tests/api-agent.test.cjs` (next model call, delivery only then, return to queue on failure), `tests/deepseek.test.cjs` and `tests/cursor-permissions.test.cjs` (cancel and re-prompt in the same session, late Allow refused; both A/B-checked), `tests/muse.test.cjs` (steer and queue; the fixture now answers a re-sent `turn/start` idempotently, as Muse does), `tests/core.test.cjs` (steer vs queue routing), `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` (tooltips).
- Verified live (2026-10-20, `.scratch/deepseek-sendnow-live.cjs`, dsh 0.2.1-rc.2, `deepseek-flash`): dsh started a 2-minute command that recorded its pid; Send now was acknowledged after about 5 s; the command's process was gone, the same dsh process (same pid) took the replacement prompt, which ran a tool (`node -e "console.log(41+1)"`) and answered "42". So the sweep left dsh's own workers alone in this case.
- Not verified live: Claude's acknowledgement timing during long tool steps, Cursor's real `session/cancel`, the API loop against a real endpoint, and Muse (billing-blocked on this account; the user will not subscribe to Muse (decided 2026-10-21), so it gets no live check and blocks nothing).

## Shared MCP connectors

Release review regressions (2026-10-23): `Connectors.closeEntry` retains pending cleanup separately from active connections, so Stop followed by Disconnect or runner shutdown waits for process cleanup; failed cleanup remains a blocker. `saveNow` checks for concurrent JSON edits before writing secrets and restores encrypted credential files if the settings write fails. Exact cases in `tests/connectors.test.cjs`: “Disconnect and shutdown wait for cleanup already started by Stop”, “a Settings save cannot overwrite a file changed while connection cleanup awaited” (including a token edit), and “a failed settings write restores the previous encrypted credentials”. Pool `shared-connectors`: 26 passed; the first two cases were reproduced as failures before their fixes.

Cross-platform test timing (2026-10-23): “local MCP processes use isolated bot workspaces, minimal env and owned cleanup” now waits for Node to reap the exited children before asserting their PIDs are gone. Linux Node 22.23.3 reproduced the immediate check failing with `/proc/<pid>/stat` state `Z`; the corrected connector suite passes all 26 cases on Linux and Windows. Windows CI also exceeded the five-second whole-test limit in multi-bot fixtures and the one-second result-delivery poll in `tests/coordinator.test.cjs`; their bounded allowances are now 15 and 5 seconds respectively, with behavior assertions unchanged.

Provider error visibility (2026-10-23): the additional Settings tab can wrap the tab strip and reduce visible body height. `ui/app.js` routes connection, install and refresh errors through `showProviderError`, which brings the message into view when Providers is open. `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` checks the complete error bounds in a 480-pixel-high window and preserves per-tab scroll positions; `.scratch/provider-rows.png` was inspected with the error fully visible.

Bundled defaults (2026-10-23): `src/connectors.cjs` installs Context7, Microsoft Learn and OpenAI Docs once, using stable IDs and a `defaultsVersion: 1` marker in `connectors.json`. The migration matches existing IDs, case-insensitive names or endpoints (ignoring a trailing slash), retaining their settings and encrypted credentials. Missing defaults are enabled with per-call approval and no authentication. Startup does not connect to them. Later edits, disablement and removal survive restart; retain `defaultsVersion` when editing the JSON manually. The store allows 53 entries so a full legacy 50-entry configuration can receive all three without losing anything. Exact regressions in `tests/connectors.test.cjs`: “default documentation connectors persist once and respect later edits and removals”, “default connector upgrade preserves existing settings and credentials without duplicates”, “default connector upgrade keeps a full legacy store and rejects corrupt migration metadata”, and “default documentation connectors use the shared discovery and approval path for every backend”. They use isolated stores and local MCP fixtures, with no public requests. `tests/connector-ui-check.cjs` checks all three appear in Settings before exercising removal and custom connectors.

Public documentation endpoints verified 2026-10-23: Context7 (`https://mcp.context7.com/mcp`, 2 tools), Microsoft Learn (`https://learn.microsoft.com/api/mcp`, 3 tools), and OpenAI Docs (`https://developers.openai.com/mcp`, 5 tools) accept unauthenticated HTTP connections with the normal per-call approval policy. The real `createRunner` wiring in `src/runner.cjs` supplies the same manager to Main Bot, normal bot chats, delegated execution and sub-bots. Live case: discover and call `resolve-library-id` with `libraryName: "Electron"`, `microsoft_docs_search` with `query: "Windows CreateFile file sharing modes"`, and `search_openai_docs` with `query: "Responses API tools", limit: 1` through `Controller.bridgeTool`; require one approval and non-error documentation content per call. All 27 calls passed across nine isolated controller contexts (main, six normal bots, delegated execution and a sub-bot). Provider startup was stubbed; this verifies shared connector wiring and real endpoints, not live model tool selection. The 20-case `shared-connectors` pool also passed. These network probes are manual, not unattended regression tests.

Live configuration reload (verified 2026-10-23): `Connectors.list`, discovery, tool calls and settings mutations reread `connectors.json` through the existing serialized mutation queue. Settings' two-second refresh picks up external additions, edits and removal without a runner restart. A complete version-1 document with stable unique IDs is required; UTF-8 BOM is accepted, and plaintext secret fields are rejected. Invalid, partial or missing files preserve loaded records and block new calls/saves until repaired. Unchanged records retain object identity and connections. Changed records close their clients and invalidate old approvals; calls recheck after a slow handshake. A pre-write comparison prevents Settings from overwriting an external edit made during asynchronous cleanup. Sources: `src/connectors.cjs`, `src/controller/connectors.cjs`, `src/runner-commands.cjs`, `ui/connectors.js`. Exact regressions: `tests/connectors.test.cjs` tests “external connector additions load through Settings and bot discovery without restarting”, “partial, invalid and missing connector files keep the last valid configuration and recover”, “external edits revoke old approvals and disabled connectors stop pending calls”, “a Settings save cannot overwrite a file changed while connection cleanup awaited”, and “a JSON edit during a slow handshake is checked again before executing a tool”. `tests/connector-ui-check.cjs` writes JSON while Settings stays open and verifies automatic display and error recovery; screenshot inspected at `.scratch/connectors-live-reload.png`. Pools: `shared-connectors`, `agents-ui`. Verification uses isolated local servers, without altering the user's PE configuration.

`src/connectors.cjs` owns the global connector configuration and MCP SDK clients; `src/runner.cjs` shares this manager across primary, normal and delegated execution controllers. Settings and the authenticated browser use the same `connectorList`, `connectorSave` and `connectorAction` commands. Model tools cannot change connector settings. `ui/connectors.js` manages the Settings tab without copying credentials back into the renderer.

`connector_tools` discovers bounded tool catalogs; `connector_call` validates input against the server schema and executes through `src/controller/connectors.cjs`. Every supported native CLI receives the existing session MCP bridge; API workers receive the same functions directly. Legacy `helperTools: false` cannot hide shared connectors. Codex keeps existing provider MCP connections separately. Native MCP responses retain their content blocks; API workers consume tool results as text/JSON. Resources, prompts and legacy HTTP/SSE transport are outside this first version. Reconnect explicitly after changing a server catalog.

Permissions live in Harness. The default requires one approval per call at every access level. A user may opt a connector into automatic calls for Full-access bots; Workspace and Ask still require approval. Cancelled-result summaries cannot invoke external tools, and restricted tasks cannot inherit Full access from their chat. Captured turn/submission/access and connector identity are checked after approval and again after opening the connection. Stop rejects pending approvals and cancels only calls owned by that controller's conversations. Failed/cancelled calls are never retried automatically because a remote write may already have completed.

`src/connectors/auth.cjs` encrypts credentials with OS `safeStorage`, binds files to the connector endpoint/program and OAuth tokens to their issuer, validates callback state, and uses the SDK's PKCE/refresh flow. Revoked providers cannot save late tokens. The current headless runner has no key store, so saved-secret/OAuth connections fail closed there. OAuth callbacks are on loopback and require the browser on the runner computer. Only HTTPS or loopback HTTP URLs are accepted, and HTTP transport redirects are refused.

`src/connectors/transport.cjs` implements stdio transport with the SDK protocol schema and Harness `spawnOwned`/`stopTree` cleanup. Programs receive a minimal SDK environment plus explicitly saved variables; each bot conversation gets its own connection/process in its workspace unless the user configured another directory. Local MCP programs still run as the OS user. Stderr is drained without copying potentially secret diagnostics into chat. Connect/disconnect waits for owned cleanup; controls remain usable after a connection error.

Regression cases verified 2026-10-22: `tests/connectors.test.cjs` (15 cases, pool `shared-connectors`) covers HTTP/stdin protocols, endpoint-bound encryption, unavailable key protection, OAuth state/PKCE/refresh/revocation, schema rejection before dispatch, independent bot cancellation, isolated process environment/workspaces/cleanup, all-backend approval dispatch, stale approvals and API-loop execution. It also checks the bundled native MCP adapter does not double-encode connector content. `tests/agent-tools-smoke.cjs` exercises installed Codex against a local Responses fixture, through discovery, a Harness approval and an actual HTTP MCP call, with no paid inference. `tests/connector-ui-check.cjs`, included in desktop smoke, covers create/edit/connect/disable/remove, secret clearing and narrow layout. Third-party account integrations remain unverified.

## Persistence and compatibility guardrails

`src/session-store.cjs` is the shared persistence boundary for controllers, orphan-worker detection and startup backups. It retains version-1 JSON snapshots and appends flushed metadata/transcript deltas to `.journal`; unchanged saves write nothing. Checkpoints flush the temporary file before atomic replacement, then truncate the journal. A snapshot revision prevents replay after a crash between replacement and truncation. A torn last record is discarded; complete malformed records and revision gaps stop loading without modifying evidence. Windows flushes files; POSIX also flushes directory entries. The runner remains the single writer.

Normal close and the 8 MiB/1,000-record threshold checkpoint the store. A failed automatic checkpoint is logged while the already-flushed journal stays authoritative. Serialization still examines all in-memory records; this removes repeated full-history disk writes, not all CPU cost or in-memory history growth. Keep JSON and journal together during backup; `backupStores` creates standalone recovery copies.

`ui/agent-state.js` supplies the same sub-bot ownership and inheritance rules to renderer and runner, including fail-closed ancestry. `ui/report-format.js` owns legacy report parsing. These modules, the store and provider contracts are checked by strict `tsconfig.json`; `npm run check` is required in CI. This is incremental checking, not a claim that the remaining controller/agent/UI orchestration is typed.

Regression cases (2026-10-22): `tests/session-store.test.cjs` covers restart replay, edits/deletions/order, tiny appends to a 2.5 MB history, torn versus corrupt records, checkpoint crash windows, failed flush/rename retries and standalone backup recovery of journal-only workers. `tests/shared-contracts.test.cjs` covers queued/completed ownership, missing/cyclic access inheritance, report markers and removed provider flags. `.github/workflows/provider-compatibility.yml` schedules latest Codex/Claude interface checks, the existing local-Responses MCP smoke and adapter regressions weekly without inference. Other installed providers are included by `tools/check-providers.cjs`; absent optional CLIs are explicit skips.

Verified 2026-10-22: `npm test` completed with 881 passes, 4 Linux `/proc` skips and no failures; all 3 new pool entries and strict checks passed. Desktop smoke and installed Codex 0.163.0 against the local Responses fixture passed. Installed CLI contracts passed for Codex 0.163.0, Claude 2.1.306, Cursor 2026.10.19-6bbf2c4, Muse 1.4.11, Antigravity 1.4.2 and DeepSeek 0.2.1-rc.2. Scheduled Linux execution and paid provider behavior were not exercised locally.

## Per-workspace rules and wiki (2026-10-25, the user's plan)

Each workspace owns its rules and wiki; Harness reads them and never writes them. Rules are the root `AGENTS.md`, with `CLAUDE.md` and `GEMINI.md` as one-line `@AGENTS.md` imports; the wiki is `docs/wiki`. Harness's own per-workspace data (lessons, trial and kept rules, setups, comparisons, chat-scan notes, skill versions) stays in `%APPDATA%/Phasma Harness/Wikis/<name>-<hash>/wiki`, decoupled from the pages.

Source: `src/workspace/project-instructions.cjs` (`projectRules`, `loadsRulesItself`), `Controller.projectContextInstructions` and `runSetup` in `src/controller.cjs`, `withoutPersonalSetup` in `src/providers/claude.cjs` (only the home `CLAUDE.md` is excluded now), the Codex thread config (no `project_doc_max_bytes: 0`; the router keeps it), `src/workspace/context-search.cjs` (walks `docs/wiki` with readdir, so a git-ignored wiki is searched), `src/controller/trials.cjs` and `src/trials.cjs` (`kept-rules.md`, `keptRulesText`), `comparedRule` in `src/controller/comparison.cjs`, `src/workspace/job-workspaces.cjs` (copies `docs/wiki`; Apply skips any `PhasmaHarness.md`). The automatic wiki-maintenance turn (`maintainWiki`) is gone; the Propose button remains.

Native loading, checked 2026-10-25 with marker files (one distinct code per file, "quote every MARKER line, do not read files"):
- Claude Code 2.1.310: `claude -p "/context"` with Harness's `withoutPersonalSetup()` flags lists the workspace `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md` once (through the import) and not `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md`. Without a `CLAUDE.md` it loads no `AGENTS.md`, so Harness passes it then.
- Codex 0.164.0 (`codex exec -m gpt-6-luna`): loads `AGENTS.md` in a folder under the trusted home and in `C:/Users/Public` with no trust entry; only an explicitly untrusted project skips it. Default cap 32 KiB, truncated silently (`codex-rs/core/src/agents_md.rs`).
- Antigravity (`agy -p`, also with a Harness-style `--agent` file): loads `AGENTS.md` and `GEMINI.md`; with `GEMINI.md` = `@AGENTS.md` the code appears once.
- Muse: `muse exec` warned that `AGENTS.md` "exists, but the workspace is untrusted, so it is skipped"; Harness passes it. Inference itself failed with billing 402.
- DeepSeek CLI: its `acp` profile (`dsh --profile acp --dump-config`) includes `@deepseek-ai/dsh-agent-instructions` with `maxBytes: 65536`, which reads `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md`; the live test below passed both turns.

Pitfalls: the running older build recreates a missing `PhasmaHarness.md` on a writable turn, so delete old entries only after restarting on this build; Codex threads and Cursor's reused sessions may keep the `AGENTS.md` they loaded; `.gitignore`d wikis are invisible to `rg --files`, hence the readdir walk; PhasmaEngine's `tools/precommit_validate.py` now checks `AGENTS.md` (under 32 KiB) and `docs/wiki/tools/lint.sh`. The Jev "Check wiki updates" setting only judged automatic wiki turns; it was removed on 2026-10-25 with the whole Settings → Wiki tab (`src/workspace/wiki-assess.cjs`, `wikiAssessment`, the `wikiFolder` agent setting, the `workspaceWiki`/`openWorkspaceWiki` IPC and the two tips that pointed there), at the user's request. `project_context` still searches the workspace's `docs/wiki`, the Propose button stays, and the decision trace now counts turns only. Old `wikiAssessment` values in saved settings are ignored.

Migration the same day: each repo's `PhasmaHarness.md` became `AGENTS.md` (Harness boilerplate and `<engine-wiki>` placeholders removed), the AppData pages went back to `docs/wiki` with absolute AppData links made relative, ATH's Crooked Ink skill went back to `.cursor/skills`, and four Harness pages plus four 2026-10-24 group-chat notes joined this repo's wiki. Backups: `%APPDATA%/Phasma Harness/backups/per-workspace-move-2026-10-25` (and the moved AppData pages under `AppData-pages`).

Test case (2026-10-25): `node --test tests/project-instructions.test.cjs tests/wiki-store.test.cjs tests/comparisons.test.cjs` and the full `npm test` (1051 tests: 1047 passed, 4 Linux-only skips); live `node tests/project-instructions-live.cjs <data dir> codex claude-cli cursor-cli`. Live result 2026-10-25: Claude (claude-sonnet-4-6) passed both turns; Codex (gpt-5.6-terra) and Cursor (composer-2.5) passed turn 1, and on turn 2 of the same chat still quoted the `AGENTS.md` code from the chat's start while reading the new wiki code. They keep the `AGENTS.md` they loaded, as they do outside Harness; a new chat picks up an edit. With `--git-ignored` (rule files and `docs/wiki` ignored, as in the user's repos) Codex and Cursor again passed turn 1 and DeepSeek (deepseek-v4-flash) passed both turns. Antigravity needs `--allow-full-access` and was not run.

## Shared workspace instructions (2026-10-23)

Superseded 2026-10-25 by "Per-workspace rules and wiki" above.


`src/workspace/project-instructions.cjs` now reads one `PhasmaHarness.md` from the actual workspace. Writable initialization consolidates the root's recognized legacy agent entries, its `.cursor/rules/*.mdc` and `.github/copilot-instructions.md`; identical mirrors appear once. Subfolders are not searched (2026-10-25). Originals are retained by automatic initialization. Read-only turns read the existing rules without creating a file. Once the canonical entry exists, later legacy edits are ignored. Ordinary unlinked text files only, with a 64 KiB canonical limit; oversized collections fail before writing. Explicit migration/removal needs a verified backup and review of imported references.

`Controller.projectContextInstructions` feeds the current entry and active WikiStore index to Main Bot and, through `workerInstructions`, direct and delegated workers. Codex sets `project_doc_max_bytes=0` and injects refreshed developer guidance when reloading a resumed thread; API workers replace their system message while retaining conversation history. Claude uses temporary prompt files, `--system-prompt-snapshot off`, and `--append-subagent-system-prompt-file` (requires 2.1.261+) and excludes native project entries. Temporary files are removed after success or failure. Cursor/native adapters retain their shared instruction injection. Standalone CLIs do not discover PhasmaHarness.md automatically, and these instructions do not create a filesystem write barrier.

Trials, chat-scan, reflection, comparisons and wiki proposals use the canonical entry. Old Undo cards initialize the shared entry when necessary and map INSTRUCTIONS.md to PhasmaHarness.md without recreating the old file; on-demand rules have no file path. Batch copies freeze the entry with other inputs, including ignored entries, and comparison setup imports legacy rules when the snapshot has no canonical entry yet. See `src/controller/{trials,comparison,reflection,chat-scan,task-gate}.cjs`, `src/comparisons.cjs` and `src/workspace/job-workspaces.cjs`.

Storage remains `<workspace>/PhasmaHarness.md` plus `%APPDATA%/Phasma Harness/Wikis/<workspace-id>/wiki` for installed Windows builds. Neither is in the installation directory, and NSIS retains AppData on uninstall. There is no new Workspaces rename or instructions/ directory. Backup remains necessary for manual deletion or disk loss.

2026-10-24: Main Bot's workspace can be a parent such as `repos`, so `Controller.projectContextInstructions` imports only that root's legacy entries (including its own Cursor and Copilot rules); a canonical entry still takes precedence. Workers retain scoped subfolder imports, but `legacyInstructionFiles` prunes nested `.git` directories/files and nested `PhasmaHarness.md` entries before enumeration, not after a recursive scan. Generated legacy `workspace-data` copies are excluded too. Root-only loading never creates a shortened canonical entry: worker initialization preserves the project's scoped rules. The reported parent folder previously collected 146 files / 273,864 bytes and failed consolidation before delegation; root-only loading returns a 1,668-byte prompt and creates no file there. Regression cases in `tests/project-instructions.test.cjs` cover forbidden-directory enumeration, preservation of canonical files, genuine consolidation overflow, links, scoped rules, and failed publication. `tests/coordinator.test.cjs` checks `Main Bot in a repositories parent starts without importing its child projects`. Superseded 2026-10-25 at the user's request: workers are root-only too, so no subfolder is ever imported and no "Workspace scope" sections are generated (`legacyInstructionFiles` lists only the root, `.cursor/rules` and `.github`; the `recursive` option is gone). On `C:\Users\Christos\repos` the read-only fallback had walked about 2,250 folders on every send and imported two copied engine `INSTRUCTIONS.md` files from a snapshot folder; it now lists the root in about 1 ms and imports nothing. Existing `PhasmaHarness.md` files keep sections imported earlier. Pool: `main-instruction-scope`. Windows verification: `npm test` passed 967 tests with 4 Linux-only skips; `main-instruction-scope` and `wiki-import-and-chat-guards` passed 51 checks. The saved chat's retry completed and contains one completed worker result; composer locking/unlocking still needs visual confirmation.

Verified 2026-10-23 for v0.3.8: `npm test` — 922 tests, 918 passed, 4 Linux-only process-test skips, no failures. `npm run check`, pool `wiki-import-and-chat-guards`, catalog validation, Windows installer tests, Electron desktop smoke and the real-Codex/local-Responses MCP smoke passed. New regression cases cover incomplete migration writes, legacy learned-rule retention and Undo, oversize comparison variants, old comparison-copy exclusions, and Main Bot instruction refresh. The migration publishes complete content exclusively; learning/reflection and setup fingerprints read the same body as workers before a writable turn creates the file.

Opt-in `tests/project-instructions-live.cjs` ran real provider turns using independent random codes in the workspace entry and AppData wiki. Codex 0.163.0 (gpt-5.6-terra low), Cursor 2026.10.22-27dcf5f (composer-2.5), DeepSeek 0.2.1-rc.2 (deepseek-v4-flash) and Antigravity 1.4.2 (gemini-3.8-flash-low) each returned both codes initially and after changing both files in the same chat. The Windows v0.3.8 package was built and installed over the locally patched v0.3.7 after checking 102 session stores for active turns and gracefully closing the app. All 49 snapshotted instruction/wiki files (including isolated test fixtures) were byte-identical afterwards. The install exceeded the test wrapper's 60-second wait; it then exited, installed files were checked, and the packaged app started on Electron 44.9.3 without startup errors. A real Codex turn through the installed Controller used the actual PhasmaEngine shared rules and read its AppData index and imported rig-panel page. Original 12 instruction/rule files and 35 wiki files remain hash-verified in AppData backups. A real uninstall was not tested.

Release validation notes: Electron 44.9.3 removes the dependency audit's pointer-lock attribution advisory; the final audit reports zero vulnerabilities. Electron Builder still reports the existing disabled-ASAR configuration and deduplicated dependency references. ### Workspace skill comparisons (verified 2026-10-23)

The existing Hub `compare` tool accepts `target: "skill"`, an existing Harness `skill` name, a workspace-local `candidatePath`, a task and a short change summary. The candidate is the complete package: `SKILL.md`, scripts and other resources; omission removes a file from that version. Checks and Workspace/Full access are required. Candidates must be in an ignored, untracked directory inside a Git project, also absent from HEAD. `JobWorkspaces.prepare()` rechecks this before capture: neither the baseline nor the changed run receives the editable candidate directory. This extends instruction comparisons without adding an autonomous skill author or exploration calls.

`src/skill-packages.cjs` stores revisions and an atomically replaced `active.json` beside the canonical AppData wiki (`Wikis/<workspace-id>/skills`). Every comparison run gets separate frozen copies of every skill, including binary assets. Links, special files, missing/invalid UTF-8 entries, packages over 256 files / 8 MiB, and entries over 64 KiB are refused. The installed `skills/` tree stays unchanged; the existing installer retains AppData (`package.json`, `deleteAppDataOnUninstall: false`). Reads create no skill store. Invalid manifests or missing/damaged overrides fall back to bundled skills with one notice per distinct problem in the chat; damaged files are preserved. Mutating a broken store still fails closed.

`Controller.skillDirectories()` selects the frozen run or the source workspace's active package. `workerInstructions()` uses each package's frontmatter description and path for every provider. Normal turns and experiments both use this on-demand index; experiments no longer force the skill body into the prompt. Description-only changes are effective; unused metadata-only changes are rejected before any snapshot is written. `router_read_output {skill}` returns raw source with frontmatter and `{{SKILL_DIR}}` intact, plus the package directory separately. Preserve placeholders while editing; resolve them against that directory when executing helpers. Candidates containing an old resolved package path are refused. Adopt/Undo changes are picked up on subsequent turns, including existing Codex chats. Setup fingerprints hash supporting files as well as the entry; other changed skills, changed attempts, missing evidence or mutated snapshots prevent a fair result.

A skill that helped is adopted through the existing comparison settlement and card. Adoption and Undo preserve intervening edits and recheck the owner's current workspace permission ceiling. The previous revision ID is preserved exactly; Undo to bundled removes the manifest entry so future Harness updates apply. Finished run snapshots are pruned, retaining active packages, in-flight snapshots and Undo references in the existing 50-comparison history. An older card loses its Undo button with an expiry explanation if its revisions are removed. Failed preparation removes partial packages; failed dispatch stops partial workers before cleanup. Unreadable history defers deletion until a save backs up the original bytes and reconstructs available records from chat cards. Recovery retains potentially referenced revisions within the 50-comparison history window. A failed wiki save preserves Undo references from chat cards.

The adopted `test-pool` package changes worker guidance and manually invoked helpers. It deliberately does not replace Harness's bundled automatic verification runner (`src/controller/task-gate.cjs`) or external CLI Stop hooks: a candidate must not replace the checker judging its work.

Exact deterministic regression cases on 2026-10-23: `tests/skill-packages.test.cjs` covers chained Undo, bundled inheritance, corrupted stores, zero-write reads/no-op rejection, metadata activation, raw-source/helper round trips, 54 adoptions with bounded retention, busy/unsaved history protection, expired cards and partial dispatch failures. `tests/coordinator.test.cjs` ("Hub compare starts") executes bot/project/skill comparisons through dispatch, candidate-free copies, normal provider input, checks, adoption, snapshot cleanup and Undo. `tests/core.test.cjs` covers real controller sends through damaged stores and resumed Codex/Claude/Cursor instruction refresh; transports are fake. `tests/job-workspaces.test.cjs` removes the candidate's ignore rule between planning and capture and verifies refusal. `tests/testpool-harness.test.cjs` verifies that an adopted helper cannot replace host verification. `.testpool/catalog.json` registers `skill-comparisons`. UI smoke verifies Undo and expiry; `.scratch/skill-comparison-expired.png` was visually inspected. No live-model quality comparison or installer upgrade was run.

Full verification after the review fixes: `npm test` passed 942 of 946 tests (4 Linux-only skips on Windows, 0 failures), `npm run check` and `npm run smoke` passed, and the `skill-comparisons` pool entry passed all 26 focused cases. UI logs contain the expected injected error fixtures; Git reports only LF-to-CRLF checkout warnings. Detailed results: `.scratch/skill-review-verification-2026-10-23.md`.

Release-runner selection (2026-10-23): `.github/workflows/ci.yml` pins Windows tests and packaging to `windows-2022`. The rolling `windows-latest` resolved to `windows-2025-vs2026` image `20261021.278.1`; release run `39995466400` timed out the first PDF/DOCX read at 60 seconds on two attempts, while the independent master run and local document tests passed. The image-specific cause remains unverified. A separate current-Windows document gate now covers this path before packaging, rather than relying only on the pin. Image reference: [GitHub Windows Server 2022 runner](https://github.com/actions/runner-images/blob/main/images/windows/Windows2022-Readme.md).

Document limiter startup (2026-10-23): `tools/build-document-job.cjs` compiles `src/document-job.cs` during dependency setup (`tools/fetch.cjs`), source-checkout reader startup and `npm run dist`. Source startup builds a missing or outdated DLL after a pull without reinstalling dependencies; installed apps omit the builder script and use only the shipped DLL. The packaged `tools/bin/document-job.dll` is loaded by `src/document-job.ps1`; first attachment reads no longer compile C#. The 768 MiB OS limit, 15-second parsing timeout and separate 60-second startup deadline remain. `src/documents.cjs` retains bounded startup errors and stops a stalled helper before releasing its waiting worker PID, then allows a fresh retry. Exact deterministic cases in `tests/documents.test.cjs`: "Windows limiter startup timeout reaps the helper and untouched parser", "Windows limiter startup errors retain their cause", and "document memory limiter build caches unchanged source". The local cold PDF read measured 639 ms; this does not establish the cause or performance on the newer hosted Windows image.

Document limiter startup on CI (fixed 2026-10-28):
- **Symptom.** The v0.3.18 master CI run (`40756605150`, commit `7b1a516`) failed twice on windows-2022. "Document reader memory limiter startup timed out" hit the 60 s deadline, while the tag run of the same commit passed.
- **How slow it was.** In 13 passing runs, every test that starts a limiter took 20–27 s on Windows (35–51 s for the first), while the containment helper, launched with the same PowerShell flags but the full environment, took 2–7 s.
- **Cause (proven on windows-2022, throwaway-branch runs `40767146899`, `40768854757`, `40769529968`):**
  - The limiter starts Windows PowerShell with `gameEnvironment()`, which drops `PSModuleAnalysisCachePath`. GitHub's image points that variable at a prebuilt module index (`C:\PSModuleAnalysisCachePath\ModuleAnalysisCache`).
  - `Add-Type` and `Join-Path` are cmdlets, so PowerShell indexed every installed module before running them.
  - Stripped environment: 28.4/23.3/23.2/23.3 s. Full environment: 0.3 s each. Stripped plus only `PSModuleAnalysisCachePath`: 0.5 and 0.3 s. Full minus only that variable: 23.1 s. Real-time Defender is off on the runner.
- **Fix.** `src/document-job.ps1` loads the DLL with `[Reflection.Assembly]::LoadFrom([IO.Path]::Combine(...))` and uses no cmdlets, so no module lookup happens in any environment. On windows-2022 with the stripped environment that start took 0.2 s in all 4 rounds. Locally all 15 `tests/documents.test.cjs` tests pass, including the decompression-bomb memory cap.
- **Cold first read on CI (2026-10-29).** With startup fast, a runner's first PDF read ("Cold PDF read" diagnostic) is pdf.js loading off a cold disk plus the parse: 4.7-8.3 s on windows-2022 in 5 runs, over the 15 s parse timeout once (run 40801001478). Later reads take about 0.6 s. The first test of `tests/documents.test.cjs` now reads with `timeoutMs: 60000`; timeout behaviour has its own test. The product's 15 s window also covers that cold load (unchanged).


### Skill comparison review safeguards (2026-10-23)

Body/resource comparisons require observed reads in both runs when their effective indexed description is unchanged. If the description changes, only the changed run must read the body: asymmetric activation can be the intended change. Description-only changes remain measurable through the index. Legacy records without description-change metadata use the stricter rule. `ToolHelpers.read` records successful named-skill reads on the current submission. `Controller.comparisonSkillReads` also accepts successful native tools from that turn with a canonical path to the frozen entry and returned body text. It handles numbered output, relative/dotted paths, nested shell quotes, nested Cursor ACP blocks, and partial reads/searches whose combined output covers every nonempty body line, including headings, within the current turn. One matching line, incomplete coverage, failures, unrelated paths and old turns cannot authorize adoption. This records observed reading, not proof that a model followed every instruction. Attempts carry evidence through `Agents.deliver` into `comparisons.runResult`; verdict and adoption both enforce it. Missing evidence remains inconclusive. Workers are directed to `router_read_output` when native file access is blocked; this grants no extra filesystem permissions.

`worker-instructions.cjs` caps indexed descriptions at 300 characters, preserving old package bodies; new oversized candidates fail before writing snapshots. Shorter bundled descriptions and defaults reduce this checkout's default instructions/index to 3,747 characters (previously 4,733). Tests bound text independently of installation path length. Adopted revisions keep bundled hashes in adjacent metadata files (outside the hashed package); mismatches or unavailable legacy provenance produce a notice without replacing the override. The primary controller displays workspace notices in the Hub and persists the last shown bundled-version notice per workspace/skill/version across chats and restarts. Dismiss removes the notice. Broken-store dismissal suppresses that same error across restarts until an observed recovery or a different error; a later recurrence notifies again.

Ordinary worker turns pin their resolved skill directories by session and canonical workspace store until settlement. Frozen comparison snapshots take precedence over ordinary pins. A Hub turn can hold multiple workspace pins without mixing their packages; pruning retains every pinned store, even if the Hub chat itself belongs to another project. Pruning also defers for active readers of the same source project and any unresolved process cleanup, then retries at settlement. Unrelated workspaces do not block cleanup. Undo restores the package and persists its card even if history writing fails; a notice reports the history problem separately. History recovery preserves the original file, reconstructs available chat records, and pins pre-recovery revision IDs until the recovery record leaves the existing 50-record retention window. I/O errors still fail closed.

Deterministic regressions: `tests/skill-packages.test.cjs` cases "skill body comparisons require observed use", "skill pins isolate Hub workspaces", "skill notices persist across chats, dismissal and restart", "skill descriptions stay short", "skill overrides notice bundled drift", "skill candidate errors distinguish non-Git projects", "skill Undo preserves running helpers", and "skill history recovery preserves corrupt bytes"; `tests/core.test.cjs` case "skill paths remain pinned until an ordinary worker finishes after Undo"; `tests/worker-defaults.test.cjs` budgets the fixed defaults/index scaffolding at 3,100 characters after substituting a fixed root, separately from the 300-character allowance for each description. A small description edit therefore does not hit an unrelated 20-character margin. `tests/ui-startup-smoke.cjs` exercises Dismiss through IPC and verifies it leaves the saved chat and rendered DOM. The registered `skill-comparisons` pool also exercises dispatch, adoption and Undo. Native transport evidence uses deterministic fixtures; no paid live-model quality comparison was run. The compressed wording remains behaviorally unverified. `src/controller/coordinator.cjs` implements target `bot` by appending a change to shared defaults, so it cannot directly compare replacements of the global defaults block; both runs would receive the same replacement. Skill descriptions can be compared through target `skill`. Additional cases: "skill read evidence requires the whole body", "workspace skill notices reach the Hub and broken-store Dismiss sticks", and "skill reads inside nested shell quotes retain evidence and the comparison verdict". The last case was verified on 2026-10-23 with actual PowerShell output: native and structured tool events preserve nested quoted paths with spaces while rejecting failed commands, incomplete body output and different packages.
